## artifact-and-project-structure: How PM artifacts and projects are structured, stored and found
**Choice:** Do PM artifacts (specs, research syntheses, decisions, status updates, prototypes, dashboards) stay ad hoc and local, follow repo conventions, live as objects in a platform (Atlassian, Notion, Linear style), sit behind a thin product-defined project descriptor, or does a product meta-harness define typed artifact primitives and a project structure itself?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Multi-party collaboration, reuse across sessions and harnesses, review, permissions and provenance all depend on a shared answer to "what is a project, what artifact types does it have, where do they live and who may see them". Today there is no cross-tool answer: "project" means different things even inside one vendor, and PM artifacts are mostly local files or scattered docs. This is the structural question behind [[context-and-memory]] (where context lives), [[context-and-memory]] (how context reaches the agent) and [[context-and-memory]] (per-user memory). Those three answer *where and how it is read*; this one answers *what the things are and how a project is defined*. Open: consider merging context-location and org-context-grounding-source into this dimension as its "location" half, or keep all four linked; I have kept them separate and cross-referenced.
**Related themes:** [[product-artifact-primitives]], [[artifact-standardization]], [[docs-as-code-prd]], [[pm-tool-connectivity]], [[project-taxonomy]], [[compounding-context-repo]], [[org-context-grounding]], [[memory-architecture]]   **Related dimensions:** [[context-and-memory]], [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[session-persistence-sharing]], [[governance-control-plane]]

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ad hoc and local | Artifacts are files and chats on each person's machine plus whichever docs tool they like; no shared project definition | Inconsistent Claude Code use today (the project's baseline); local session history | No change cost; maximum flexibility | Findability and sharing depend on one person; local transcripts auto-deleted after 30 days by default; agents cannot answer "what exists for project X" | mainstream | [itdaily-claude-code-30-day-transcript-deletion](../research/sources/itdaily-claude-code-30-day-transcript-deletion.md) (independent); [geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party, Geoff); [hn-ask-claude-code-non-coding-workflows](../research/sources/hn-ask-claude-code-non-coding-workflows.md) (anecdote) |
| Repo-native conventions (docs-as-code) | PRDs, specs, decisions live as typed files in a repo with agreed folders and frontmatter | Spec Kit `specs/NNN-feature/`, Kiro `requirements/design/tasks`, ADR/MADR `docs/decisions/`, AGENTS.md, Gherkin | Versioning, review (PRs), permissions and history come free; works with any harness; agents read it natively | Spec-driven tools disagree on file sets; specs are developer-feature specs, not research/strategy; review burden high and agents may ignore specs; non-engineers may not use Git; no adoption data for PMs | early-adopter (engineering); emerging (PM) | [github-spec-kit-sdd](../research/sources/github-spec-kit-sdd.md), [kiro-specs-docs](../research/sources/kiro-specs-docs.md) (first-party); [fowler-bockeler-sdd-kiro-speckit-tessl](../research/sources/fowler-bockeler-sdd-kiro-speckit-tessl.md) (independent); [adr-madr-template](../research/sources/adr-madr-template.md) (first-party); [lenny-cursor-better-product-manager](../research/sources/lenny-cursor-better-product-manager.md) (anecdote) |
| Platform-owned objects | Artifacts and projects are objects in a work/knowledge platform, reached by agents through that platform's MCP and permissions | Atlassian (Jira work items in spaces, Confluence spaces, Home projects, Teamwork Graph), Notion databases, Linear initiative > project > issue + documents, Productboard product hierarchy with specs on features | Existing governance, permissions, UI and history; many PMs already live there; Ramp uses Notion and Linear as the formal record | "Project" differs per platform (Atlassian has three); no vendor graph has native spec/insight/decision types; long-form specs second-class in issue-centric tools; MCP coverage and plan gating uneven; cross-platform view missing | mainstream (platforms); early-adopter (agent access) | [atlassian-teamwork-graph-object-types](../research/sources/atlassian-teamwork-graph-object-types.md), [atlassian-jira-space-definition](../research/sources/atlassian-jira-space-definition.md), [atlassian-confluence-space-definition](../research/sources/atlassian-confluence-space-definition.md), [atlassian-rovo-mcp-server-supported-tools](../research/sources/atlassian-rovo-mcp-server-supported-tools.md), [notion-api-databases-data-sources](../research/sources/notion-api-databases-data-sources.md), [linear-initiatives-docs](../research/sources/linear-initiatives-docs.md), [productboard-product-hierarchy](../research/sources/productboard-product-hierarchy.md) (first-party) |
| Inferred enterprise context layer | A vendor graph indexes many tools and infers entities (projects, people, customers) rather than prescribing structure | Glean Enterprise Graph; Atlassian Teamwork Graph (partly inferred relationships) | No migration; cross-tool search for agents | Inferred, not authored: no owner/status/version semantics; vendor claims only; permission mirroring is only as good as connectors | early-adopter | [glean-enterprise-graph-product-page](../research/sources/glean-enterprise-graph-product-page.md) (vendor claim); [atlassian-teamwork-graph-glossary](../research/sources/atlassian-teamwork-graph-glossary.md) (first-party) |
| Thin project descriptor that points at things | A small manifest (name, owner, sources, instructions, artifact index) per project that points at objects in repos and platforms, not a competing store; exposed to agents as a resource | Cowork project (description, folders, instructions, links, scoped memory; local-only); Backstage `catalog-info.yaml` envelope; MCP resources (uri, mimeType, lastModified) | Gives agents a stable answer to "what is this project"; harness-agnostic if plain text; cheap to start | Needs a convention and an owner; no existing standard for typed PM artifacts; MCP resources are under-implemented; Cowork project not shareable | emerging (concept; components mature) | [anthropic-cowork-projects-docs](../research/sources/anthropic-cowork-projects-docs.md), [backstage-descriptor-format](../research/sources/backstage-descriptor-format.md), [mcp-spec-resources](../research/sources/mcp-spec-resources.md) (first-party); [anthropic-claude-projects-support](../research/sources/anthropic-claude-projects-support.md) (first-party: shareable project is files-only) |
| Meta-harness-defined artifact primitives and project structure | The harness defines typed artifact kinds (e.g. intent/spec, evidence, decision, risk, status update), a project schema, lifecycle states, provenance and permissions, and keeps or syncs them to repos and platforms | Spec Kit's constitution/spec/plan/tasks set (narrow); Geoff's implied primitives (intent, evidence, risk taxonomy, shaping skill); Backstage envelope + MADR-style frontmatter as nearest composition | A shared vocabulary for multi-party work; cross-harness consistency; lifecycle and provenance can be enforced; stories can reference types | Biggest build; risk of a competing system of record; migration and adoption cost; schema must survive model and tool change; nothing like it exists to test | emerging (concept) | [geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md), [geoff-intent-shaping-well-managed-environment](../research/sources/geoff-intent-shaping-well-managed-environment.md) (first-party, Geoff's concepts); [github-spec-kit-readme](../research/sources/github-spec-kit-readme.md) (first-party); [w3c-prov-overview](../research/sources/w3c-prov-overview.md) (first-party, provenance vocabulary) |
| Hybrid: repo canonical for long-form, platform for tracking, descriptor ties them | Specs, decisions and research notes as files; work items and roadmap in the platform; a descriptor links both | Productboard spec-to-coding-agent hand-off over MCP; Spec Kit plus Linear/Jira; Ramp (Notion/Linear as record, skills in Git) | Each system does what it is good at | Two sources of truth to keep in step; permissions and identity differ across systems | early-adopter | [productboard-mcp-server-spark](../research/sources/productboard-mcp-server-spark.md) (first-party); [x-sebgoddijn-glass-article](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party, Ramp) |
| Name one system of record per artifact | For each artifact, choose repo-authoritative, legacy-tool-authoritative (Claude reads and writes via MCP), or two-way linkage (record ID in the file, commit SHA in the record) as the minimum | Playbook "Legacy systems and the source of truth" sidebar | Lets an AI-native chain coexist with Jira or a regulator-accepted traceability tool | Two sources of truth under linkage; sync discipline | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Brand and style as skills | Conformance to voice, visual and design-system standards enforced by skills (and an admin-locked design system) rather than templates or review | Blank Metal; Claude Design | Consistent output without a review step | Skill drift; the "lock" is not described in detail | early-adopter | [blank-metal](../research/sources/claude-customers-blank-metal-cowork.md) (vendor claim), [design-on-brand](../research/sources/claude-design-on-brand.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Project as a coordinator over parallel threads | A project conversation splits a goal into threads, each a cloud session on its own branch; conflicts resolved as merge conflicts | Claude Code projects (beta) | The plan-to-code half of the chain as one product object | Single owner; no team sharing described; burns usage limits faster | emerging | [projects-redesigned](../research/sources/claude-code-projects-redesigned.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Sub-projects owned by each function, with a master roll-up | One project per area, owned by its function; a master project combines them | DoP product-health setup on Claude Projects | Delegated ownership; smaller contexts | The roll-up is manual copy-paste, and filename prefixes stand in for structure | early-adopter | [DoP Projects product health](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-claude-projects-product-health.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Decision queue as a first-class artifact | Open decisions kept as records (the call, its kind, who it waits on, age, context), extracted from inbox and chat. "A forcing function", not a to-do list | DoP product dashboard | Makes a PM's real bottleneck visible | Extraction accuracy; overlaps with trackers | experimental | [DoP product dashboard](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-product-dashboard-stakeholder-updates.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Stage-artifact skill family | Skills split by stage (what, then how, then build), each writing a durable file the next one reads | Every compound-engineering; gstack; Atlassian spec-to-backlog (the breakdown is approved first) | Stages can be inspected and swapped; no dependence on one long chat | A convention, not a standard; files drift | emerging | [DoP agent skills](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-practical-agent-skills.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Living prototype library | Prototypes deployed as bounded microsites in the product's design language, catalogued so sales, success, support and execs can demo or train before build | Acolyte Health | Prototypes outlive the meeting; faster customer validation | Customer expectation risk; drift from what ships; no provenance to the spec | emerging | [acolyte](../research/sources/howiai-acolyte-health-meeting-notes-prototypes.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Shared live artifact rendering each viewer's own data | An agent-built web artifact shared across a team; each visitor approves their own connectors and sees only what their grants allow | ChatGPT Sites with plugins | One artifact serves many roles without copying data | Vendor-specific; enforcement model not explained; weak usage analytics | emerging (vendor GA, Oct 2026) | [korevec](../research/sources/howiai-openai-korevec-chatgpt-sites.md) (vendor claim) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Point-in-time intent docs plus living capability pages | PRDs and briefs are dated statements of intent, not maintained; current state lives in one living page per capability | One unnamed org in a community thread | Clear split between what was intended and what is true now; one current page for agents and GTM | Someone must keep capability pages current | emerging | [cw-eng-outpaces](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineering-outpaces-product.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

#### Audit correction (user-story-mapping, 2026-10-24)
- Repo-native row: the Lenny/Cursor note is evidence of PMs drafting PRDs with a coding agent over the codebase and pushing them to Confluence/Notion, not of PRDs kept in git; the PRD-in-repo practice itself is the project's observation (`[G]`) with no published adoption figure. The row's "early-adopter (engineering); emerging (PM)" maturity stands on the spec-driven-development evidence only.
- Platform-owned row, Atlassian detail (verification pass, verbatim re-reads): Rovo MCP `delete_jira` and `manage_jira` are off by default and destructive calls go through a separate confirmation path ([tools](../research/sources/atlassian-rovo-mcp-server-supported-tools.md)); the Teamwork Graph API is read-only, Forge-app-only and org-admin installed, so it is not a direct data path for an external harness ([scopes](../research/sources/atlassian-teamwork-graph-scopes.md), [limitations](../research/sources/atlassian-teamwork-graph-limitations.md)); Atlassian's guidance on where PRDs and decisions live is Confluence page templates (PRD, project poster, DACI) linked to Jira by embedded issue links ([PRD template](../research/sources/atlassian-confluence-prd-template.md), [DACI](../research/sources/atlassian-confluence-daci-decision-template.md)); Confluence spaces carry agent-readable "space instructions", a per-container context file ([tools](../research/sources/atlassian-rovo-mcp-server-supported-tools.md)). JPD idea access over MCP is claimed in a vendor blog but unconfirmed ([blog](../research/sources/atlassian-blog-mcp-enhancements-jpd.md)).
- Hybrid row: "Glass projects are per-project cost meters" is an inference from two sentences in the Glyman essay; what a Glass project contains is unestablished.
- Platform-owned row: Ramp's use of Notion and Linear as "the formal record" is confirmed verbatim in the Charles transcript at 11:43 ([transcript](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.transcript.md)).

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Repo-native conventions (docs-as-code)*: an `intent/` folder in the product repo for one product, a dedicated intent repo only when intent spans many repos ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Artifacts live beside the conversation and evolve in place*: Docs, Slides and Design produced in the same thread at shareable links ([cowork-chat-unified](../research/sources/claude-cowork-chat-unified.md), first-party); Airtree's board prep diffs itself against the previous prep ([airtree](../research/sources/claude-customers-airtree-cowork.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Project as intent plus governance container*: Claude Code projects are now a goal plus repo, environment, connectors, plugins and model, coordinating parallel cloud threads ([projects-redesigned](../research/sources/claude-code-projects-redesigned.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Ad hoc and local**: the per-initiative AI Project (ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini Gems) is the de facto PM project container today — [pm-second-brain](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-pm-second-brain-chatgpt.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Project as intent plus governance container**: a PM creates one Claude Project per feature initiative and shares it with CS, PMM and marketing, who upload their deliverables there — [cw-team-velocity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-team-velocity.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Repo-native conventions (docs-as-code)**: a `{project}-product` repo whose CLAUDE.md points at engineering repos, with verbose commits as decision context — [everyone-claude-code](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-everyone-claude-code.md) (anecdote); a visual editor over agent-written JSON plan files that saves edits back for the agent — [cj-hess](../research/sources/howiai-tenex-cj-hess-flowy.md) (anecdote); Notion's prototype repo is all files on disk with metadata collated into an index — [notion-playground](../research/sources/howiai-notion-brian-lovin-prototype-playground.md) (first-party); a PM hub repo with Markdown PRDs and prototypes side by side — [claire-vo-prd-to-code](../research/sources/howiai-claire-vo-beginner-prd-to-code.md) (anecdote); a proposed intake command that updates a golden PRD with a keep/undo diff and change log; agents work from the current version with history preserved — [cw-eng-outpaces](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineering-outpaces-product.md), [cw-prds-for-agents](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prds-for-agents.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Artifacts live beside the conversation and evolve in place**: Cowork live artifacts refresh from connectors and reuse connector auth — [rieseberg](../research/sources/howiai-felix-rieseberg-cowork.md) (vendor claim); Block's publishing MCP turns agent-made HTML into an internal URL — [block-goose](../research/sources/howiai-block-goose-brosamer-axen.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Bidirectional sync as a pattern: Figma MCP keeps design and code in sync both ways (code to editable frames; frame edits reconciled into code) — [figma](../research/sources/howiai-figma-design-code-loop.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Key questions to choose
1. How do PMs (not engineers) actually store and find artifacts today, per artifact type, and how long does finding the last version take? Evidence is the project's description and anecdotes. — *answered by:* 6–8 walkthrough interviews of real folders, sessions and tools; an artifact audit of 2–3 recent initiatives — *affects:* all
2. What share of PMs (and of the reviewing roles) can and will work in Git for long-form artifacts? No adoption figure was found. — *answered by:* survey or interviews; check with engineering leadership — *affects:* repo-native, hybrid
3. Which artifact types does a multi-party team actually need to share and review (spec, research synthesis, decision, status, prototype, dashboard), and which can be left platform-specific? — *answered by:* story inventory (S05, S13, S16, S17) scored with PMs, designers, engineers — *affects:* primitives, descriptor
4. Does a team already agree what a "project" is (initiative, epic, space, feature folder), or would the harness be imposing one? — *answered by:* ask 3–5 teams to list their current projects and boundaries; compare with Atlassian/Linear/Backstage models — *affects:* descriptor, meta-harness-defined
5. Can the main BI and research tools be reached by agents with user-level permissions without admin work (Amplitude, Dovetail, Notion yes; Snowflake needs an admin, Looker a self-run server), and what is the blocking tool in our target orgs? — *answered by:* connectivity inventory in a target org; security owner — *affects:* platform-owned, inferred layer, hybrid
6. Would a thin descriptor plus MCP resources give agents enough structure, or do agents need typed primitives with lifecycle and provenance to be reliable and reviewable? — *answered by:* prototype one project in both forms (see S05 journeys); small eval on retrieval and review tasks — *affects:* descriptor vs meta-harness-defined
7. What should provenance and versioning mean for PM artifacts (who or what produced it, from which sources, which version was ratified)? Git covers files; MCP resources carry only `lastModified`; platforms have their own history. — *answered by:* reviewers' and risk owners' requirements; prototype of an artifact record — *affects:* primitives, hybrid
8. Who would own the schema and keep it current (product ops, a domain owner per area, the harness vendor)? — *answered by:* product ops and domain leads; compare with ADR/Backstage governance — *affects:* all but ad hoc
9. If sharing happens by project, what permissions model applies when artifacts live in several systems with different ACLs (mirror the viewer's rights per source, or a project-level ACL)? — *answered by:* security/policy owner; test with two platforms — *affects:* descriptor, meta-harness-defined, inferred layer
10. Can a standard be adopted bottom-up through a skill or template people choose (as MADR and AGENTS.md spread) or does it need a platform behind it? — *answered by:* look at adoption paths of ADR, AGENTS.md, Spec Kit; pilot a template in one team — *affects:* repo-native, descriptor, meta-harness-defined
11. In the target org, which system is the record for intent, spec and approval today (Jira, Confluence, a requirements tool), and would auditors accept the repo instead? — *answered by:* the policy owner and an auditor — *affects:* Name one system of record, Repo-native conventions. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
12. Does "X-as-code" (rules and templates in git, as ABC Legal does) transfer to PRDs and roadmaps? — *answered by:* a prototype — *affects:* Repo-native conventions ([abc-legal](../research/sources/claude-abc-legal-managed-agents-builders.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
13. Do roll-ups across projects (team to master) need to be native to our project model, or is a scheduled summary skill enough? — *answered by:* interviews with PMs who run health checks across functions — *affects:* sub-project roll-up, project-taxonomy (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
14. Is "open decision" an artifact type we should model? — *answered by:* PM research on where decisions stall — *affects:* decision queue, status-update sequencing (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
15. Should PM artifacts (specs, designs, data samples) be bidirectionally synced with the tools humans edit them in, or exported one way into the agent's workspace? — *answered by:* comparing drift and rework under each pattern in a pilot — *affects:* artifact-and-project-structure, session-persistence-sharing. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Current lean (optional) and what would change it
No lean. Evidence is thin on PM-side practice (anecdote and vendor guides); the strongest independent evidence says spec-driven tooling is young and divergent. The thin-descriptor option is the cheapest to test and the meta-harness-defined option the largest bet; Q1, Q4 and Q6 decide between them.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Project as intent plus governance container | A project states what the user is trying to do (a strong intent signal) and carries its skills, tools, model and policies; open to join | Stripe Kai projects | Better routing and safer defaults per workflow | Another object to curate | early-adopter | [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |

## target-user: Which users and personas we serve first, including for shared agents
**Choice:** IC PM at a mid or large company; head of product or CPO; founder-PM or AI builder; technical or platform PM and product ops; adjacent roles; every employee including non-engineers (agent workspace); engineers running many agents in parallel; and, for a shared persistent agent, which users and jobs it serves first.
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Personas differ in which jobs they own, how much AI they already use and what they would pay for; the shared-agent options differ sharply by job (interactive co-working vs unattended jobs) and the evidence base is mostly engineering.
**Related themes:** [[pm-persona-role-expansion]], [[pm-ai-usage-surveys]], [[non-engineer-builders]], [[shared-persistent-sessions]]   **Related dimensions:** [[target-outcome]], [[sequencing]], [[work-structure]], [[adoption-model]]
Formerly: target-user, shared-agent-users-and-jobs (merged per 01-synthesis.md D.0; the shared-agent options are kept as examples)

### Sub-choice: target-user — Which users and personas we serve first
**Choice:** Do we build first for the IC PM, the group PM / head of product / CPO, the founder-PM or "AI builder", the technical or platform PM, product ops, or adjacent roles (designer, eng lead, PMM)?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Personas differ in which jobs they own, how much AI they already use and what they would pay for; the choice shapes the PM jobs map used for everything else.
**Related themes:** [[pm-persona-role-expansion]], [[pm-ai-usage-surveys]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]]   **Related dimensions:** [[sequencing]], [[work-structure]], [[adoption-model]]

| Generalist product role in a lean pod | One product person per pod of 4-6 engineers does PM work plus routine design, data and research with internal AI tools; specialists added only when needed | Instagram/Meta "product staff" (2026); proposed "shipyard" pod of six capabilities; Sundial calls everyone a builder | A persona whose job is exactly the multi-hat work a harness would support | One company plus proposals; no outcome data; converts include designers and data scientists, not only PMs | early-adopter | [mosseri](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-adam-mosseri-product-staff.md) (first-party); [udezue](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-udezue-ai-reshaping-pm-role.md) (anecdote); [zhuo](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-julie-zhuo-managing-ai.md) (first-party, small startup) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- Non-engineering functions: Anthropic says "the vast majority" of Cowork use is outside engineering, and its session analysis puts business ops (33%) and content (16%) at about half, with no PM or design category ([cowork-for-enterprise](../research/sources/claude-cowork-for-enterprise.md), [how-people-use-cowork](../research/sources/claude-how-people-use-cowork.md), first-party telemetry). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- PMs and designers as builders: "Our PMs code a lot now" ([running-ai-native-engineering-org](../research/sources/claude-running-ai-native-engineering-org.md)); Epic says over half of Claude Code use is from non-developers ([epic](../research/sources/claude-customers-epic-internal-adoption.md)); a Twilio product director built a 425-tool platform ([twilio](../research/sources/claude-customers-twilio-pm-platform.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Product owner as approver*: the playbook's chain puts a product owner at every early gate, with any employee as originator ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **IC PM at a mid/large company** (the squeeze): one Anthropic PM effectively faces 15-20 engineers' output; the response is more PMs plus deputised product-minded engineers below a two-week threshold — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Head of product / CPO**: heads of product build oversight agents (status, standups, product reviews); PM leverage is oversight tooling, not being "the 51st engineer" — [singhal](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-nikhyl-singhal-half-of-pms.md) (anecdote); Webflow's CPO runs a personal chief-of-staff agent — [wolan](../research/sources/howiai-webflow-rachel-wolan-chief-of-staff.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Adjacent roles** / **Founder-PM / AI builder**: product-engineer pilots move discovery, KPIs and UX to engineers with the leader owning the why — [cw-product-engineers](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-product-engineers-pilot.md) (anecdote); TPMs and IT staff vibe-code prototypes that win VP buy-in — [cw-non-pms](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-non-pms-shipping.md) (anecdote); Anthropic designers are becoming PM- and engineering-shaped — [jenny-wen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-jenny-wen-design-process-dead.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Adjacent roles gain less so far: OpenAI's internal surveys put engineers at 10-100x while designers are not, because design stays an iterative loop — [silber](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-ian-silber-openai-design.md) (first-party); designers 55% enjoy work more vs 70% of PMs and engineers — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (informal poll). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Role convergence: Airtable's CEO says every EPD role must be minimally good at the other two — [liu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-howie-liu-airtable-reorg.md); Google Labs' head says roles are not collapsing and PMs should keep their major — [woodward](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-josh-woodward-labs-team.md) (both first-party opinion). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Which persona feels the most pain and has budget and authority to adopt? — *answered by:* interviews with each persona; sponsor interviews — *affects:* all
2. Do the personas we would serve share a jobs map, or do we need one per persona? — *answered by:* card-sort of the PM jobs map with each persona — *affects:* agent-unit, pm-job-sequencing
3. Is the "AI builder" collapse real in the orgs we target, or do roles expand instead (Atlassian's view at 10,000+ people)? — *answered by:* target-org discovery — *affects:* founder-PM vs IC options
4. Is the buyer of a meta-harness the platform/engineering team (governance) while the user is a PM who just wants a workflow? If so, which do we target first? — *answered by:* sponsor and user interviews — *affects:* all harness-ownership options

- (Track 6) Do the first users mainly do open work (individual PMs ideating and planning) or standardise a team process (a lead or product-ops owner)? The fit of runtime patterns differs sharply between the two. — *answered by:* interviews with both kinds of user; check who owns the standard (PRD review, launch checklist) today — *affects:* general-harness vs task-agent options
- (user-story-mapping) The stories are performer-agnostic by design (the performer can be PM, analyst, designer or engineer). Do non-PM performers want the same variants, or does the performer's role change which variant fits? — *answered by:* interviews with analysts, designers and engineers who do PM jobs ([S01](../tasks/user-story-mapping/stories-jobs.md#s01-turn-a-batch-of-customer-conversations-into-what-we-learned), S04, S10) — *affects:* all
5. What share of Cowork and Claude Code use is PM, design and analyst work, and what is the task mix? — *answered by:* Anthropic's Economic Index team or the target org's admin analytics — *affects:* sequencing. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
6. Is the one-PM, multi-week platform build (Twilio) repeatable for typical PMs, or a senior-domain-expert outlier? — *answered by:* Twilio; a pilot cohort — *affects:* PM-as-builder. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
7. Is the first user the IC PM, the engineer or builder acting as PM on small (sub-two-week) work, or a generalist product-staff role; and does the agent serve non-PM builders as intake, the PM as gatekeeper and prioritiser, or both? — *answered by:* share of the target org's shipped work under a small-work threshold and who shapes and approves it; role mix of pilot early adopters; interviews with merged-role teams; who owns the "what" in regulated multi-audience products (engineer-led building has worked where the builder is the user and the knowledge landscape is stable — [cw-building-without-pm](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-building-without-pm-requirements.md), anecdote) — *affects:* target-user, sequencing, intent-shaping-and-spec, agent-interface. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
8. Do non-engineer PMs prefer a powerful developer-shaped agent app over a simplified one built for them (OpenAI's experience), and does that hold in a regulated enterprise? — *answered by:* pilot both shapes with the same PM group — *affects:* target-user, agent-interface. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
9. Is "PM ships code" a target outcome at all, or only "PM ships a reviewed spec or prototype"? Large-company PMs may ship only contained UI changes finished by an engineer, once the codebase is agent-ready — *answered by:* engineering leadership position plus a pilot on one agent-ready repo — *affects:* target-user, target-outcome. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)


### Sub-choice: shared-agent-users-and-jobs — Which users and jobs a shared, persistent agent serves first
**Choice:** Do we aim shared/persistent sessions at engineers' code work, at non-engineers building things, at recurring scheduled digests and triage, or at team Q&A in chat, and for which roles?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The infrastructure options differ sharply by the job (interactive co-working vs unattended jobs), and the evidence base is mostly engineering.
**Related themes:** [[non-engineer-builders]], [[shared-persistent-sessions]], [[one-job-per-agent]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[adoption-model]], [[session-persistence-sharing]], [[target-user]], [[target-outcome]] (overlap: consider merging this into target-user)

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Engineers' background code tasks | Parallel PR-producing agents | Ramp Inspect, Stripe Minions, Copilot cloud agent | Strongest reported scale (Stripe >1,300 merged PRs/week; Inspect about half of Ramp's merged PRs) | Needs tests and dev infra; figures are self-reported and vary by source | early-adopter | [Stripe Minions](../research/sources/stripe-minions-part-2.md) (first-party); [Modal on Inspect](../research/sources/modal-ramp-inspect-background-agent.md) (vendor claim) |
| Non-engineers building via a shared agent | Product, design, legal, finance use a multiplayer agent with Slack/web/Chrome extension | Ramp Inspect | New builders | Review capacity and permissions; Ramp-specific | early-adopter | [Modal on Inspect](../research/sources/modal-ramp-inspect-background-agent.md) (vendor claim) |
| Scheduled digests and triage | Recurring cloud runs that read systems and post to Slack or open PRs | Claude scheduled tasks (backlog grooming, alert triage, docs drift) | Fits PM monitoring jobs | Personal ownership; success not visible from run status | early-adopter | [Claude scheduled tasks](../research/sources/anthropic-claude-code-scheduled-tasks.md) (first-party) |
| Team Q&A and requests in chat | A shared agent in Slack channels | Claude Tag, Codex in Slack, Cursor in Slack, Devin | Where teams already work | Channel as access boundary; identity choices | emerging | [Tenable](../research/sources/tenable-claude-tag-slack-access-model.md) (independent); [Codex Slack docs](../research/sources/openai-codex-cloud-slack.md) (first-party) |
| Mostly private, individual sessions | No shared agent; individual harnesses with team skills | Claude Code locally | Least risk | Corrections do not propagate (vendor argument, unverified) | mainstream | [Unblocked](../research/sources/unblocked-agent-doom-loop.md) (vendor claim) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Non-engineers building via a shared agent**: marketers change production pages in v0 and open PRs — [v0-git](../research/sources/howiai-guillermo-rauch-v0-git-workflow.md) (vendor claim). Hard case: non-technical staff find GitHub-based shared context overwhelming — [cw-claude-context-sharing](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-claude-context-sharing.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Which PM or team jobs (from the PM jobs map) are recurring and unattended versus interactive and shared? — *answered by:* score the jobs map with users — *affects:* scheduled vs multiplayer
2. Is there any independent outcome evidence (quality, time saved, incidents) for non-engineer use of shared agents? None found beyond Ramp's accounts. — *answered by:* more research; interviews with adopters — *affects:* non-engineer option
3. Who is the first user group worth a pilot, and what decision would a pilot inform? — *answered by:* the project owner / user interviews — *affects:* all
- See also: key question 4 (what value outcome we target, and how to measure it) moved to [[target-outcome]].


#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Every employee including non-engineers (evidence row) | One company agent for all functions | Stripe Kai: 83% weekly; Marketing 95%, GTM 87%, higher than engineering; GTM was the pilot | Broad reach; GTM pulled hardest | Engineers less served; numbers self-reported | early-adopter | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party); [LangChain case study](../research/sources/langchain-how-stripe-built-kai-deep-agents.md) (vendor claim) |

## target-outcome: Which outcome or value we target
**Choice:** Do PM agents aim at PM time saved, faster delivery (throughput), better decisions and spec quality, lower risk, or product and business outcomes?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It decides the pitch, the measurement ([[pm-agent-evaluation]]) and which jobs rank first. Surveys report time saved and yet only about 1 in 4 PMs have a strategy to show for it (Productside, vendor survey).
**Related themes:** [[outcome-measurement-gap]], [[pm-ai-usage-surveys]], [[intent-factory-before-discovery]]   **Related dimensions:** [[pm-agent-evaluation]], [[sequencing]], [[target-user]]
Also holds: key question 4 from shared-agent-users-and-jobs (now under [[target-user]]).

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM time saved | Remove note-taking, updates, drafting | 63% of PMs save 4+ h/week (Lenny survey); Atlassian "no manual updates" | Easy to demonstrate | Reclaimed time goes to reactive work (Atlassian survey); weak strategic value | mainstream | [Lenny survey](../research/sources/lennys-newsletter-ai-tools-overdelivering-results.md) (independent); [Atlassian State of Product](../research/sources/atlassian-state-of-product-2026-webinar-event.md) (vendor survey) |
| Delivery speed / throughput | Idea-to-customer cycle time, features shipped | Atlassian: 6-8 weeks vs ~6 months, ~3x Jira throughput | Concrete and executive-friendly | Self-reported, no baselines; attribution unclear | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party) |
| Decision and spec quality | Better-grounded specs, fewer gaps, fewer rework loops | Torres-style quote checks; Meta VP paragraph+prototype in place of PRD | Aligns with the intent-first thesis | Nobody publishes measures; needs rubrics | emerging | [Torres](../research/sources/echai-torres-ai-interview-analysis.md) (independent); [Meta VP piece](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-meta-chawla-pm-leader-agents.md) (first-party) |
| Product / business outcomes | Adoption, retention, revenue | None found | The real goal | Long lag; confounded | emerging | none found (unsourced) |
| Wait time per platform and gate as the funded outcome (new row) | Target the share of elapsed time spent waiting on other parties, and the intake-to-decision lead time per platform, measured by the agent from real asks | No organisation found publishes these; the Maersk and Planview accounts show the shape | The measurement nobody has; independent evidence says waiting is where the time goes | Vendor figures only on the benefit side; a measurement, not yet a saving | emerging | [Herbsleb and Mockus](../research/sources/herbsleb-mockus-speed-communication-distributed-development.md) (independent); [Planview](../research/sources/planview-project-to-product-state-of-industry-2023.md), [Black Swan Farming](../research/sources/blackswanfarming-maersk-cost-of-delay.md) (vendor claim) (from research-dependency-agent) |
| Fewer late surprises (new row) | Target dependencies discovered in week one that used to arrive at integration or at the gate | NAO on Crossrail; HealthCare.gov's late components; the FCA's dependency finding | The outcome the independent evidence names as the cause of overrun | Hard to count what did not happen; needs a baseline from past programmes | emerging | [NAO](../research/sources/nao-framework-to-review-programmes.md), [HHS OIG](../research/sources/hhs-oig-healthcare-gov-case-study.md) (independent); [FCA](../research/sources/fca-implementing-technology-change.md) (regulator) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Delivery speed / throughput**, costs: 82% of 5,332 tech workers say AI makes them better at their job, but open answers say more and faster rather than higher quality; burnout rose from 44.7% to 55.7% in a year — [tech-workers-2026](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tech-workers-2026-survey.md) (independent, self-selected). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Decision and spec quality** (bottleneck moved to intent): second-hand, a Google executive reports two years of roadmap in three months, with deciding what to add as the hard problem — [acharya](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-anish-acharya-company-loops.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Key questions to choose
1. What outcome would a sponsor measure success against (hours, cycle time, quality, revenue)? — *answered by:* sponsor interviews — *affects:* all
2. Is reclaimed PM time actually redirected to higher-value work in target orgs, or absorbed by reactive work? — *answered by:* pilot with before/after time diary — *affects:* time-saved option
- (Track 6) Is the target outcome speed, consistency (same standard applied every time), reduced review effort, or org learning? Consistency and reduced noise are the measurable outcomes in the code-review cases (Cloudflare, CR-Bench); speed alone is contested (METR RCT, early-2025: AI-allowed tasks 19% slower while experts believed faster). — *answered by:* choose metric with the project owner; baseline trial — *affects:* task-agent vs general harness
- (moved from shared-agent-users-and-jobs, Track 5:)
4. What value outcome do we target (cycle time, review load, consistency, learning once), and how would we measure it, noting Wavect's point to measure accepted changes per review effort rather than PR count? — *answered by:* metrics design with policy owner — *affects:* all
5. Would the sponsor fund a measurement (wait time per platform) before a saving, and for how long? — *answered by:* sponsor interviews — *affects:* wait time per platform and gate; fewer late surprises (from research-dependency-agent)
6. Can we reconstruct, for two past programmes, when each dependency was discovered and what it cost? — *answered by:* a retrospective with the programme managers — *affects:* fewer late surprises (from research-dependency-agent)



---

## pm-agent-evaluation: How we would know a PM agent works
**Choice:** What counts as success: self-reported time saved, throughput (PRs deployed, features delivered), output quality against a human baseline, or product outcomes?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Nobody has published outcome measures; Atlassian says it hasn't figured it out. A measurement approach is both a risk and a possible differentiator.
**Related themes:** [[outcome-measurement-gap]], [[vendor-claims-vs-independent-evidence]], [[evals-autonomy-trust]]   **Related dimensions:** [[review-and-autonomy]], [[sequencing]]

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Self-reported time saved | Survey users | Lenny survey (63% of PMs save 4+ h/week); vendor case claims | Cheap | Biased, says nothing on quality | mainstream | [Lenny survey](../research/sources/lennys-newsletter-ai-tools-overdelivering-results.md) (independent, self-selected) |
| Delivery throughput | PRs deployed, features delivered, idea-to-customer time, usage | Atlassian's measures | Objective, available | Team vs org measurement, attribution unclear | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party) |
| Output quality vs human baseline | Blind-rated agent vs human artifacts, evals | Torres's quote/fact evals; Fireside five-tool test (weak) | Tests the real claim | Needs rubrics and raters; none published for PM jobs | emerging | [Torres](../research/sources/echai-torres-ai-interview-analysis.md) (independent) |
| Product outcomes | Adoption, retention, revenue effects | Not found for any PM agent | The true goal | Long lag, confounded | emerging | none found (unsourced) |
| Intent-factory KRs | Share of well-specified intent delivered end to end by agents before human intervention; intent-completeness rate against an agreed standard; time to remediate a defect or vulnerability (target: near-instant) | Geoff's Actions 1 and 3; Stripe's (~30% of merges by Minions) and Ramp's (75% of PRs by Inspect) reported shares | Measures the factory, not PM opinion; ties to a business goal | Attribution across shaping and build; needs an agreed completeness standard first | emerging | [Intent Factory Actions 1, 3](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party proposal; company shares are vendor claims, relayed) |
| Classification accuracy vs SME labels | For judgement jobs framed as classification (e.g. risk review), grade a sample of agent outputs against SME labels for false positives and negatives | Geoff's risk-agent concept | Objective, cheap to sample; each error points to a heuristic to fix | Only fits jobs with a known taxonomy; needs SME labelling time | emerging (concept) | [Intent Shaping in a Well-Managed Environment](../research/sources/geoff-intent-shaping-well-managed-environment.md) (first-party concept) |
| Evals on the agent's own configuration in CI | 20-50 real tasks run non-interactively whenever CLAUDE.md, skills or hooks change, and nightly; config changes that drop the pass rate are reviewed; each incident adds an eval | Playbook Stage 4 | Regression testing for the harness, not just the code | Eval upkeep; cases stop discriminating as models improve | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Draft-retention rate | Share of agent drafts kept without a meaningful rewrite; Epic treats both 100% unedited and 0% as alarms | Financial-industry guide; Epic | A cheap pilot metric for document work | Measures acceptance, not correctness | early-adopter | [financial-guide](../research/sources/claude-guide-financial-industry.md) (first-party), [epic](../research/sources/claude-customers-epic-internal-adoption.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Human reactions harvested as labels | Emoji and thread replies in chat become an eval set and drive weekly prompt-tuning PRs | ABC Legal Harvester/Tuner | Evals without an extra step for reviewers | Label noise; selection bias | early-adopter | [abc-legal](../research/sources/claude-abc-legal-managed-agents-builders.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Rubric-graded outcomes by an independent grader | A separate grader in its own context scores against a rubric that encodes "good" | Managed Agents outcomes | Works for non-code output (docs, decks) | Rubric quality; internal benchmarks only | emerging | [dreaming-outcomes](../research/sources/claude-managed-agents-dreaming-outcomes.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Four-axis builder eval | Outcome, execution path, quality vs a curated reference, resource use; rerun on every model or framework change | Cresta Conductor | Catches regressions on model swaps | Needs a curated reference set | early-adopter | [cresta](../research/sources/claude-cresta-agent-builder-evals.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Proposal funnel: would-do rate vs ship rate | Measure the share of agent-proposed work a human says they would do against the share that actually ships, and train a judge to predict human approval | Amplitude Wave (~50% would do, ~11% shipped) | Measures the proposal pipeline, not activity. A rare candid vendor number | Says nothing about the outcome or quality of what shipped | emerging | [DoP self-improving product](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-self-improving-product.md) (vendor claim, relayed) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Calibrated LLM-judge stack | Programmatic checks first, then an LLM judge calibrated to 80–90% agreement with human labels, then human review on top | Airbnb; DoorDash (from ~1 human review a day to 2,000 judged sessions) | Grading at a scale humans can't reach | Needs a calibration set per job; second-hand claims | early-adopter | [DoP managing agents](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-managing-ai-agents.md) (independent, relaying vendor claims) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| The most-asked real questions as the eval set | Use the 20–30 questions people ask most as both the build target and the eval, and require ~80% before rollout | Anthropic data team (reported 21%→95% after a semantic layer and two skills) | Grounded in real demand | Narrow coverage; the 80% bar is arbitrary | emerging | [DoP semantic layers](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-semantic-data-layers.md) (independent, relaying vendor claims) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| A PM-kept "model sense" log | A prompt library, a log of what each model claimed against what was observed, and a small domain eval rerun at each model release | DoP Model Sense Notebook | A cheap regression check on PM jobs, owned by PMs | Manual; subjective scoring | practice | [DoP model sense](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-model-sense-testing.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Error-analysis-led evals (trace review first, judges validated against human labels) | A named domain expert (often the PM) reads about 100 real traces, marks pass/fail with critiques, clusters failures into under 10 modes; code checks for objective modes, LLM judges for subjective ones validated against held-out human labels; CI golden set plus sampled production judging; repeat as criteria drift | Husain and Shankar method (Nurture Boss, ReChat); evals-skills plugin | Metrics the team trusts because they track real failures; a regression net; criteria that sharpen through review | Needs a named expert's time and logged traces; judge alignment is work; repeats as criteria move; sources sell a course | early-adopter (practitioners claim mainstream) | [advanced-evals](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-advanced-evals-error-discovery.md), [evals-hamel-shreya](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-evals-hamel-shreya.md), [husain](../research/sources/howiai-hamel-husain-error-analysis.md), [hamel-shreya-podcast](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-hamel-shreya-evals.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Human touches and draft retention per unit of work | Count human touches needed to finish an agent task (re-prompts, tracker comments, corrections) and how much of an agent draft the human keeps, edits or deletes, plus implicit signals (regenerations, switching off) | Warp Factories "human interactions per PR"; deployment consultants' draft-retention logging | Measures steering load directly; for PM artifacts needs no new instrumentation if drafts and finals are stored | Rewards less steering even when steering is the valuable judgment; may reward bland drafts; Warp unsure it is the right metric | emerging | [warp-factory](../research/sources/howiai-warp-zach-lloyd-software-factory.md) (vendor claim); [reganti-badam](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-reganti-badam-ai-deployments.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Replay past tasks under new configs | Freeze the harness definition, rerun a corpus of real past tasks with a different model or config, score with the same judge | Warp Factories cost-quality chart; Anthropic's CPO on rerunning captured traces per model | Evidence-based model routing on your own work, not public benchmarks; regression check per model release | Depends on trusting the judge; needs recorded, replayable tasks | emerging | [warp-factory](../research/sources/howiai-warp-zach-lloyd-software-factory.md) (vendor claim); [krieger](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-mike-krieger-anthropic-cpo.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Rework rate on agent-assisted delivery | Measure clarification loops, reopened tickets, spec/design mismatches, review burden and first-pass acceptance instead of speed | Proposed in a 1,500-person org's spec-driven SDLC thread | Catches impressive-looking work that creates rework; ties intent quality to delivery | Needs a baseline and ticket hygiene; a proposal, not a result | emerging (to be researched) | [cw-quarterly-planning](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-quarterly-planning.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Minimum viable quality bars plus cost envelope | Before launch, set acceptable, delight and do-not-ship bars from behavioural signals (retries, "no, I meant" corrections) and a per-user cost range | Speaker-ID and AI meeting-notes examples | Shared launch criteria that include trust and cost | Thresholds left to context; illustrative only | emerging | [nika-2](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-product-sense-part-2.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Output quality vs human baseline / evals*: skill-creator's blind skill vs no-skill A/B ([skill-creator-evals](../research/sources/claude-skill-creator-evals.md)); PMs hand-craft evals to make abstract features concrete ([pm-on-the-ai-exponential](../research/sources/claude-pm-on-the-ai-exponential.md)); Anthropic's analytics agent with snapshot-pinned offline evals, a ~90% per-domain launch gate and ablation on every PR ([self-service-analytics](../research/sources/claude-anthropic-self-service-analytics.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Output quality vs human baseline**: CASH scores each of four loop stages as an eval and hill-climbs them against a rough junior-PM win rate — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party, no method published); a private senior-engineer benchmark (models about 30/100 until GPT 5.5 at about 62; human seniors high 80s to low 90s), with the caveat that benchmarks score already-framed problems — [shipper-paradox](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-ai-paradox.md) (anecdote); LLM judge calibrated to 10-100 human labels with a 90% agreement target — [khan](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-aman-khan-evals-guide.md) (vendor claim); a reference set of 20-100 input/output pairs plus review of the 20-50 worst cases per segment — [cc-cd](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-cc-cd-ai-lifecycle.md) (anecdote); "10 great evals" plus five trusted users' qualitative read — [cat-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-cat-wu-anthropic-product-team.md) (first-party); agents used as judges on 100 production traces missed failures needing product judgment and flagged good outputs, so agent-only grading is unsafe without human calibration — [advanced-evals](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-advanced-evals-error-discovery.md) (anecdote); quote verification (VERIFIED / PARAPHRASE / NOT FOUND) as a cheap gradeable eval for synthesis — [ai-analysis](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-analysis-you-can-trust.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Delivery throughput**: Gusto measured PRs landed via DX (a designer at the 94th percentile; median review nine minutes) — [kim](../research/sources/howiai-gusto-eddie-kim-cofounder.md) (first-party, self-reported); Coinbase cites PR review time cut from 150 to 15 hours across 1,000+ engineers — [coinbase](../research/sources/howiai-coinbase-chintan-turakhia-ai-adoption.md) (first-party, show notes, method unknown); LinkedIn's org-level formula, experiment volume times quality over time to launch, no numbers yet — [cohen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tomer-cohen-full-stack-builder.md) (first-party). Against: lines of code, significant lines and time-to-land all failed as measures at Anthropic — [fung](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-fiona-fung-after-coding-solved.md) (first-party); a quoted Planview finding that enterprises could double dev teams with no appreciable business-outcome gain — [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (quoted third party, method not given); token leaderboards likened to counting lines of code — [graham](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-molly-graham-delegating-to-ai.md) (anecdote); token spend as a vanity metric — [schoening](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-max-schoening-agency.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Usage and merge-share metrics**: Sendbird token tiers (kept out of performance review); Ramp power users at 5+ actions/week; Intercom merged PRs about 20% YoY; Shopify peer-rated AI use in reviews; all input or proxy measures — [sendbird](../research/sources/howiai-sendbird-john-kim-adoption-playbook.md) (first-party), [25-tactics](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-25-ai-adoption-tactics.md) (anecdote). A cheap classifier over PRs and sessions by initiative shows where effort goes — [jev](../research/sources/howiai-claire-vo-jev-decision-model.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Self-reported time saved** is biased: see the tech-workers survey above; Anthropic's CPO measures time saved on real work (a prototype in 20-25 minutes vs six hours) but calls it harder to quantify — [krieger](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-mike-krieger-anthropic-cpo.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Experience quality beyond accuracy: swear words as a frustration proxy; per-team "bad" (unrecoverable) and "sad" (recoverable) events; verifying that the experience is what you wanted is unsolved — [fung](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-fiona-fung-after-coding-solved.md) (first-party). Per-architecture agent metrics (automation rate, human review rate, cost per run) measure the agent, not PM outcomes — [three-agent-categories](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-three-agent-categories.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Evals catch only errors you already know about; Codex relies on regression evals plus A/B tests plus each engineer's hard problems — [reganti-badam](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-reganti-badam-ai-deployments.md) (anecdote), against evals as "the defining skill" — [khan](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-aman-khan-evals-guide.md) (vendor claim). For a novel capability, start with open-ended testing and write evals once the use-case cluster is known — [liu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-howie-liu-airtable-reorg.md) (first-party); reconcilable with error analysis, since both say criteria come from looking at outputs first. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Overlapping rows (contribution merge 2026-10-24): *Draft-retention rate* and *Human touches and draft retention per unit of work* share the draft-retention measure; *Calibrated LLM-judge stack* and *Error-analysis-led evals* share judges calibrated to human labels. Kept as separate rows because each adds a distinct mechanism; merge if preferred.

### Key questions to choose
1. What would a sceptical exec accept as proof that a PM agent helped? — *answered by:* exec/sponsor interviews — *affects:* all
2. Can we get a before/after baseline for the first job in a pilot org? — *answered by:* pilot design with a policy owner — *affects:* throughput, quality
3. Which PM jobs can be framed as classification against a known taxonomy (risk review, feedback routing, spec-completeness checks), so that SME-labelled accuracy becomes the measure? — *answered by:* reviewing the PM jobs map with domain SMEs — *affects:* classification accuracy
- (user-story-mapping) Can each story's success criteria be measured as written (for example 'few gaps in first review', 'reliance matches measured accuracy')? Which need new instrumentation? — *answered by:* review the 15 stories' success criteria with whoever would run an evaluation — *affects:* all evaluation options
7. What would a product-work counterpart to contribution metrics measure (specs accepted, rework, decisions), and is per-user tracking acceptable? Anthropic itself calls PRs "an incomplete measure". — *answered by:* the project owner and PM leads — *affects:* all ([contribution-metrics](../research/sources/claude-code-contribution-metrics.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
8. Can PM SMEs write rubrics good enough for judgement skills (e.g. spec review), and who reviews the evals? — *answered by:* a pilot — *affects:* Rubric-graded outcomes. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
9. How are the vendor headline numbers measured (95% analytics accuracy on live queries; 80% "measurable financial impact" in the agents survey)? — *answered by:* Anthropic data team; Material/Anthropic questionnaire — *affects:* any lean that relies on them. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
10. Would PMs accept an agent that proposes backlog items from metrics and tickets, and what ship rate would make reviewing its proposals worthwhile? — *answered by:* a pilot on one product area that logs why items are rejected — *affects:* proposal funnel, intent-shaping "agent-originated intent" (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
11. Which PM jobs are in the "frontier zone" (possible now, reliable in about six months), so they can be prototyped internally without shipping? — *answered by:* a fixed PM eval suite rerun at each release — *affects:* model-sense log, sequencing (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
12. Who is the named expert who labels PM-agent output pass/fail, how much of their time can we fund, how many reviewed traces until the criteria stop moving, and which failure modes recur (invented decisions, wrong owners)? Coding agents may be a special case (developer as domain expert, constant dogfooding) — *answered by:* a two-week error-analysis pass on about 100 real outputs of one PM-agent job, with a PM as the labelling expert — *affects:* pm-agent-evaluation, review-and-autonomy, sequencing. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
13. What crisp pass/fail signal exists for each candidate PM job (the equivalent of a sanitizer build), and which jobs have none? — *answered by:* a job-by-job inventory of verifiable signals — *affects:* pm-agent-evaluation, sequencing, review-and-autonomy. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
14. Is draft retention (how much of an agent's PRD or brief survives review) a usable quality proxy, or does it reward bland drafts? — *answered by:* comparing retention against blind expert ratings on a sample — *affects:* pm-agent-evaluation, review-and-autonomy. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)


#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Within-user on/off comparison | Compare the same people's outcomes in weeks they use the agent vs weeks they don't; power vs low users in a cohort | Stripe Kai sales metrics (+39% deals) | Cheap, uses existing CRM data | Selection bias and reverse causality; not a controlled test | emerging | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party); [AI Founders critique](../research/sources/aifounders-stripe-kai-dont-copy-sales-numbers.md) (independent) |
| Handling-time reduction plus helpfulness rating on a fixed workflow | Measure median handling time on a decomposed workflow and ask reviewers to rate each agent answer | Stripe compliance agents (-26%, >96% helpful) | Concrete, attributable to one workflow | Narrow; rating can be lenient | early-adopter | [AWS/Stripe compliance post](../research/sources/aws-stripe-compliance-agents.md) (first-party, vendor-hosted) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
6. Can we run a randomised or staggered rollout instead of on/off comparisons, so outcome claims survive the critique Stripe's numbers face? — *answered by:* experiment design with a pilot team — *affects:* all evaluation options



River/Aquifer additions (competitive-teardown-shopify-river, 2026-10-24):

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Outcome-reached per item, not motion | Measure whether each item reaches an evidence-supported outcome (fixed, rejected, escalated); "PR count and green-branch count measure motion, not remediation" | Shopify River security remediation | Guards against vanity metrics like thread counts | Defined for security; PM equivalent unclear | early-adopter | [vuln remediation](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-river-vulnerability-remediation.md) (first-party) |
| Usage and merge-share metrics | Threads, sessions, coauthored-PR share, merge rate | Shopify River (36%→77% merge rate; ~1 in 8 merged PRs; 121,804 threads/month) | Easy to collect from the agent's own tables | No definitions; volume not value (Dealroom) | early-adopter | [Tobi article](../research/sources/x-tobi-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (first-party); [Dealroom](../research/sources/dealroom-river-threads-121804.md) (independent) |

## sequencing: Which jobs first and which layer first
**Choice:** Job level: feedback triage and synthesis (J1, J10); status and "ask the org" (J8); spec/PRD draft and review (J4); prototyping (J3); metrics review (J9); strategy and prioritisation (J2, J5). Stage level: delivery factory first, shaping layer first, or discovery first.
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It decides what gets built or tested first and what evidence we can gather soonest; the two sub-choices ask the same "what first" question at different granularity.
**Related themes:** [[intent-factory-before-discovery]], [[agent-factory-sdlc]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[activity-classification]], [[review-and-autonomy]], [[harness-upkeep]], [[adoption-model]]
Formerly: pm-job-sequencing, investment-sequencing (kept as two sub-choices and presented together, per 01-synthesis.md D.0)

### Sub-choice: pm-job-sequencing — Which PM jobs to automate first
**Choice:** Which PM jobs does the first agent(s) take on: feedback triage and synthesis, status and comms, spec/PRD drafting and review, prototyping, metrics review, or strategy and prioritization?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It decides what gets built or tested first and what evidence we can gather soonest. Related to Geoff's thesis of accelerating intent shaping before discovery.
**Related themes:** [[feedback-triage-agents]], [[status-update-agents]], [[prd-agents]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]], [[intent-factory-before-discovery]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[review-and-autonomy]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Feedback triage and research synthesis | Cluster and route feedback, calls, tickets | Atlassian Rovo categorized 900+ items; Ramp Glass feature-request capture (~20k/month); Notion routing agent; Enterpret, Unwrap, Dovetail | Most claims; bounded; reviewable | Invented or merged quotes, silent omissions; vendor-heavy evidence | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party); [Torres failures](../research/sources/echai-torres-ai-interview-analysis.md) (independent); [Enterpret](../research/sources/enterpret-canva-customer-story.md) (vendor claim) |
| Status, updates and "ask the org" | Draft weekly updates; answer launch/status questions from org context | Atlassian weekly-update agent and CEO's Rovo query; Meta VP decision punch list | Removes PM follow-up work; easy to check | Needs accurate org context; little outcome evidence | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party); [Meta VP piece](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-meta-chawla-pm-leader-agents.md) (first-party; by Nikhyl Singhal, cross-posted by Lenny's Newsletter, not sponsored content) |
| Spec / PRD draft and review | First drafts, gap and edge-case review | ChatPRD, Cowork write-spec, Notion/Atlassian agents | PRDs are PMs' top AI use (21.5%) | Generic output when context thin; no quality comparison vs human PRDs | early-adopter | [Lenny survey](../research/sources/lennys-newsletter-ai-tools-overdelivering-results.md) (independent, self-selected); [Fireside test](../research/sources/fireside-pm-tested-5-ai-tools-prd.md) (independent, weak) |
| Prototyping (spec becomes prototype) | PM or agent builds a working prototype as the spec | Atlassian Loom-to-coding-agent flow; Lovable/Figma Make/v0 | Fast tight iteration; strong PM anecdotes | Breaks as complexity grows; handoff to engineering | early-adopter | [Product Talk roundup](../research/sources/producttalk-ai-prototyping-lovable.md) (independent, candid); [Atlassian](../research/sources/atlassian-ai-turns-pms-into-builders.md) (first-party) |
| Metrics review | Metric Q&A, anomaly flags, adoption analytics | Amplitude, Mixpanel, Pendo agents | Cheap to pilot on existing data | Evidence is vendor launches; interpretation needs judgment | emerging | [Mixpanel](../research/sources/mixpanel-spark-agent-mcp.md) (vendor claim); [Amplitude](../research/sources/amplitude-ai-agents-launch.md) (vendor claim) |
| Strategy, opportunity selection, prioritization | Agent prepares, human decides | Cowork roadmap skill; scoring tools | Where leverage may be highest | Practitioners say judgment stays human; weakest evidence; surveys show strategy lagging | emerging | [SVPG](../research/sources/svpg-four-big-risks.md) (first-party opinion); [Productside survey](../research/sources/productside-state-of-ai-for-pms-2026.md) (vendor survey) |
| Start with connective jobs | Status roll-ups, decks and trackers, where general-agent use already concentrates | Cowork telemetry | Quick wins | Low strategic value | emerging | [how-people-use-cowork](../research/sources/claude-how-people-use-cowork.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Dependency mapping as the first shaping-adjacent job (new row) | Start with the map-ask-record loop on real features, because it needs no platform cooperation and produces the context every later job needs | [06-mvp.md](../tasks/research-dependency-agent/06-mvp.md) | Context that compounds; the lead-time measurement; a standard shape platforms can adopt | Value bounded by owners' verification rate; the slowest dependencies (support functions, third parties) are the hardest to find | emerging | [Tkalich et al.](../research/sources/arxiv-tkalich-critical-dependencies-cse.md) (independent); [Stripe panel](../research/sources/stripe-sessions-2024-10x-engineer-panel.md) (first-party) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Status, updates and "ask the org"**: weekly agent scans of Slack for cross-team misalignment caught cases that would have had teams spinning; cross-functional coordination named as the constraint at larger companies — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party); Cowork chases missing weekly status entries from one prompt — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party); a PM's live incident-command site from Slack and Notion — [korevec](../research/sources/howiai-openai-korevec-chatgpt-sites.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Metrics review** as low-risk and early: a scheduled agent reads 20-25 dashboards each morning — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party); Whatnot's CPO calls data science AI's biggest PM unlock, ahead of prototyping, dependent on data engineering and labelling quality — [verrilli](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tom-verrilli-whatnot-pm.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Job-level ladder: recurring outputs (changelogs, internal newsletters, competitor monitoring) as first candidates for scheduled rubric loops; whole to-dos via connectors as the next rung — [pm-leverage-ladders](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-pm-leverage-ladders.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Which jobs do the target PMs actually find most painful and most repetitive (frequency x pain x cost of error)? — *answered by:* PM interviews and a time diary; scoring the PM jobs map — *affects:* all options
2. Do we agree with the intent-first order (shape intent and delivery before AI discovery)? — *answered by:* the strategy owner — *affects:* order of options
3. For the first job, what baseline would let us show improvement (review time, rework, cycle time)? — *answered by:* a trial with before/after measurement — *affects:* all
4. Which jobs have org data we can legally and technically reach (calls, tickets, analytics)? — *answered by:* data-access inventory with policy owners — *affects:* feedback, metrics, status

- (Track 6) Score each PM job step on the activity axes (predictability, verifiability, repeatability, error cost, audit need, context breadth) before choosing: jobs high on repeatability, audit and context are candidates for a constrained agent; jobs low on all are better left in the general harness. — *answered by:* scoring workshop with PMs and a policy owner — *affects:* order of options; see activity-classification
- (user-story-mapping) Do the cross-cutting needs (S11 continuity, S12 context, S13 hand-off, S15 trust) need to be met before the job stories pay off, or can job stories succeed on their own? The baseline pain describes suggests the former; no evidence tests it. — *answered by:* prototype one job story (S01 or S05) with and without the cross-cutting need met; PM interviews — *affects:* sequencing
5. Is metrics review a better first job than spec drafting for PMs in our org, given data readiness? — *answered by:* a data-catalogue and labelling audit plus a PM time-use survey — *affects:* sequencing, context-and-memory. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Current lean (optional) and what would change it
Evidence so far leans toward bounded, reviewable jobs (feedback triage, status/updates, spec first draft + gap review) as first candidates, because they have the most first-party use and checkable output; it would change if user interviews show a different top pain, or if a trial shows review cost cancels the time saved.


### Sub-choice: investment-sequencing — Where to invest first
**Choice:** Should investment go to the delivery factory first (agentic coding, verification, gates), the intent-shaping layer first, or AI discovery first?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Geoff's thesis is that order matters. Discovery without a faster factory swells the backlog, and a factory without agent-ready intent idles. This choice shapes the next phase of work directly.
**Related themes:** [[intent-factory-before-discovery]], [[agent-factory-sdlc]], [[build-vs-buy]]   **Related dimensions:** [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[loop-source]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Delivery factory first | Sustained capacity for agentic delivery with KRs (one SDD approach, verification, gates) | Stripe Minions, Ramp Inspect | Throughput and quality; frees capacity | Starved by vague intent; engineering-led | early-adopter | [Intent Factory Actions 1–2](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party) |
| Shaping layer first | A shaping skill, spec standard and context repo before or alongside the factory | Ramp Glass as the define-stage agent | Agent-ready intent ready when the factory scales | Little visible delivery gain on its own | early-adopter | [Intent Factory Actions 3–5](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party); [Charles on Lenny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (vendor claim) |
| Discovery first | AI for feedback, insight and opportunity discovery | Productboard Spark, feedback-analysis tools | Visible PM value fast | Balloons the backlog if delivery doesn't scale (Geoff's argument) | early-adopter | [Productboard Spark](../research/sources/productboard-introducing-spark.md) (vendor claim) |
| Adopt plays in dependency order, prompting by hand first | Start with plays that need nothing (intent capture, CLAUDE.md, skills, hooks as gates, feedback loop); automate triggers only later | Playbook dependency graph | Low-risk entry; each play has its own measure | Value of the loop arrives late | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
