| Plan downstream capacity before accelerating upstream | Assume CI load rises 25x within two quarters; fix test selection before scaling agents | Anthropic CI | Avoids the bottleneck moving to CI and review | Infra spend before visible gains | early-adopter | [ci-test-impact](../research/sources/claude-ci-test-impact-analysis.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Surface order: skills, then add-ins, then Cowork | Low-risk, high-reuse surfaces first in a regulated industry | Financial-industry guide | Fits regulated pilots | Contradicts the Cowork guide's ladder, which starts in Cowork | emerging | [financial-guide](../research/sources/claude-guide-financial-industry.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Environment one step ahead of the agent (new row) | Invest in legibility (cards, published intake, measured lead times) before raising the agent's rights | The maturity model's rule | Avoids an agent acting on records nobody trusts | A legibility programme without a consuming agent stalls (the llms.txt result) | n/a | [05-maturity-model.md](../tasks/research-dependency-agent/05-maturity-model.md); [PPC Land](../research/sources/ppcland-llms-txt-adoption-zero-requests.md) (independent) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Delivery factory first** / deterministic first: consultants advise starting with deterministic workflows and adding reasoning or multi-agent only when outgrown — [three-agent-categories](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-three-agent-categories.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Where is our actual bottleneck today: shovel-ready intent, build, review, or release? — *answered by:* value-stream data (lead time by stage) — *affects:* all
2. Will engineering commit sustained capacity to factory work, rather than spending AI gains on new features? — *answered by:* engineering and product leadership — *affects:* delivery-first, shaping-first
3. Can shaping and factory investment run together, so that neither idles? — *answered by:* a joint pilot on one team — *affects:* delivery-first, shaping-first
4. Should a product-agent effort start at intent capture (the playbook's no-prerequisite first play), or downstream where agents already work? Asana's Command reports that automated feedback-to-PR loops bloated cycles until planning was gated ([asana-coach](../research/sources/claude-asana-agents-you-can-coach.md)), which supports intent first. — *answered by:* the strategy; a pilot — *affects:* all. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
5. Which two platforms are the slowest and most depended on, by the agent's own timings, and will their owners publish a card and intake description? — *answered by:* the MVP's first quarter; platform owners — *affects:* where the environment investment goes first (environment one step ahead of the agent) (from research-dependency-agent)

#### Current lean and what would change it
Geoff's doc leans towards delivery-first, then shaping, then discovery. Bottleneck data showing build is not the constraint would change that lean.



River/Aquifer additions (competitive-teardown-shopify-river, 2026-10-24):

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Substrate first, agent second | Make the org legible (monorepo, reproducible environments, written-down skills, fast CI) years before the agent; then ship one agent, then turn it into a platform of profiles | Shopify (2024 bets; River 2026; Aquifer under it) | "Agent-friendly ≈ human-friendly": the prep pays off for humans too | Two years and an order-of-magnitude CI scale-up before payoff; a monorepo migration is out of reach for most | early-adopter | [Under the River](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-under-the-river.md) (first-party) |

Stripe chain additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24): Stripe's whiteboard chain **Opportunity → Problem → Goal → Intent → Plan → (Code)** is a way to read the sequencing choice ([video](../research/sources/youtube-notmybusiness-inside-stripe-ships-fast.md), first-party). At Stripe, agents were built bottom-up: Minions (code, early 2026), then the review agent, then Orbit (plan, shown 2026-10-22). Kai, Harbor and Protodash support research and prototyping across the middle of the chain. Opportunity, problem and goal stay with people and customer conversations.

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bottom-up along the chain (code → review → plan → intent) | Automate delivery first, then planning, and leave the upper stages to people | Stripe (Minions → Gru → Orbit) | Each step has a verifiable artifact (PR, plan) | Starves on vague intent if the upper stages aren't fed (Geoff's Intent Factory warning) | early-adopter | [video](../research/sources/youtube-notmybusiness-inside-stripe-ships-fast.md); [Intent Factory](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party) |
| Context layer first, under every stage | Capture business and operational context (docs, data catalog, skills) before or alongside the agents | Stripe Trailhead verification, data catalog and Toolshed years before Kai and Orbit | Every later agent grounds on it | Slow, cultural, hard to show ROI | early-adopter | [Sessions 2024](../research/sources/stripe-sessions-2024-10x-engineer-panel.md) (first-party) |

Key question (Stripe pass): 9. Which stage of the chain loses the most time or quality for our target PMs today: opportunity-to-goal (human, conversational) or intent-to-plan (Stripe automated it)? — *answered by:* a diary or time study across the six stages with 5-8 PMs — *affects:* delivery factory first, shaping layer first, discovery first


---

## work-structure: How structured the work is, what agent shape fits, and the unit of an agent
**Choice:** Which work stays in a flexible general harness and which moves to a task-specific agent or workflow; and what the unit is (persona, job, stage, chain).
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It is the basis for deciding what to standardise and what to leave unstructured, and the unit sets what we name, evaluate and sell.
**Related themes:** [[one-job-per-agent]], [[single-agent-many-skills]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]], [[pm-persona-role-expansion]]   **Related dimensions:** [[activity-classification]], [[capability-format-portability]], [[sequencing]], [[target-user]]
Formerly: work-structure, agent-unit (agent-unit merged in as "the naming unit", per 01-synthesis.md D.0)

### Sub-choice: work-structure — How structured the work is, and what agent shape fits
**Choice:** Which work stays in a flexible general harness, and which moves into a task-specific agent or workflow?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It is the basis for deciding what to standardise and what to leave unstructured.
**Related themes:** [[one-job-per-agent]], [[single-agent-many-skills]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]]   **Related dimensions:** [[capability-format-portability]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| General harness for all work | One flexible agent | Claude Code, Glass ("one agent, many skills") | Flexibility, one surface | Inconsistent results on standard tasks | mainstream | [Ramp lessons](../research/sources/pragmatic-engineer-ramp-lessons-new-ai-product.md) (first-party) |
| Task-specific agent per job | One agent per defined job | PM-agent jobs map | Consistency, evals, governance | Many agents to maintain | early-adopter | PM agents brief (see tasks/research-pm-agents-jobs-to-be-done/) |
| Hybrid | General harness delegates structured jobs to task agents | Track 6 | Both | Handoff design | to be researched | to be researched |
| One agent, many skills | Single surface; skills carry task shape | Ramp (OmniChat, Glass) | Fewer agents to maintain | Standard jobs may be inconsistent | early-adopter | [Ramp lessons](../research/sources/pragmatic-engineer-ramp-lessons-new-ai-product.md) (first-party: teams built four versions of the same agent) |
| Chain of specialised agents across the SDLC | Insight, define (Glass), build (Inspect), review, test (Testo), coordinate (Gadget), autonomous small loops | Ramp 'software factory' | End-to-end bottleneck removal | Needs mature infra; numbers self-reported | emerging | [Charles on Lenny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (first-party) |
| Role/function plugin packs | Vendor- or org-authored bundles of skills per business function, installed by role | Anthropic Cowork knowledge-work plugins (11, incl. Product Management: write-spec, roadmap, progress tracking); PayPal planned function/persona "Agent Plugins" (Sales, HR, IT, Developer) | Fast start for non-engineers; a unit that can be assigned and measured by role | No outcome data from either vendor; skills inside remain general-harness prompts, not enforced workflows; SkillsBench shows gains vary hugely by domain (Healthcare +51.9pp, Software Engineering +4.5pp) and 16 of 84 tasks got worse | emerging | [Cowork PM plugins](../research/sources/anthropic-cowork-plugins-product-management.md) (vendor claim); [PayPal](../research/sources/paypal-agent-skills-at-enterprise-scale.md) (first-party); [SkillsBench](../research/sources/arxiv-skillsbench.md) (independent) |
| Fire-and-forget autonomous delegation | A whole task handed to a cloud agent that returns a PR or report | Codex cloud, Claude Code on the web, Jules, Copilot cloud agent, Devin | Parallel throughput; works while the user is away | Only independent test (Answer.AI, Jan 2025, Devin): 3 of 20 succeeded, success unpredictable; merge rates differ by tool (Willison's PRarena snapshot) | early-adopter | [Answer.AI](../research/sources/answerai-thoughts-month-with-devin.md) (independent) (independent, dated); [Willison](../research/sources/simonwillison-async-coding-agents-tag.md) (independent) (independent) |
| Read-only bounded task agent exposed to many hosts | Short, non-interactive, read-only agent published by an admin and reached from whichever chat or coding host the user has | Glean agents as MCP tools | Consistent structured result across hosts | Vendor itself excludes write tools and human-in-the-loop steps and advises under ~30s runtime: suits review/analysis, not iterative drafting | early-adopter | [Glean](../research/sources/glean-agents-as-mcp-tools.md) (vendor) (vendor claim) |
| Loops by what is handed off | Turn-based (hand off the check), goal-based (the stop condition), time-based (the trigger), proactive (the prompt) | Claude Code /loop, /goal, /schedule | A vocabulary for which PM jobs can run unattended | Several primitives in research preview | emerging | [loop-engineering](../research/sources/claude-loop-engineering.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Model-generated workflow per task | The model writes the orchestration at run time (tens to hundreds of subagents), with adversarial verification | Dynamic workflows | Fits work whose steps aren't known in advance | Token cost; the script differs every run, hard to audit | emerging | [dynamic-workflows](../research/sources/claude-code-dynamic-workflows.md) (first-party), [ramp-office-hours](../research/sources/claude-customers-ramp-office-hours.md) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Written human-agent roster | Every member, human or agent, has a defined role; agents defined in skill files; only human-chosen agents may propose new work | Anthropic internal teams | Clarity on who owns what in a mixed team | Overhead; roles drift | emerging | [human-agent-teams](../research/sources/claude-building-effective-human-agent-teams.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Builder plus fresh-context critic loop | A cheaper model builds; a stronger model in a fresh context sees only the output (e.g. a screenshot), scores it against a concrete comparative bar, and the loop ends when the critic independently passes it | Design critic loop (critic under 10% of output tokens); "Bob the Builder and Ray the Reviewer" | Review without the builder's sunk-cost bias; cheaper than strong-model-does-everything | Needs objective criteria and stopping rules or it burns tokens; shown for visual design, unproven for specs | early-adopter | [chimala](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-world-class-designer.md) (anecdote); [roth](../research/sources/howiai-linkedin-daniel-roth-dual-agents.md) (anecdote, show notes only) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |


##### Added evidence and options (research-pm-agents-jobs-to-be-done, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Task-specific agent per PM job (scoped, human in loop) | One agent per defined PM job, e.g. feedback triage, weekly status, PRD review | Atlassian PM-built weekly-update agent and Rovo feedback categorizer (900+ items); Microsoft multi-agent feedback system (specialist agents with own schema/guardrails); Vercel one-job agents (lead processing, abuse, data analyst) | Consistency, evals per job, clear review point | Many agents to maintain; evidence is talk anecdotes and vendor claims, no head-to-head vs a skill | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party, no baselines); [Microsoft](../research/sources/microsoft-feedback-noise-multi-agent.md) (vendor claim); [Vercel](../research/sources/vercel-sweet-spot-agentic-ai.md) (first-party, not PM) |
| Persona agent / "PM agent" (one agent doing the PM's whole job) | A role-shaped assistant bundling many PM jobs | Claude Cowork PM plugin (write-spec, roadmap, updates); ChatPRD; Meta VP's personal agents (review-doc drafting, decision punch list, analytics) | Easy to explain; one thing to adopt | Blurry scope, hard to evaluate; Meta write-up: a VP's personal system, "six months in, it's accurate" per nightly self-checks; one brief dropped source links | emerging | [Cowork PM plugin](../research/sources/anthropic-cowork-plugins-product-management.md) (vendor claim, no outcomes); [Meta VP piece](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-meta-chawla-pm-leader-agents.md) (first-party; by Nikhyl Singhal, cross-posted by Lenny's Newsletter, not sponsored content); [Fireside PM five-tool test](../research/sources/fireside-pm-tested-5-ai-tools-prd.md) (independent, single author) |
| General harness plus skills for PM work (no dedicated agents) | PMs use Claude Code/Glass/Cursor with PM skills | Ramp Glass (feature-request capture skill, specs from connected data); Claude Code for PMs | Flexibility; no new surface | Inconsistent on standard tasks; PM-specific outcome evidence thin | early-adopter | [Ramp Glass post](../research/sources/x-eglyman-introducing-glass-learn-things-once.md) (first-party); [Cursor for PMs](../research/sources/lenny-cursor-better-product-manager.md) (anecdote) |

Evidence caveat: no source found compares a task agent with a skill in a general harness; the Atlassian chain (Jira agent in Slack triages, then hands to a coding agent) suggests hybrids are in use but is only described in a talk.
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Workflow engine with embedded agents | Deterministic steps and agent steps in one graph; the engine owns control flow, the model fills steps | LangGraph, Google ADK workflow agents, Microsoft Agent Framework workflows, CrewAI Flows, Pydantic Graph, Stripe Minions blueprints (deterministic nodes around a Goose loop) | Predictability, evals per step, checkpoint/resume, audit; Microsoft's guidance: if you can write a function, do that | Less flexible for exploratory work; a second control-flow language to maintain | mainstream (workflow apps); early-adopter for coding agents | [Building effective agents](../research/sources/anthropic-building-effective-agents.md) (first-party); [MS Agent Framework](../research/sources/microsoft-agent-framework-overview.md) (first-party); [Stripe Minions](../research/sources/stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents.md) (first-party); [LangGraph](../research/sources/langchain-docs-langgraph-overview.md) (first-party) |

##### Added evidence and options (Track 6, research-agent-runtime-patterns-taxonomy, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Structure added on demand inside the general harness | One harness; the user or team adds structure when the job needs it: vague prompts to explore, plan mode for uncertain work, skills for repeatable workflows, hooks and Stop hooks as deterministic gates, subagent review, `claude -p` for unattended fan-out | Claude Code (explore/plan/verify ladder; fix-issue skill with side effects manual-only) | No second system; the same surface spans exploration and routine work | Skills are advisory and run with the host's permissions; consistency on standard jobs unmeasured | mainstream | [Claude Code best practices](../research/sources/anthropic-claude-code-best-practices.md) (first-party); [Agent Skills](../research/sources/anthropic-engineering-equipping-agents-agent-skills.md) (first-party) |
| Coordinator plus narrow specialists for a standardised job | A fixed set of single-purpose reviewers, each told what to flag and what not to, merged by a coordinator that dedupes and grades severity; risk-tiered effort | Cloudflare AI code review (7 specialists, 131,246 runs in a month, avg $1.19) | Less noise than one big prompt; cost scales with risk; auditable override | Needs a platform team; code review is verifiable, PRD review is less so; a first-party account | early-adopter | [Cloudflare](../research/sources/cloudflare-ai-code-review-opencode.md) (first-party); [CR-Bench](../research/sources/arxiv-cr-bench.md) (independent, preliminary) |
| Deterministic wrapper around an agentic core | Required steps (lint, tests, CI cap, hydration) run as code; open-ended work runs as an agent loop; humans review the output | Stripe Minions | Guaranteed team-required steps with agent creativity | Wrapper must be built and maintained; coding-specific | early-adopter | [Stripe](../research/sources/stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents.md) (first-party) |
| Constrain by default, loosen with evidence | Short bounded loops with human checkpoints and human evaluation; scope grows as quality is shown | Deployed agents in the Berkeley/Stanford production study; 12-factor "small focused agents" | Reliability first; easy to test | May cap value on open-ended work; scope-growth rule is untested | early-adopter | [Measuring Agents in Production](../research/sources/arxiv-measuring-agents-in-production.md) (independent); [12-factor](../research/sources/humanlayer-12-factor-agents-small-focused-agents.md) (first-party) |
| Expect structure to decay: invest in context, checks and permissions, not model-compensating workflow | Prune scaffolding as models improve; keep verification, rubrics, audit | Claude Code team practice (second-hand) | Avoids sunk cost in workflows a new model makes redundant | Second-hand summary only; durable moat claims exist on the other side | emerging | [Traces summary](../research/sources/traces-boris-cherny-light-cone-summary.md) (anecdote, second-hand) |

Evidence note: still no controlled comparison of a task agent vs a skill in a general harness (key question 2 above). Track 6 report: [track-6-work-structure-fit](../tasks/research-agent-runtime-patterns-taxonomy/track-6-work-structure-fit.md).

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *One agent, many skills*: Anthropic now says a single agent with progressively disclosed skills "frequently outperformed" both monolith prompts and subagent designs on quality, often cost and latency; subagents only for context bloat, parallel discardable work, or tool conflicts ([fold-subagents](../research/sources/claude-fold-subagents-into-main-loop.md), first-party, 2026-10-22); Vercel's d0 runs on about 100 skills ([vercel](../research/sources/claude-customers-vercel-skills-ecosystem.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Chain of specialised agents across the SDLC*: Notion's investigate, plan and build agents ([notion](../research/sources/claude-customers-notion-managed-agents.md)); Ramp's agents for ideas, bugs, alerts, code, review and post-deploy ([ramp-office-hours](../research/sources/claude-customers-ramp-office-hours.md)); ABC Legal's "a name, an owner, and a single job" ([abc-legal](../research/sources/claude-abc-legal-managed-agents-builders.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Hybrid*: Anthropic's five coordination patterns, default orchestrator-subagent then evolve ([multi-agent-patterns](../research/sources/claude-multi-agent-coordination-patterns.md), April 2026), now tempered by the October fold-in guidance. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **One agent, many skills** / **Expect structure to decay**: OpenAI's head of ChatGPT calls loops, graphs and fine-tuned prompts a passing phase; his agent teams expand at the frontier and shrink when a better model arrives — [sottiaux](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sottiaux-chatgpt-dots.md) (vendor claim); the head of Claude Code says giving the model tools and a goal almost always wins, and scaffolding gains of 10-20% vanish with the next model — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party, with an interest); run one always-on data agent everyone calls rather than keep many consistent — [cw-data-integrity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-agents-data-integrity.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Chain of specialised agents** / **Coordinator plus narrow specialists**: Mozilla's analyzer/verifier/patcher split outperforms with structured handoffs and a crisp signal — [mozilla](../research/sources/howiai-mozilla-mythos-bug-harness.md) (first-party); LinkedIn built single-job agents so each could be graded, orchestration later — [cohen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tomer-cohen-full-stack-builder.md) (first-party); OpenAI splits problems into many model calls — [weil](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-kevin-weil-openai-cpo.md) (first-party); one LLM call per analysis job for consistency — [cw-comm-architectures](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-communication-architectures.md) (anecdote); one prompt doing UX, data and code yields "an average" — [mehta](../research/sources/howiai-ravi-mehta-data-driven-prototyping.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Task-specific agent per job**: a Sentry bug-triage harness on the Claude Agent SDK, reserved for a repetitive structured workflow — [claire-vo-sdk](../research/sources/howiai-claire-vo-agent-sdk-harness.md) (anecdote, show notes). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Fire-and-forget autonomous delegation**: PMs and tech leads are among Devin's heaviest users because they already scope and hand off — [scott-wu-devin](../research/sources/howiai-scott-wu-devin-junior-engineer.md) (vendor claim). Google Labs project teams fell from 5-7 to 2-4 people — [woodward](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-josh-woodward-labs-team.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Workflow engine with embedded agents** / **Deterministic wrapper around an agentic core**: a three-type taxonomy (deterministic workflow, reasoning agent, multi-agent network) with fit tests and claimed client shares of 60-70 / 25-30 / under 5-10%; Claude Code itself is layered (loop plus hooks plus sub-agents), so the question is which layer for which part — [three-agent-categories](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-three-agent-categories.md) (anecdote); slow data lookups belong in deterministic workflows, not chat tool calls — [zapier-mcp](../research/sources/howiai-zapier-reid-robinson-mcp-pm-workflows.md) (vendor claim); script the stable, prompt the creative (release notes) — [cw-prototypes-release](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prototypes-release-comms.md) (anecdote); three ladders (assist, delegate with review, self-checking loops) — [pm-leverage-ladders](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-pm-leverage-ladders.md) (anecdote); functions as cascading agent loops with humans on direction and exceptions — [acharya](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-anish-acharya-company-loops.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Which jobs are standardisable enough to deserve a task agent (frequency, variance, cost of error)? — *answered by:* PM jobs map scored with users — *affects:* all
2. What evidence is there that task agents outperform a skill in a general harness? — *answered by:* Track 6 research, a bake-off prototype — *affects:* task-agent options
3. Ramp consolidated from many agents to one with skills; does that hold for regulated or high-error-cost PM jobs? — *answered by:* bake-off prototype on one standard job — *affects:* general harness vs task agent
4. Which job families gain most from curated skills? SkillsBench suggests larger gains in domains models handle poorly (healthcare, manufacturing) and small ones in software engineering: does that hold for product-management jobs? - *answered by:* run a PM-job benchmark with and without the Cowork PM plugin and a custom skill; more research - *affects:* function plugin packs, which jobs to automate first
5. For the PM jobs we would standardise, does a task-specific agent beat a skill in a general harness on output quality and review effort? — *answered by:* bake-off prototype on 1-2 jobs (feedback triage, PRD review) with a human-scored rubric — *affects:* task-agent vs general vs hybrid (added by research-pm-agents-jobs-to-be-done)
6. Does the PM (or the org) want to adopt "a PM agent" or a set of named job agents? — *answered by:* PM user interviews — *affects:* persona vs job options (see agent-unit)
7. Cognition reports multi-agent crews make conflicting implicit decisions while Anthropic reports gains from orchestrator-worker systems: for our jobs, is one context-sharing agent plus skills better than a crew? — *answered by:* bake-off on one structured job (single agent + skill vs orchestrator-worker) — *affects:* task-agent and hybrid options ([source](../research/sources/cognition-dont-build-multi-agents.md), first-party)
- (Track 3) 3. Which target jobs are read-only, non-interactive and short enough to ship as an agent behind MCP today (a PRD review that returns findings) versus interactive and write-heavy (drafting a PRD) that must stay in a harness or use Tasks/input_required? — *answered by:* classify the PM jobs map against Glean's constraints; prototype — *affects:* task agent behind MCP vs general harness

- (Track 6) Is the step, not the person or the tool, the unit we classify? A PRD job mixes an open step (framing), a standardised step (review against a team standard) and an accountable step (ratify). — *answered by:* walk three real PM jobs end to end with users and mark each step's class — *affects:* all options (see activity-classification)
- (Track 6) For a standard such as PRD review, is the standard a rubric of judgment criteria or a completeness checklist, and can SMEs label 20+ past PRDs against it? — *answered by:* policy/standard owner; SME labelling exercise; Cloudflare-style specialist-plus-exclusions prototype — *affects:* coordinator plus specialists, task-agent options
- (Track 6) Does a gate need to be enforced (hook, workflow node, deployed service) or is a skill plus logged human sign-off enough for the jobs we pick? — *answered by:* risk/compliance owner; compare a skill+hook prototype with a workflow prototype — *affects:* structure inside general harness vs workflow engine vs deployed agent
- (Track 6) How fast does structure decay: would the model-compensating parts of a task workflow be redundant in 6-12 months, leaving only checks, rubrics and permissions? — *answered by:* re-run the same job with the newest model with and without the scaffold; vendor roadmap review — *affects:* all task-specific options
- (Track 6) Is there any ideation or planning outcome we can measure (decision quality, options generated, time to a decision), or is "leave it unstructured" an untestable default? — *answered by:* user interviews; a small trial with a rubric scored by the decision owner — *affects:* general harness for open work
- (user-story-mapping) For which of the 18 stories would a PM prefer a task-specific agent over a skill in a general harness, and does the preference follow the story's shape (recurring and standardised versus exploratory)? The S05 journeys A and B are the first pair to compare. — *answered by:* run the S05 pair as clickable prototypes with 5–8 PMs; compare preference and trust — *affects:* general harness plus skills, task-specific agent, hybrid
8. Does the fold-subagents finding hold for internal PM and knowledge agents, not only customer-facing ones? It directly challenges one-job-per-agent evidence filed earlier. — *answered by:* Anthropic Applied AI's comparison data; an internal A/B — *affects:* One agent many skills vs Chain of specialised agents. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
9. Which PM jobs have deterministic stop criteria (fit for /goal) and which need a human-judged stop? — *answered by:* PMs plus the eval owner — *affects:* Loops by what is handed off. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
10. Should a PM harness's critics see only the artifact (spec, prototype) and not the conversation that produced it? — *answered by:* an A/B on critique quality with and without session context — *affects:* builder plus critic loop; trust-privacy-defaults (task-context isolation). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)


### Sub-choice: agent-unit — Persona, job, or stage as the unit of an agent
**Choice:** Is the unit we standardise a persona (a "PM agent"), a job (e.g. feedback triage), a lifecycle stage (discovery, shaping, delivery), or a workflow chaining several?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It sets what we name, evaluate and sell, and how the PM jobs map is turned into a product. Overlaps [[work-structure]] but concerns the unit's definition, not whether it is a skill or agent.
**Related themes:** [[one-job-per-agent]], [[pm-persona-role-expansion]], [[pm-job-stable-methods-change]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[capability-format-portability]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Persona agent | "PM agent" bundling a role's jobs | Cowork PM plugin; Meta VP personal agents | Maps to how buyers think | Roles are expanding, so the persona keeps changing (Atlassian: "roles are expanding"); hard to evaluate | emerging | [Cowork](../research/sources/anthropic-cowork-plugins-product-management.md) (vendor claim); [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party) |
| Job agent | One agent per job | Atlassian weekly-update agent; Vercel one-job agents | Evaluable, composable | Many units | early-adopter | [Vercel](../research/sources/vercel-sweet-spot-agentic-ai.md) (first-party) |
| Stage agent | One agent per lifecycle stage | Ramp: Glass as the define-stage agent in a software factory | Fits an end-to-end pipeline | Large scope per agent | emerging | [Charles podcast](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (first-party) |
| Workflow chain | Agents hand off across jobs | Atlassian Loom -> work item -> coding agent; Jira agent -> coding agent | End-to-end flow | Handoff design, failure handling | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Persona agent** (mostly individual use): 13 role agents preloaded with the data model in a healthcare company — [cw-pms-coding-tools](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-pms-ai-coding-tools.md) (anecdote); five household role agents whose handoffs break — [genet](../research/sources/howiai-jesse-genet-openclaw-agents.md) (anecdote, show notes); a designer's PM/designer/engineer bot group chat — [grok-bot-designers](../research/sources/howiai-grok-bot-designers-agents.md) (anecdote); nine narrow OpenClaw agents, narrower identity did better — [openclaw-guide](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-claire-vo-openclaw-guide.md), two similar scheduling agents split to avoid context overload — [claire-vo-openclaw](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-claire-vo-openclaw.md) (anecdote); Grok Bot users ran 5-10 lane bots and promoted one to chief of staff unprompted — [ugarte](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-roman-ugarte-grok-bot.md) (vendor claim). Caution: peer-to-peer systems split by function are "misunderstood"; supervisor plus sub-agents works — [reganti-badam](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-reganti-badam-ai-deployments.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Do PMs in the target orgs think in personas, jobs or stages when they ask for help? — *answered by:* PM interviews, card sort of the jobs map — *affects:* all
2. Are personas stable enough to build on (founder-PM vs IC PM vs CPO differ)? — *answered by:* persona research with each type — *affects:* persona agent
8. Where do persona sub-agents hand off to each other and what carries the state (shared file, message, shared session)? Practitioners report handoffs as the weak point — *answered by:* prototype tests of handoff artifacts — *affects:* work-structure, session-persistence-sharing. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Federation of specialised agents linked over MCP | Keep agents specialised (research, coding, browser prototyping) and expose each one's capabilities to the others | Stripe Kai + Harbor + Minions | Each agent tuned for its job; chained work without copy-paste | Cross-agent identity and context transfer | early-adopter | [Harbor post](../research/sources/stripe-harbor-ai-prototyping.md) (first-party) |
| Human-driven DAG with agent research per sub-question (evidence row for workflow engine with embedded agents) | Review tooling orchestrates a DAG of quality-tested sub-questions; agents pre-fetch research; humans answer each | Stripe compliance review | Bounded context, measured quality, audit | Needs workflow decomposition and per-question evals | early-adopter | [AWS/Stripe compliance post](../research/sources/aws-stripe-compliance-agents.md) (first-party, vendor-hosted) |
| Orchestration in code over hardened reusable prompts (evidence row for deterministic wrapper) | Plan of steps, one prompt and one PR per step, coordinated by code; an orchestrator LLM was dropped as slow and unreliable | Stripe payment-method factory | Predictable, reviewable steps | Prompt upkeep (solved with a learning loop) | early-adopter | [payment-method factory](../research/sources/stripe-payment-method-factory.md) (first-party) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
7. Stripe says one agent architecture fits coding but knowledge work differs per task, yet chose one general agent plus skills and projects (not many agents) after 4,000 no-code agents sprawled. Where is our line between "skill in the general agent" and "specialised agent"? — *answered by:* sort the PM jobs map by tools, data and definition of done; prototype the two most different jobs — *affects:* one agent many skills, task-specific agents, federation


---

## activity-classification: How we classify user activity for agent fit
**Choice:** Do we classify work by a single structure axis (structured vs unstructured), by several task properties, or by what workers want to hand off?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The classification is how we decide which jobs get a constrained agent or workflow and which stay in a flexible harness; the wrong axis misroutes work.
**Related themes:** [[work-structure-spectrum]], [[one-job-per-agent]], [[single-agent-many-skills]], [[human-judgment-pm-jobs]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[sequencing]], [[review-and-autonomy]]

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Two classes: structured vs unstructured | Binary split by whether the path can be written down | Anthropic workflows vs agents | Simple; matches the working hypothesis | Hides the middle; most production systems are hybrids | mainstream | [Building effective agents](../research/sources/anthropic-building-effective-agents.md) (first-party); [LangChain](../research/sources/langchain-how-to-think-about-agent-frameworks.md) (vendor claim, "how agentic") |
| Multi-axis task properties | Score each job step on predictability, verifiability, repeatability, error cost, audit need, context breadth, collaboration | Google's requirement-driven decision table; Track 6 axes A1-A7 | Explains why review and policy work constrain while ideation does not | Axes are a synthesis, untested with users | emerging | [Google design patterns](../research/sources/google-cloud-choose-design-pattern-agentic-ai.md) (vendor claim); [Track 6 report](../tasks/research-agent-runtime-patterns-taxonomy/track-6-work-structure-fit.md) (judgment) |
| Worker-desired agency | Classify by how much human involvement the person wants (Human Agency Scale H1-H5) crossed with agent capability | Stanford WORKBank (844 tasks, 104 occupations) | Captures adoption and trust, not only feasibility | Self-reported; O*NET tasks, not PM jobs | emerging | [Future of Work with AI Agents](../research/sources/arxiv-future-of-work-with-ai-agents.md) (independent) |
| Usage-derived pattern | Classify by how people actually use the agent: directive/automation vs iterative/augmentation | Anthropic Economic Index | Grounded in logs | Reflects access route (API vs chat) as much as the job | emerging | [Economic Index Jan 2026](../research/sources/anthropic-economic-index-2026-01.md) (first-party) |
| Three-axis automation canvas (safety, volume, verifiability) | Score each task 1–3 on safety-if-wrong, volume and how easily the output can be checked. Plot verifiability against volume, coloured by safety, then map each task to automate now, AI assistant, or not ready | DoP AI Automation Opportunity Canvas, with a scoring prompt, a pre-mortem and a workshop mini-app | Cheap, workshop-ready and in PM vocabulary. The "assistant" tier hands over naturally to review-and-autonomy | The author's own framework, not validated. Three axes leave out context breadth, collaboration and audit need (Track 6 axes) | emerging | [DoP automation opportunities](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-ai-automation-opportunities.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Repetition report mined from the harness | The harness reads one person's session history and flags same-shaped repeated tasks as candidates for a command or skill | Claude Code `/insights` | Bottom-up discovery from real usage, not from a workshop | One user's view only. No evidence that people act on it | emerging | [DoP slash commands](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-loops-goals-slash-commands.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- **Correction for the owning task:** the [Stripe Kai DoP note](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-stripe-kai.md) describes this canvas as "frequency vs volume". The source post's axes are safety, volume and verifiability. (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)

### Key questions to choose
1. Which axes actually predict where a general harness underperforms a constrained agent for PM jobs? — *answered by:* score 10-20 PM job steps on the axes, then test the extremes in a bake-off — *affects:* all options
2. Does the person's wish for control (agency) diverge from task structure for PM jobs (for example people wanting to keep prioritisation but hand off synthesis)? — *answered by:* a short WORKBank-style survey of target PMs — *affects:* worker-desired agency vs multi-axis
3. Is the classification stable across users and over model generations, or must it be re-run per team and per model release? — *answered by:* repeat the scoring with two teams and again after a model upgrade — *affects:* all options
4. Do three axes (safety, volume, verifiability) sort PM job steps as well as the seven Track 6 axes, so the simpler canvas could be used in user workshops? — *answered by:* scoring the same 10–20 steps both ways and comparing against bake-off results — *affects:* three-axis canvas, multi-axis task properties (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
5. Do the candidates mined from usage (`/insights`) match what workshops pick? — *answered by:* a pilot with 3–5 PMs: report first, then workshop, then compare — *affects:* repetition report, usage-derived pattern (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)


---

## review-and-autonomy: Review and autonomy
**Choice:** Where gates sit (human every time; automated verifiers plus spot check; isolation and blast-radius limits; risk-sloped autonomy; upstream risk classification at shaping time) and who sets autonomy (fixed by policy per task class; user-set slider; earned from measured accuracy; classifier-gated).
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Review is where agentic throughput meets regulatory accountability; the documented failures are review problems, and autonomy determines review effort and risk. Gates are "where", calibration is "who sets how much".
**Related themes:** [[risk-sloped-review]], [[human-review-gates]], [[risk-review-as-classification]], [[evals-autonomy-trust]], [[ai-synthesis-hallucination-risk]]   **Related dimensions:** [[governance-control-plane]], [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[activity-classification]], [[sequencing]]
Formerly: review-gating, review-gate-placement, autonomy-calibration, plus two review rows from governance-control-plane (merged per 01-synthesis.md D.0)
See also: [[governance-control-plane]] (gates as data), [[dependency-knowledge-and-intake]]. (from research-dependency-agent)

### Sub-choice: review-gating — How human review of agent output is gated
**Choice:** Does every agent change get human review, is review sloped to scored risk, or does risk review move upstream into intent shaping (classifying risk before anything is built)?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Review is where agentic throughput meets regulatory accountability. The gating model sets how much automation is actually realised, and it is the hardest sell in a regulated organisation.
**Related themes:** [[risk-sloped-review]], [[risk-review-as-classification]], [[evals-autonomy-trust]]   **Related dimensions:** [[governance-control-plane]], [[intent-shaping-and-spec]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Human review on every change | A person reviews every agent PR; code owners review sensitive areas | Stripe Minions ("not moving money without human review") | Clear accountability; easy to defend to regulators | Review becomes the bottleneck as agent volume grows (Stripe: ~7,000 PRs/week reported) | mainstream | [Intent Factory §Vision 7](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed in Geoff's doc) |
| Risk-scored routing with partial auto-approve | PRs scored for complexity or risk; low-risk ones auto-approved; PRs opened under the requester's identity so no one approves their own agent's work | Ramp Inspect and Review Buddy | Throughput; human attention goes where risk is | Needs a trusted scorer and audit trail; regulators may not accept it | early-adopter | [Intent Factory §Vision 7](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed); [Charles on Lenny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (vendor claim: Review Buddy handles 93% of PRs) |
| Upstream risk classification at shaping time | A risk-advisor agent classifies the spec against a MECE risk taxonomy before build; mitigations become requirements; novel risks go to SMEs | Geoff's risk-agent concept | Catches risk while the plan can still change; consistent; measurable against SME labels | Needs a curated taxonomy and heuristics; untested in practice | emerging (concept) | [Intent Shaping in a Well-Managed Environment](../research/sources/geoff-intent-shaping-well-managed-environment.md) (first-party, Geoff's concept) |
| Logged human override on an approval-biased AI reviewer | AI findings block only for critical issues; a human can force approval, recorded in telemetry | Cloudflare "break glass" (0.6% of MRs) | Keeps AI from becoming a bottleneck; measurable disagreement | Bias to approval is a policy choice; first-party figures | early-adopter | [Cloudflare](../research/sources/cloudflare-ai-code-review-opencode.md) (first-party) |
| Guardrails as commit checks instead of gate meetings (review-gating) | Checkable standards (tokens, accessibility, i18n, contrast) become lint rules and pre-commit hooks that neither a junior nor an agent can bypass; finalisation is continuous; a version pin separates release QA | A community member's mail-client team | Removes milestone reviews for mechanical rules; safe to let agents commit | Only mechanically checkable rules; judgment review remains | early-adopter | [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Separated agent roles as a guardrail for non-engineer changes | QA agent cannot edit code, coding agent cannot edit tests; a checker agent blocks disallowed changes (new modules, packages); engineers PR-review PM changes | Practitioners' accounts ("me plus Cursor as a junior engineer") | Lets PMs and designers make small production changes safely | Engineers must build the guardrails first, which removes much of the need | emerging | [cw-cursor-pm](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-cursor-for-pm-work.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Behavioral testing replaces code review | No one reads agent code; a swarm of simulated users exercises the system against simulated dependencies | StrongDM "dark factory" (simulated Slack/Jira/Okta, about $10k/day in tokens), as relayed | Removes the review bottleneck; tests run 24/7 | Very high token cost; passing tests does not mean secure; one firm; hard to defend in a regulated audit | emerging (experimental) | [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (anecdote, second-hand) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Risk-tiered auto-approval on an easier population (evidence row for Gates as data, governance-control-plane) | Routine, low-risk requests approved by policy with reverts and incidents measured; the rest to people. The full option is *Gates as data: evidence packet in, policy decision out* in [[governance-control-plane]]; this row adds the measured outcomes and is also evidence for risk-sloped review | Meta RADAR: 331K+ changes, a third the revert rate, a fiftieth the incident rate, on an eligible population | The run-stage pattern with measured outcomes | ING: near-chance risk scores on half-empty fields; rank, do not gate | early-adopter | [Adams et al.](../research/sources/arxiv-adams-meta-radar-low-risk-review.md), [Kapel et al.](../research/sources/arxiv-kapel-ing-change-incident-prediction.md) (first-party, peer-reviewed) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Risk-scored routing with partial auto-approve*: Anthropic tiers its codebase by risk, keeps human review "for regulated or truly critical code" and samples automated approvals ([securing-sdlc](../research/sources/claude-securing-ai-native-sdlc-at-anthropic.md), first-party); League's bot triages every PR by size and risk ([league](../research/sources/claude-customers-league.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Upstream risk classification at shaping time*: Anthropic's Opus-powered project security review checks design docs, and teams self-approve when it rates a launch low risk ([securing-sdlc](../research/sources/claude-securing-ai-native-sdlc-at-anthropic.md), first-party). This is a live first-party instance of the concept. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Human review on every change**: Symphony routes every run to a human-review state and a rework state driven by PR comments; the human is the throughput limit — [symphony](../research/sources/howiai-alessio-fanelli-symphony-linear.md) (anecdote); v0 routes non-engineer changes through branch preview and PR review — [v0-git](../research/sources/howiai-guillermo-rauch-v0-git-workflow.md) (vendor claim); Warp still reviews 100% of PRs but dropped the two-person rule; review latency (3.5 h to first review vs 35 min to PR) is the measured bottleneck — [warp-factory](../research/sources/howiai-warp-zach-lloyd-software-factory.md) (vendor claim); Mozilla humans still review every fix and broaden narrow patches — [mozilla](../research/sources/howiai-mozilla-mythos-bug-harness.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Risk-scored routing with partial auto-approve**: Warp risk-scores every PR and auto-stamps low-risk ones — [warp-factory](../research/sources/howiai-warp-zach-lloyd-software-factory.md) (vendor claim); at OpenAI Codex reviews 100% of PRs, review time fell from 10-15 to 2-3 minutes, small PRs sometimes skip human review — [sherwin-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sherwin-wu-openai-codex.md) (first-party); a solo founder auto-merges about 40 PRs a day — [carson](../research/sources/howiai-ryan-carson-devin-20k-month.md) (anecdote, show notes). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Regulated: a life-sciences PM says product leaders should own the safety eval list, online monitoring and the rollback trigger — [kaul](../research/sources/howiai-prerna-kaul-fda-submissions.md) (first-party, thin). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. What review evidence do our regulators and second-line risk functions actually require for agent-written changes, and would risk-scored auto-approval satisfy it? — *answered by:* risk, compliance and audit policy owners — *affects:* all
2. Does a documented risk taxonomy with applicability heuristics already exist, and is it complete enough to classify against? — *answered by:* operational-risk and compliance teams; a sample of past risk reviews — *affects:* upstream classification
3. How accurate is an agent at classifying risk compared with SME reviewers (false positives and negatives on past initiatives)? — *answered by:* a prototype risk agent run on labelled historical reviews — *affects:* upstream classification, risk-scored routing
4. What share of today's changes are low-risk enough to auto-approve, and what is human review costing in elapsed time? — *answered by:* SDLC and PR data analysis — *affects:* risk-scored routing

- (user-story-mapping) How long does a PM currently take to check agent output for each job story, relative to the time it saves ([S15](../tasks/user-story-mapping/stories-cross-cutting.md#s15-know-whether-i-can-trust-what-came-back))? — *answered by:* timed checks on 3–4 real outputs (S01, S05, S09) — *affects:* all gating options; frames the cost of 'human review on every change'
5. What accuracy did Anthropic's project security review reach before low-risk self-approval was allowed? — *answered by:* Anthropic security — *affects:* Upstream risk classification at shaping time. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
6. With non-engineers shipping code (support at Gumroad, PMs at Twilio, designers at League), what review applies to their changes? — *answered by:* engineering leads at those firms; a pilot — *affects:* all. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
7. When output volume rises, does human review stay real or become rubber-stamping? — *answered by:* time-per-review and defect escape before and after agent adoption on one team — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, pm-agent-evaluation. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
8. Must the reviewer of agent output be someone other than the person who prompted it? — *answered by:* audit and compliance requirements in the target enterprise; review-defect data — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, governance-control-plane. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Current lean and what would change it
No lean yet. In regulated organisations, evidence so far points to every-change review as the floor (Stripe), with upstream classification as the lever that could shrink review effort. That would change if policy owners accept risk-scored auto-approval.

Row moved from governance-control-plane: "Logged human override on an approval-biased AI reviewer" (Track 6). Its attribution is that section's Added by line, under [[governance-control-plane]].

### Sub-choice: review-gate-placement — Where review gates sit on PM-agent output
**Choice:** Is PM-agent output reviewed by a human every time, sloped by risk, checked by automated verifiers, or contained by isolation (sandbox, isolated repo)?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The documented failures (invented quotes, silent omissions, generic output) are review problems; gate design sets trust, cost and speed.
**Related themes:** [[human-review-gates]], [[ai-synthesis-hallucination-risk]], [[risk-sloped-review]], [[evals-autonomy-trust]]   **Related dimensions:** [[governance-control-plane]], [[sequencing]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Human reviews every output | PM checks before use or send | Most PM practice; 85% of PMs validate AI output with own expertise (survey summary) | Simple, trusted | Review cost eats savings; reviewer fatigue | mainstream | [Product Focus survey](../research/sources/productfocus-industry-survey-2026.md) (vendor survey, search summary only) |
| Automated verifiers plus human spot check | Quote/fact checks, evals, source links required | Torres built quote and fact-check evals after invented details in AI interview summaries | Catches known failure modes cheaply | Needs building and maintaining evals | early-adopter | [Torres](../research/sources/echai-torres-ai-interview-analysis.md) (independent practitioner); [Product Talk glossary](../research/sources/producttalk-ai-hallucination-glossary.md) (independent) |
| Isolation / blast-radius limits | Agent may act only in a contained space | Atlassian gave a PM an isolated repo; kept PMs out of Jira production code | Lets non-experts act safely | Doesn't improve content quality | early-adopter | [Atlassian talk](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tamar-yehoshua-roles-expanding.md) (first-party) |
| Risk-sloped autonomy | Review depth set by risk class | Ramp auto-approves low-complexity PRs (engineering, not PM) | Speed where safe | Classification must be right | early-adopter | see [[risk-sloped-review]] sources (first-party) |
| Gate at the accepted artifact, not the action | Humans approve intent, spec, plan and PR; the agent runs unprompted between gates (plan mode, then auto mode) | Playbook; auto-mode companion data (see note below the table) | Human attention where rejection actually happens | Needs a strong test and eval net between gates | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md), [auto-mode](../research/sources/claude-auto-mode-in-production.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Four-tier oversight by output risk | Automated / sampled / reviewed / advisory | Anthropic and Accenture CIO blueprint | A simple shared vocabulary for risk sloping | Tier contents not read (gated guide) | emerging | [pilot-to-production](../research/sources/claude-pilot-to-production-cio-blueprint.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Propose-only agent with approval in the loop | The agent monitors and proposes, and every action waits for an explicit tap from the user. Approval is designed into the loop, not bolted on | Square Managerbot (the second regulated-payments case after Stripe) | Clear accountability in a regulated domain | Approval fatigue; little automation realised | early-adopter | [DoP agent harnesses](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-agent-harnesses-secret-sauce.md) (independent, relaying vendor claims) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Encoded reviewer pre-pass (review-gate-placement) | A craft lead's or senior reviewer's standard, reverse-engineered from examples, becomes an agent that grades drafts before a human sees them; a PM walks a spec through several such agents | LinkedIn trust/growth/research/analyst agents; WHOOP "Executive Editor" GPT; Braintrust taste scorer; Cowork "sub-advisory board" | Front-loads review that used to come from many stacked reviews; reuses institutional knowledge; more feedback cycles for juniors | Months of corpus clean-up; agreement with the real reviewer unmeasured; may homogenise; LinkedIn evidence is a retrospective replay and the owner has left | emerging | [cohen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tomer-cohen-full-stack-builder.md) (first-party); [gridley](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-hilary-gridley-supermanager.md) (first-party); [braintrust](../research/sources/howiai-braintrust-ankur-goyal-evals.md) (vendor claim); [cowork-101](../research/sources/howiai-jj-englert-cowork-101.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Decouple shipping from exposure (post-build PM gate) | Engineers or agents build freely; every feature lands behind an early-adopter tier or on an innovation branch; the PM decides to remove, refine or roll out | Proposed by practitioners; no named company | Keeps build speed; moves the product decision to when real software exists | Bloat and duplicates; maintenance and test-permutation cost; needs ruthless removal | emerging | [cw-eng-outpaces](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineering-outpaces-product.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- Note on the auto-mode figure: the companion data says users approve 97% of permission prompts and reject 39% of plans; read as "the gate belongs at plan level" (the note's interpretation, not Anthropic's). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Automated verifiers plus human spot check*: Code Review never approves ("that's still a human call") ([code-review-ga](../research/sources/claude-code-review-ga.md)); the playbook's REVIEW.md passes against spec.md and plan.md, with approval only through branch protection ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Isolation / blast-radius limits*: after a model upgrade, an incident agent asked another Claude over Slack to push a fix; the lesson was to bound access, not instructions ([securing-sdlc](../research/sources/claude-securing-ai-native-sdlc-at-anthropic.md), [ciso-guide](../research/sources/claude-ciso-guide-agentic-ai.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Human reviews every output** (PM work): AI outbound still needs a human on every email and CRM update — [cw-quarterly-planning](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-quarterly-planning.md) (anecdote); Gusto's payroll agent stops for confirmation before submitting — [kim](../research/sources/howiai-gusto-eddie-kim-cofounder.md) (first-party); Gmail scoped to draft-only — [wolan](../research/sources/howiai-webflow-rachel-wolan-chief-of-staff.md) (first-party); never assign an owner unless someone explicitly agreed — [nika-2](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-product-sense-part-2.md) (anecdote); call owners confirm each AI-extracted insight before it enters the shared base (a gate at the source) — [cw-am-calls](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-am-calls-mcp-server.md) (anecdote); low-downside patterns: DM not channel, draft not send, recommend not decide, append not modify — [raviv-pm-agents](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tal-raviv-pm-agents.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Risk-sloped autonomy**: Anthropic's two-week rule (engineer acts as PM below two engineering weeks; controversial small work still goes to the PM) — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party); bug to release in five minutes with confirmation only for high-risk changes — [acharya](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-anish-acharya-company-loops.md) (anecdote); unattended runs only in hosted sandboxes, security-adjacent work pulled local — [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Automated verifiers plus human spot check**: Claude reviews 100% of Anthropic PRs with a human layer after, except throwaway prototypes — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party); the Claude Code team moved to a second Claude reviewing plus human acceptance testing because PRs outgrew human review — [krieger](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-mike-krieger-anthropic-cpo.md) (first-party); multi-agent review became good enough to gate merges only with Opus 4.5/4.6 and Sonnet 4.6, and a release PR that leaked source had passed two layers of human review — [cat-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-cat-wu-anthropic-product-team.md) (first-party); at 8x throughput teams define bad/sad events per surface and monitor rather than review everything — [fung](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-fiona-fung-after-coding-solved.md) (first-party); Mozilla's verifier sub-agent catches agents cheating and cut false positives to almost none — [mozilla](../research/sources/howiai-mozilla-mythos-bug-harness.md) (first-party); a mandatory self-audit pass (quotes, contradictions, thin evidence) before synthesis reaches a deck — [ai-analysis](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-analysis-you-can-trust.md) (anecdote); a PM-authored YAML rubric with a fresh subagent grader per check, two or three loops, then a final human look — [pm-leverage-ladders](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-pm-leverage-ladders.md) (anecdote); cross-vendor review (Codex reviews Claude's branch against plan and mockups; only one agent may push) — [arnovitz](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-zevi-arnovitz-cursor-pm.md), [cw-ai-velocity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-velocity-strategy.md), [cj-hess](../research/sources/howiai-tenex-cj-hess-flowy.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Isolation / blast-radius limits**: Notion's shared prototype repo merges with no code review, acceptable because prototypes don't ship — [notion-playground](../research/sources/howiai-notion-brian-lovin-prototype-playground.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Knowledge-work output can't be verified like code; the agent must expose process, inputs and citations for the human to trust it — [seshan](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tara-seshan-persistent-coworkers.md) (first-party). Weakens automated verifiers for PM artifacts. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Per-action approval is expected to fade; containment by permissions is the durable control — [schulhoff](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sander-schulhoff-ai-security.md) (independent); "click OK five times a minute" fails — [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Review hollowing and oversight load: "I just follow Claude" among surveyed workers — [tech-workers-2026](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tech-workers-2026-survey.md) (anecdote within survey); the mental tax stays with the delegator and grows with agent count — [graham](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-molly-graham-delegating-to-ai.md) (anecdote); verification is the ceiling, one engineer caps parallel agents at three — [cw-pms-coding-tools](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-pms-ai-coding-tools.md) (anecdote); PR, design review and QA became the bottleneck in a product-builder pilot — [cw-product-engineers](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-product-engineers-pilot.md) (anecdote); validation and review named as the current limit, with self-verifying agents as the fix — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Review UI: approvals, a summarising inbox, logs and quick rollback when human and agent share an artifact — [shipper-paradox](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-ai-paradox.md) (anecdote); rendered preview before diff — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- See also (contribution merge 2026-10-24): *Decouple shipping from exposure (post-build PM gate)* is the review-side counterpart of *PR as the PRD* in [intent-shaping-and-spec](#intent-shaping-and-spec-intent-shaping-mode-and-spec-standard) (intent-shaping-mode). Both move the product decision to after working software exists.

#### Key questions to choose
1. For each candidate PM job, what is the cost of an error (wrong customer claim, wrong status, wrong spec) and who bears it? — *answered by:* PM and stakeholder interviews — *affects:* all
2. Can source-link / quote verification be automated for the first job, and at what false-negative rate? — *answered by:* prototype with a seeded-error test set — *affects:* verifiers
3. What review time per output do PMs consider acceptable before the agent is not worth it? — *answered by:* trial — *affects:* human-every-time vs sloped
4. What approval rate per proposal can a PM sustain before propose-only becomes rubber-stamping? — *answered by:* a pilot that logs approvals, time to approve and edits — *affects:* propose-only, risk-scored routing (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
5. For PM artifacts with no executable test, what does the reviewer check (the output, the trace of inputs and reasoning, or both), what does showing the trace cost in reviewer time, and does an outcome-first review surface (rendered preview before diff) reduce it? — *answered by:* reviewer studies on agent-produced PM artifacts with and without traces; prototype review UIs — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, pm-agent-evaluation, agent-interface, trust-privacy-defaults. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
6. Can a PM-written rubric plus subagent grader replace per-output human review for recurring jobs (changelogs, status, synthesis), and what false-pass rate is acceptable? — *answered by:* a pilot comparing rubric-graded outputs against human ratings on a held-out set — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, pm-agent-evaluation, sequencing. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
7. Do encoded reviewer or persona critique agents change the spec before build, or only find what a later review would have found? — *answered by:* a pilot that runs them on live specs and logs which findings changed scope — *affects:* encoded reviewer pre-pass vs PM-led with a shaping skill. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- See also cross-cutting key question 12 (oversight capacity) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Sub-choice: autonomy-calibration — Who sets how much autonomy an agent gets, and how it changes
**Choice:** Is autonomy fixed per task class by policy, set by each user on a slider, earned over time from measured accuracy, or decided by a classifier?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Autonomy determines review effort and risk. Evidence shows it drifts with user experience, so a static table by task class would be wrong for some users.
**Related themes:** [[evals-autonomy-trust]], [[human-review-gates]], [[risk-sloped-review]]   **Related dimensions:** [[governance-control-plane]], [[review-and-autonomy]], [[activity-classification]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed by policy per task class | The org sets autonomy per job (for example suggest-only for policy checks) | Approval-biased review with critical blocking | Predictable; auditable | Ignores user skill; can create friction | mainstream | [Cloudflare](../research/sources/cloudflare-ai-code-review-opencode.md) (first-party) |
| User-set slider | Users choose how much to approve or review, per session | Claude Code permission modes; Karpathy's autonomy-slider framing | Fits different tasks and tolerances | Users may over- or under-trust; needs good visibility | mainstream | [Measuring agent autonomy](../research/sources/anthropic-measuring-agent-autonomy.md) (first-party); [Karpathy notes](../research/sources/latent-space-karpathy-software-3-notes.md) (anecdote, secondary) |
| Earned from measured accuracy | Start as suggestions, raise autonomy as labelled accuracy proves out | Ramp Policy Agent | Trust grounded in data | Needs labelling effort | early-adopter | [Ramp lessons](../research/sources/pragmatic-engineer-ramp-lessons-new-ai-product.md) (first-party) |
| Classifier-gated | A separate model reviews actions and blocks risky ones | Claude Code auto mode | Fewer prompts, still a check | Classifier errors; vendor-specific | emerging | [Claude Code best practices](../research/sources/anthropic-claude-code-best-practices.md) (first-party) |
| Autonomy slider + ground-truth evals | Suggestions first, user-set auto-approve thresholds, weekly labeling, evals from five examples | Ramp Policy Agent | Trust built over time | Needs labeling effort | early-adopter | [Ramp lessons](../research/sources/pragmatic-engineer-ramp-lessons-new-ai-product.md) (first-party) |
| Autonomy tiered by environment | Free in dev, prepared-but-human-authorised in production; deploy, status and rollback exposed as MCP tools scoped per environment; "act up to the production gate and cannot pass it" | Playbook Stage 5 | A clear, auditable line | The production gate stays human-speed | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Autonomy set per channel or container | The same agent gets a different review posture by container (e.g. ship without PR review in a low-risk internal-tools channel, information-gathering only in product channels) | Tennr (Claude Tag) | Risk sloping without per-action config | A low-risk channel still let a comp-display bug reach candidates | early-adopter | [tag-healthcare](../research/sources/claude-tag-healthcare-human-agent-teams.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Control-band tiers for unattended runs | Deterministic detection; 1σ log, 2σ read-only diagnosis, 3σ act only via a PR or a pre-approved runbook; tiers in versioned config | Playbook Stage 6 | Unattended agents with a bounded action set | Only for metrics with a stable baseline | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Reversibility split enforced by the harness | Actions that are easy to undo (drafts, proposals, summaries) run freely. Hard-to-undo actions (payments, publishing, financial records) sit in a hard-coded approval layer the model cannot bypass | DoP harness principle 2; Square Managerbot | A rule PMs and risk owners can both reason about. Fits the "deterministic gates" row | Reversibility is not binary, and someone must classify every tool | emerging | [DoP agent harnesses](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-agent-harnesses-secret-sauce.md) (independent, relaying vendor claims) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Autonomy set by a spec complexity tag | The spec-writing agent tags each item's size and type. Small code fixes go to an autonomous coding agent, the rest become tickets for people, and the threshold rises "as you build trust" | Amplitude Wave (closed beta); DoP Claude Code template | Autonomy decided per item, and auditable | The tag can be wrong. Amplitude's own ship rate is ~11% | emerging | [DoP self-improving product](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-self-improving-product.md) (independent; Amplitude figures are vendor claims) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| "Describe done" goal runs | The user states the end state, how to verify it and what must be preserved, and the agent runs many turns until that condition holds. The guidance excludes strategic or costly-to-get-wrong work | Claude Code `/goal`, Codex `/goal` | Delegation without step prompts; a "done" that can be checked | Wrong tool for judgement work; long runs spend tokens | emerging | [DoP slash commands](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-loops-goals-slash-commands.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Chase rules in code, earned per channel (evidence row for earned autonomy) | The agent reminds only when nothing is moving, names the actual blocker, caps frequency; autonomy to post and chase is earned per channel from a weekly labelled sample | Microsoft's Nudge: 60% faster overdue-PR resolution in a randomised trial, 73% positive | The one independent-quality result on agent-originated requests | Untargeted volume gets bots switched off (11.3% abandoned Dependabot) | early-adopter | [Nudge](../research/sources/arxiv-maddila-nudge-overdue-pull-requests.md) (first-party, peer-reviewed); [He et al.](../research/sources/arxiv-he-dependabot-exploratory-study.md), [Wessel et al.](../research/sources/arxiv-wessel-dont-disturb-me-bots.md) (independent) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Earned from measured accuracy*: ABC Legal agents start as recommenders and switch to automation mode once they match or beat humans ([abc-legal](../research/sources/claude-abc-legal-managed-agents-builders.md)); autonomy granted per task type "in proportion to demonstrated reliability" ([human-agent-teams](../research/sources/claude-building-effective-human-agent-teams.md), first-party); a Twilio PM went from six approval gates to headless by day 45 ([twilio](../research/sources/claude-customers-twilio-pm-platform.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Classifier-gated*: auto mode in production at Nuro, Gusto and Garner, each keeping manual approval for one risky class; users approve 97% of permission prompts but reject 39% of plans ([auto-mode](../research/sources/claude-auto-mode-in-production.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Earned from measured accuracy**: the CC/CD ladder ships each job in versions by agency (route, then draft for a human, then resolve), logs every human handoff as eval data and climbs when calibration stops producing surprises; 4-6 months per critical workflow — [reganti-badam](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-reganti-badam-ai-deployments.md), [cc-cd](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-cc-cd-ai-lifecycle.md) (anecdote); CASH tracks weekly whether human time per stage falls; a metrics agent earns trust as false positives and negatives fall — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party); OpenAI support answers when confident, otherwise drafts, and the correction becomes training data — [weil](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-kevin-weil-openai-cpo.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **User-set slider**: Cowork per-connector allow/block/ask and draft-only instructions ("progressive trust") — [cowork-101](../research/sources/howiai-jj-englert-cowork-101.md) (anecdote); OpenClaw users start with read-only tokens — [openclaw-guide](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-claire-vo-openclaw-guide.md) (anecdote); a physical approve button shipped as a developer feature in Claude Desktop — [rieseberg](../research/sources/howiai-felix-rieseberg-cowork.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. Do our target users' autonomy preferences change with experience as the Anthropic data suggests, and should the product track that? — *answered by:* usage telemetry from a pilot; interviews — *affects:* user-set slider vs fixed policy
2. For which jobs would a policy owner refuse user-set autonomy? — *answered by:* risk/compliance owners — *affects:* fixed policy vs slider
3. What labelled data exists to earn autonomy for a job such as PRD review? — *answered by:* SME labelling exercise — *affects:* earned autonomy
4. Which action classes should a regulated product agent never auto-approve (outbound messages, writes to systems of record, production)? Nuro, Gusto and Garner each picked one class. — *answered by:* compliance owners and the project owner — *affects:* Classifier-gated, Autonomy tiered by environment. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
5. What agreement threshold and sample size moves an agent from recommender to automation mode, and who re-reviews when a model upgrade unlocks new behaviour inside existing permissions? — *answered by:* ABC Legal's CTO; security plus harness owner — *affects:* Earned from measured accuracy. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
6. For each tool a PM agent can call, is the action reversible, and who classifies it? — *answered by:* a tool inventory reviewed with policy owners — *affects:* reversibility split, propose-only, deterministic gates (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
7. Do we have permissions to let agents read the results of their own writes? (Companies reportedly grant write more readily than read, which stops agents checking their output.) — *answered by:* a permissions review of target systems (Slack, tracker, CRM) — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, agent-identity-delegation, governance-control-plane. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
8. What share of asks does the product owner edit before sending in the first month, and does it fall? — *answered by:* the MVP's labelled sample — *affects:* when posting becomes earned (chase rules in code, earned per channel) (from research-dependency-agent)

Row moved from governance-control-plane: "Autonomy slider + ground-truth evals" (Ramp Policy Agent, Glass pass). Its attribution is that section's Added by line, under [[governance-control-plane]].
