#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed by policy per task class (evidence row) | Project owner marks sensitive tools as needing human confirmation for that project only | Stripe Kai projects | Friction only where risk is | Owner judgment; not adaptive | early-adopter | [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |
| Human reviews every output (evidence row, knowledge work) | Agent output is supplementary research; the human answers every sub-question | Stripe compliance agents | Accountability kept; 26% faster | Caps the gain | early-adopter | [AWS/Stripe compliance post](../research/sources/aws-stripe-compliance-agents.md) (first-party, vendor-hosted) |



River/Aquifer additions (competitive-teardown-shopify-river, 2026-10-24):

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Agent drives the pipeline, hands off one small decision | Agent validates live state, repairs, and routes "the smallest question that an owner has to answer" with evidence; owners approve and merge; guarantees enforced in code, judgement in prompts | Shopify River security remediation | Owners stop shuttling state; trust kept by explicit stopping points | Prompts alone failed to enforce complete enumeration (observed) | early-adopter | [vuln remediation](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-river-vulnerability-remediation.md) (first-party) |

## intent-shaping-and-spec: Intent shaping mode and spec standard
**Choice:** Who shapes intent (PM-led bespoke; PM-led with a versioned shaping skill; agent-led interrogation and research; prototype-first; agent-originated) and what counts as agent-ready intent (narrative PRD; short evidence-tied spec with a completeness standard; prototype as spec; canonical spec plus risk mitigations; spec pushed straight to coding agents).
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Refinement is the stated bottleneck of the intent factory and the core job a "product agent" would take on; the spec standard is the contract between the product agent and the coding factory.
**Related themes:** [[agent-led-intent-shaping]], [[agent-ready-intent]], [[intent-factory-before-discovery]], [[prd-agents]]   **Related dimensions:** [[work-structure]], [[review-and-autonomy]], [[sequencing]], [[artifact-and-project-structure]]
Formerly: intent-shaping-mode, spec-standard (grouped as two sub-choices, per 01-synthesis.md D.19)

### Sub-choice: intent-shaping-mode — Who shapes intent, and how
**Choice:** Is rough intent turned into delivery-ready requirements ad hoc by a PM, by a PM following a standard question set, by an agent-led interrogation and research skill, or by building a prototype first?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** Refinement is the stated bottleneck of the intent factory. This is the core job a "product agent" would take on.
**Related themes:** [[agent-led-intent-shaping]], [[intent-factory-before-discovery]], [[agent-factory-sdlc]]   **Related dimensions:** [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[work-structure]], [[review-and-autonomy]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM-led, bespoke | Each PM shapes intent their own way, just in time | Most teams | Flexibility | Inconsistent; slow; refinement 1–2 sprints ahead at most | mainstream | [Intent Factory §Current State](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party observation) |
| PM-led with a versioned shaping skill | A standard question set, a named source per question, answers written back, revised on a cadence | Geoff's Action 4 | Consistency; compounds; a low-tech start | Needs upkeep and adoption; the PM still does most of the work | emerging | [Intent Factory Action 4](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party) |
| Agent-led interrogation and research | The agent runs parallel research (code, wiki, competitors, calls), asks set questions, and challenges requirements that lack evidence | Ramp's shaping skill in Glass; Stripe Kai for research | Speed; evidence-tied requirements | Depends on context access; risk of shallow answers | early-adopter | [Intent Factory §Vision 2–3](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed); [Charles on Lenny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (vendor claim) |
| Prototype-first | The PM or designer builds a working prototype in place of a written spec | Stripe Harbor (12,000 prototypes reported) | Concrete, testable intent quickly | Hidden requirements (risk, edge cases) go unstated | early-adopter | [Intent Factory §Vision 4](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed) |
| Agent-originated intent | An agent mines tickets, calls and surveys and proposes the work | Ramp (90 days of tickets in ~8 minutes) | Signal-driven backlog | Noise; needs human prioritisation | emerging | [Intent Factory §Vision 1](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed) |
| Product owner reviews, agent writes the spec | The accepted intent goes to a spec pass under org skills (brand, security, compliance, UX) that flags contradictory policies; the PO reviews and routes flags to named policy owners | Playbook Stage 2 | Moves the PO from author to reviewer; policy applied while writing, not weeks later | Spec quality bounded by the skills' fidelity to the policy; a skill is advisory, not enforced | emerging | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Validate before build, with scope-amendment criteria | A scope doc states the user evidence needed before a feature is added, checked at each coding session; adversarial pressure-testing before any prototype | Anthropic founders' playbook | Guards against "a prototype existing is not validation" and against scope creep from near-free building | Depends on discipline unless an agent enforces it | emerging | [founders-playbook](../research/sources/claude-founders-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Diverge-first ideation agent | The agent researches named products and proposes N directions. The PM picks one, the agent drafts a PRD for editing, then it generates screens and a deck | Google Stitch Ideate; the Stitch MCP chained in Claude Code | Breadth before convergence; fast material for stakeholders | Output is for discussion only; beta | emerging | [DoP Stitch Ideate](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-google-stitch-ideate-agent.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Framework-scripted sparring partner | The agent recommends three frameworks (Kano, pre-mortem, JTBD...) and walks the PM through the chosen one step by step: "cognitive expansion, not offloading" | DoP Strategy Thought Partner | Keeps the PM's own reasoning in the loop; a repeatable shape | Frameworks can become theatre; risk of sycophancy | prototype | [DoP strategy thought partner](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-strategy-thought-partner.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Synthetic persona panel as a check before research | The feature is put to several AI personas built from research data, optionally on a voice call, before real users see it | DoP Persona Feature Tester | A fast check of assumptions from several viewpoints | The demo's "grounding" data was itself AI-generated. NN/g documents the optimism of synthetic users | emerging | [DoP persona tester](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-persona-feature-tester.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| PR as the PRD (intent-shaping-mode) | A small team builds each candidate feature as a review-ready PR on a hidden production page, discusses it live, merges or deletes; no specs, tickets or design files | Gusto Cofounder (5 people, 10 weeks; a whiteboard photo the only document) | Scope decided on working software; very fast zero-to-one | Needs co-founder-level permission; zero-to-one only by the CTO's account; regulated change control untested | early-adopter | [kim](../research/sources/howiai-gusto-eddie-kim-cofounder.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Shaped pitch within an appetite (intent-shaping-mode) | Before build time is committed, PM, designer and senior engineer narrow the problem, set a time budget and breadboard the solution to under 10 moving parts, opening the code to surface hidden branches; done when an engineer says they know what to build | 37signals Shape Up; a few teams in a large pharmaceutical company | A concrete completeness test for intent; rabbit holes found before green-light; PM moves upstream | Needs a senior engineer in every session; pre-AI, no agent version found; "not enough detail" the common failure | mainstream as a human method | [singer](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-ryan-singer-shape-up.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Agent-led interrogation and research*: the playbook has Claude ask "the questions an analyst would ask" while the originator writes intent.md ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md), first-party); the Fable field guide's "blind spot pass" and "interview me one question at a time" ([fable-finding-unknowns](../research/sources/claude-fable-finding-unknowns.md), first-party); the founders' playbook makes Claude argue against the hypothesis and audit interview questions for leading wording ([founders-playbook](../research/sources/claude-founders-playbook.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Agent-originated intent*: playbook Stage 6 has a monitoring agent write intent.md on a control-band breach, and Claude Security findings too large for one PR become intent.md ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Prototype-first*: the Claude Code team plans with prototypes and internal users, not design docs ([pm-on-the-ai-exponential](../research/sources/claude-pm-on-the-ai-exponential.md), [running-ai-native-engineering-org](../research/sources/claude-running-ai-native-engineering-org.md), first-party); the designer behind Claude Design generates many options, then hands off to Claude Code ([design-designer-workflow](../research/sources/claude-design-designer-workflow.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *PM-led with a versioned shaping skill*: the intent.md template is encoded as a skill, set up by a technical member and signed off by a lead ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Closed sense-decide-act-learn loop (evidence for "agent-originated intent")** (filed by the contribution as evidence for *Agent-originated intent*; merged here rather than added as a row): Agents read analytics, support and errors against a map of product areas. They judge and spec opportunities (checked against the codebase, tagged for complexity), route them, check the PR against the acceptance criteria and write memory for each area Examples: Amplitude Wave; DoP Claude Code template. Gives you: A signal-driven backlog that learns from rejections. Costs / risks: ~11% shipped against ~50% would do. Chasing metrics can harm coherence ("AI optimized product slop"). Maturity: emerging. Evidence: [DoP self-improving product](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-self-improving-product.md) (independent; vendor numbers) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
- **Prototype-first** / **Prototype as spec** (supporting): 60-80% of Anthropic growth work ships without a PRD; small work starts in Slack, large work in a 30-minute cross-functional kickoff — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party); "mocks not docs" — [seshan](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tara-seshan-persistent-coworkers.md) (first-party); prototypes over decks, a PRD's claims about AI behaviour are just words — [liu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-howie-liu-airtable-reorg.md) (first-party); direction-setting is often a prototype that points people the right way — [jenny-wen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-jenny-wen-design-process-dead.md) (first-party); the first 10% of a project is now free, so no PRD — [schoening](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-max-schoening-agency.md) (first-party); Figma Slides ran without PRDs on annotated design files plus one vision deck — [figma-slides](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-figma-slides-ship-like-startup.md) (first-party); Microsoft's CPO requires prototypes and prompt sets — [chennapragada](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-aparna-chennapragada-microsoft.md) (first-party); no meeting with Webflow's CPO without a prototype — [wolan](../research/sources/howiai-webflow-rachel-wolan-chief-of-staff.md) (first-party); AI chat experiences can't be designed in Figma, so Notion prototypes against live models — [notion-playground](../research/sources/howiai-notion-brian-lovin-prototype-playground.md) (first-party); a showcase app of about 100 screens in 775 states as the spec — [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (anecdote); a design director hands developers a Cursor prototype showing interactive intent — [unger](../research/sources/howiai-atlassian-joel-unger-cursor-design.md) (first-party, show notes); Gumroad's CEO iterates in v0 then pastes the final prompt into Devin — [lavingia](../research/sources/howiai-gumroad-sahil-lavingia-v0-devin.md) (first-party); practitioners' prototypes carry about 80% with the PRD a footnote — [cw-prototypes-release](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prototypes-release-comms.md) (anecdote); prototype plus light PRD — [acolyte](../research/sources/howiai-acolyte-health-meeting-notes-prototypes.md) (anecdote); PRDs shrink to problem-only, then v0 screens the team debates — [cw-team-velocity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-team-velocity.md) (anecdote); idea, prototype, PRD if needed, Figma, Jira — [cw-dev-cycles](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-product-dev-cycles.md) (anecdote); a 25-year PM converting PRD writing into prototype writing — [udezue](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-udezue-ai-reshaping-pm-role.md) (anecdote); regulated variant: a fast prototype loop separated from the release train by a version pin — [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Agent-led interrogation and research**: an agent asks 17 grouped questions, the human answers by voice on a board, the agent assembles the PRD — [lennyrpg](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-how-i-built-lennyrpg.md) (anecdote); Codex "plan.md first" for long runs — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim); requirements are externalised by reacting to model output — [husain](../research/sources/howiai-hamel-husain-error-analysis.md) (anecdote citing a paper); CASH has Claude find growth opportunities, build, test and analyse at a junior-PM win rate — [avasare](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-amol-avasare-anthropic-growth.md) (first-party, no numbers). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **PM-led with a versioned shaping skill**: Every keeps a PRD-generating prompt as slash commands in a shared GitHub repo — [shipper-every](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-every-ai-native.md) (first-party); a team-agreed PRD template as a gate plus a devil's-advocate pass — [acolyte](../research/sources/howiai-acolyte-health-meeting-notes-prototypes.md) (anecdote); a non-technical PM's slash-command chain (issue, clarifying questions, plan file, execute, cross-model review), which he would not use inside a large company — [arnovitz](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-zevi-arnovitz-cursor-pm.md) (anecdote); a PM copilot that emits a developer-ready spec assuming no prior context — [raviv-copilot](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tal-raviv-ai-copilot.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Agent-originated intent**: Claude reads Anthropic's internal feedback channel, bugs and telemetry and proposes fixes as PRs — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party); Codex's lead favours "chatter-driven development" over specs — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim); risk: engineers fed call transcripts straight into implementation and half the shipped suggestions were judged bloat — [cw-eng-outpaces](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineering-outpaces-product.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- The bottleneck has moved upstream: with about 90% AI-written code the limit is decision making and alignment; Anthropic's CPO still seeks a "minimum viable strategy" — [krieger](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-mike-krieger-anthropic-cpo.md) (first-party); spec-to-implementation from three weeks to three hours — [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent); "I'm the bottleneck, not the engineer" — [rauch](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-guillermo-rauch-v0.md) (vendor claim); building got cheap but confusion didn't; slipped estimates trace to weak specs — [cw-ai-velocity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-velocity-strategy.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Prototype abundance: thousands of prototypes not aimed at an agreed problem — [stone](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-elizabeth-stone-netflix-systems-thinkers.md) (first-party), [cw-am-calls](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-am-calls-mcp-server.md) (anecdote); coding tools are linear and bias to one direction, so divergent exploration still happens on a canvas first — [jenny-wen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-jenny-wen-design-process-dead.md) (first-party); a champion of three prototypes per feature has no confident way to choose among them and rejects simulated users — [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- See also (contribution merge 2026-10-24): *PR as the PRD* pairs with *Decouple shipping from exposure (post-build PM gate)* in [review-and-autonomy](#review-and-autonomy-review-and-autonomy) (review-gate-placement): build first, then decide what ships.

#### Key questions to choose
1. What are the universal questions every piece of intent must answer here (including risk questions), and where is the source for each answer? — *answered by:* a workshop with PMs plus a review of past specs — *affects:* shaping skill, agent-led
2. Which of those answers can an agent find with today's context access, and which stay with a person? — *answered by:* a prototype shaping skill run on 3–5 live initiatives — *affects:* agent-led
3. How far ahead of delivery is shovel-ready intent today, and does a shaping skill extend that? — *answered by:* backlog analysis before and after a pilot — *affects:* all
4. Is a prototype acceptable as the spec of record in a regulated setting? — *answered by:* risk and compliance policy owners — *affects:* prototype-first
5. Anthropic's own trends report collapses requirements and design into "express intent (minutes)" ([trends](../research/sources/claude-2026-agentic-coding-trends.md)), and the playbook folds problem and outcome into intent.md. Who does the shaping between an idea and an accepted intent.md, and is that the product agent's job? — *answered by:* the strategy plus a PM prototype — *affects:* all options. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
6. Does prototype-first, light-documentation planning survive a regulated org's need for a decision record, or does the committed artifact chain become mandatory? — *answered by:* risk/compliance owners plus a PM trial — *affects:* Prototype-first, Committed artifact chain. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
7. Do synthetic persona verdicts agree with what real users later said, for our own features? — *answered by:* a back-test on past features whose reception is known — *affects:* persona panel (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
8. Extending existing Q4 beyond prototypes: when the handoff is an eval set, a PR or a prototype rather than a document, who outside the team (risk, compliance, design) can read and sign it off, does it carry the why a regulated reviewer needs, and can a leader grant "no spec" permission at all or only a lighter approved artifact? — *answered by:* a spec-as-evals handoff with one risk reviewer on one feature; control-owner and sponsor interviews — *affects:* eval set as spec, PR as the PRD, prototype-first, adoption-model. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
9. When prototypes are nearly free, what decides which of several variants proceeds, who decides and how fast, where are decisions logged, and can any of it be delegated (synthetic vs human usability signals)? — *answered by:* observing teams that prototype in parallel; a time study of decision latency; whether teams that gate prototyping on a framed problem ship fewer reworked features — *affects:* prototype-first, all shaping modes, sequencing, pm-agent-evaluation. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
10. When building is nearly free, does shaping happen before the build (pitch, spec) or after it (build freely, decouple shipping from exposure, PM decides what to keep)? — *answered by:* trials comparing pre-build shaping with post-build triage on rework, bloat and maintenance cost — *affects:* intent-shaping-and-spec, review-and-autonomy, target-outcome. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)


### Sub-choice: spec-standard — What counts as agent-ready intent
**Choice:** What artifact hands intent to delivery agents: a narrative PRD or deck, a short evidence-tied spec held to a completeness standard with evals, a prototype, or a canonical spec that also carries risk mitigations?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** No delivery approach reaches its automation potential on vague intent. The standard is the contract between the product agent and the coding factory.
**Related themes:** [[agent-ready-intent]], [[prd-agents]]   **Related dimensions:** [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[review-and-autonomy]]

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Narrative PRD or deck (AI-drafted or not) | A high-level document; the PM answers questions during development | Most teams; ChatPRD-style drafting | Familiar; quick to draft | Not executable by agents; refinement deferred | mainstream | [Intent Factory §Current State](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party observation); [ChatPRD guide](../research/sources/aakashg-chatprd-guide.md) (vendor claim) |
| Short evidence-tied spec with a completeness standard | Numbered requirements, "what not how", evidence links; templates, examples, and evals that gate delivery | Ramp's seven-part spec; Geoff's Action 3 | Agent-executable; measurable as a KR | Upfront work to define and maintain the standard | early-adopter | [Intent Factory Action 3](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (first-party; Ramp example a vendor claim) |
| Prototype as spec | A working prototype is the handoff | Stripe Harbor; Ramp's "data + requirements + prototype" | Unambiguous behaviour | Non-functional and risk requirements implicit | early-adopter | [Intent Factory §Vision 4](../research/sources/geoff-building-accelerating-intent-factory.md) (vendor claim, relayed); [Charles on Lenny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-geoff-charles-product-when-coding-solved.md) (vendor claim) |
| Canonical spec plus risk mitigations | A spec that absorbs mitigations as requirements and is re-assessed for residual risk | Geoff's risk-agent concept | Risk traceable to requirements | Heavier artifact; concept only | emerging (concept) | [Intent Shaping in a Well-Managed Environment](../research/sources/geoff-intent-shaping-well-managed-environment.md) (first-party) |
| Spec pushed straight to coding agents | A PM tool writes the spec and hands it to coding agents via MCP | Productboard Spark | Closes the define-to-build gap inside a vendor tool | Vendor lock-in; launch claims only | emerging | [Productboard Spark](../research/sources/productboard-introducing-spark.md) (vendor claim) |
| Committed artifact chain (intent.md → spec.md → plan.md) | Each stage writes one markdown file to version control; the merge of one fires the next stage; the commit chain is the audit trail | Anthropic AI-native SDLC playbook | One record of who asked, what was decided, who approved; machine-readable by the next agent; works for non-engineers via a git connector | Assumes the repo (or a linked tool) is the system of record; the playbook reports no outcomes; three files per change may be heavy for small work | emerging (prescriptive) | [playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Spec as a verified executable contract | The agent emits specs (behaviour, data, authorization), not code; a deterministic kernel verifies and runs them | Datadog Temper | What is verified is what runs; human line review is replaced by verification | Only shown for control-logic software; heavy platform build | emerging | [datadog](../research/sources/claude-datadog-universal-machine-tool.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Comprehension gate before merge | The approver must pass a Claude-written quiz on the change, or reads an explainer package, before merging | Fable field guide | Review proves understanding instead of skimming diffs | Untested at team scale; adds friction | emerging | [fable-finding-unknowns](../research/sources/claude-fable-finding-unknowns.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Two-layer spec artifact (machine tokens plus rationale prose) | Exact values in front matter and judgement guidance in prose, checked by a CLI linter | Google DESIGN.md (open-sourced) | Precise where it can be, guided where it can't; lintable | Agents fill gaps silently with plausible values, and finished-looking output suppresses critique | early-adopter | [DoP DESIGN.md](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-design-md-explained.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Eval set or golden examples as the spec (spec-standard) | The PM's main artifact is 30-40 representative prompts with golden answers, or example end-user interactions written before any PRD; rerun on every model or harness change; a short PRD kept where cross-functional alignment or an ambiguous bet needs it | Anthropic research PM team ("evals are the new PRDs"); Claude Code's handful of evals with run instructions; Yelp "golden conversations"; Braintrust scorers; Microsoft "prompt sets" | Success defined and re-checkable; one artifact serves as spec, eval seed and prototype input; shortens pain-to-fix | Fits gradeable, mostly AI-feature output; covers neither non-functional, risk nor dependency requirements; criteria drift means revision after launch; transfer to ordinary feature work unproven | emerging | [penn](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dianne-penn-evals-prds.md) (first-party); [cat-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-cat-wu-anthropic-product-team.md) (first-party); [yelp](../research/sources/howiai-yelp-priya-badger-golden-conversations.md) (first-party); [braintrust](../research/sources/howiai-braintrust-ankur-goyal-evals.md) (vendor claim); [chennapragada](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-aparna-chennapragada-microsoft.md) (first-party); [advanced-evals](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-advanced-evals-error-discovery.md), [hamel-shreya-podcast](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-hamel-shreya-evals.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Spec or goal with an executable verification contract (spec-standard) | The handoff carries outcome, constraints that can't regress, allowed tools and files, and a "how to verify" section the agent must run (tests, a CLI that drives the product, screenshots); the agent loops until it has evidence; the spec lives in the repo and later changes go to the spec, not the code | Notion `agent specs` folder plus Notion AI CLI; OpenAI Codex /goal; Anthropic's checked-in statement of what good looks like | Multi-hour unattended runs with a checkable finish line; automated review has something to test against; spec history as changelog | Only where verification is executable; verification tooling must exist first; engineer-authored today; token cost (one 4-hour run about 6M tokens); spec drift if people edit code | early-adopter (coding) | [nystrom](../research/sources/howiai-notion-ryan-nystrom-spec-driven.md) (first-party); [fung](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-fiona-fung-after-coding-solved.md) (first-party); [codex-goal](../research/sources/howiai-claire-vo-codex-goal.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Typed intent model as canonical spec (spec-standard) | The PRD is a version-controlled structured file (entities, state machines, mutation contracts, acceptance rules); prose is generated on demand; undecided items live in an `open_questions[]` field so work proceeds on degraded states | One community member's white-label mail-client team | Agents traverse and enforce it; less drift than prose; delivery need not wait for every decision | Non-technical stakeholders don't read JSON; who keeps it canonical; one anecdote | emerging | [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Data schema or sample data as the spec (spec-standard) | Intent expressed as a realistic structured data set from the real schema, which a prototyping or coding agent builds the UI around; swap data sets to test segments and edge cases | Ravi Mehta's data-driven prototyping; pasting engineering's schema into PRDs and prototyping tools | Removes ambiguity prose leaves; the agent "doesn't get fuzzy" with data | Needs the schema; covers data and UI, not behaviour, constraints or risk; demoed on consumer UI | emerging | [mehta](../research/sources/howiai-ravi-mehta-data-driven-prototyping.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Human-owned canonical intent doc, agent-maintained derived docs (spec-standard) | A product-intent doc in the repo that humans maintain and agents may not edit; architecture and customer docs regenerated by agents on triggers and reviewed | A community practitioner; code-as-truth counterview from a hiring-software PM | A reference to test whether code does what it should; drift visible | Human upkeep; repo docs easy to manipulate; some find code-derived intent enough | emerging | [cw-architecture-docs](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-architecture-docs-intent.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Layered requirements files with a checker pass, compiled to tasks (spec-standard) | A brain-dump becomes a requirements/acceptance file the human edits in full; a separate checker agent with its own context flags dependencies and open questions; master plan, design guidelines and journeys compile to a task file read before every prompt | An engineer-founder and a healthcare product director with 13 role agents; Lovable's professional vibe coder | Makes "constraints resolved, dependencies mapped" an explicit step; acceptance criteria become test input; low-touch execution | Solo-builder evidence only; about a day of planning per project; the human reading everything is the bottleneck by design | emerging | [cw-pms-coding-tools](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-pms-ai-coding-tools.md) (anecdote); [jovanovic](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-lazar-jovanovic-vibe-coder.md) (anecdote, vendor employee) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Plan as the agent-ready artifact*: plan mode produces plan.md, which the PR review checks the diff against ([playbook](../research/sources/claude-ai-native-sdlc-playbook.md)); CodeRabbit's planning layer, where "the plan itself becomes a quality gate" and is validated by stakeholders ([coderabbit](../research/sources/claude-coderabbit-plan-orchestration.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Plan as the agent-ready artifact**: a few minutes of human-plus-agent research (DeepWiki) turns a rough ask into a full task prompt, then Devin runs unattended ("tasks, not problems") — [scott-wu-devin](../research/sources/howiai-scott-wu-devin-junior-engineer.md) (vendor claim); in Symphony the human writes a short spec in a Linear ticket and the agent writes a work pad with plan, acceptance criteria and validations — [symphony](../research/sources/howiai-alessio-fanelli-symphony-linear.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Spec pushed straight to coding agents**: Notion's repo `agent specs` (see the verification-contract row); every author shown is an engineer — [nystrom](../research/sources/howiai-notion-ryan-nystrom-spec-driven.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Narrative PRD or deck**: HTML rather than Markdown specs, with throwaway micro-UIs and inline annotations, for better engagement — [shihipar](../research/sources/howiai-thariq-shihipar-html-specs.md) (first-party, show notes); PRDs are not dead: pick the medium for the point and say the artifact's stage aloud — [ambrosino](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-ambrosino-codex-product-work.md), [penn](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dianne-penn-evals-prds.md) (first-party); Claude Code writes PRDs only as one-pagers for ambiguous features or multi-month infra — [cat-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-cat-wu-anthropic-product-team.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Short evidence-tied spec with a completeness standard**: a 1,500-person org's spec-driven SDLC converges on a source-of-truth hierarchy, handoff packets, decision records, acceptance criteria and verification gates — [cw-quarterly-planning](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-quarterly-planning.md) (anecdote); practitioners add a first-pass eval rubric to the PRD and spend more time on specs for agents because the agent uses any wiggle room — [cw-prds-for-agents](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prds-for-agents.md) (anecdote); healthtech and fintech teams keep a linked PRD, architecture, legal and clinical reviews because of interdependencies — [cw-prd-vs-prototype](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prd-vs-prototype.md) (anecdote); best teams prototype synchronously first, then the PM writes specs with AI for engineers — [cw-ai-operating-models](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-operating-models.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Against prototype as spec: a prototype can't carry the why, constraints, edge cases or non-functional requirements — [cw-prototyping-vs-prds](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prototyping-vs-prds.md) (anecdote); people interpret the same prototype differently — [cw-prototypes-release](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-prototypes-release-comms.md) (anecdote); AI prototype code is mostly useless to engineering unless built on real components and doesn't shorten 2-6 week timelines — [colin-matthews-team](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-colin-matthews-team-prototyping.md), [colin-matthews-brand](../research/sources/howiai-colin-matthews-brand-prototypes.md) (anecdote); a design prototype handed to a coding agent reached 60-70% fidelity until every state was explicit and a screenshot check added — [cw-claude-design-handoff](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-claude-design-handoff.md) (anecdote); prototypes called "clickable PRDs, too immature" while engineers treat them as PRDs — [cw-non-pms](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-non-pms-shipping.md) (anecdote); the dominant real-world failure is not enough detail, and high-fidelity artifacts without engineering input blow out — [singer](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-ryan-singer-shape-up.md) (anecdote); keep discovery prototypes flat — [margolis](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-michael-margolis-bullseye.md) (anecdote); prototype-first teams insist review and engineering quality do not get cheaper; the last 10% is 90% of the work — [kim](../research/sources/howiai-gusto-eddie-kim-cofounder.md), [schoening](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-max-schoening-agency.md). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Against spec-first: people can't specify complete requirements up front; criteria emerge from reviewing outputs — [evals-hamel-shreya](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-evals-hamel-shreya.md) (anecdote); Codex's lead doubts spec-driven development wins because people dislike writing specs — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim); Penn's product is the model and her users are researchers, so eval-as-spec transfer is unproven. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

#### Key questions to choose
1. What did the specs behind our highest-automation deliveries have in common, and what did the failures lack? — *answered by:* a retrospective on agent-delivered work — *affects:* all
2. Can product and engineering agree on one completeness standard, and can an eval judge it reliably? — *answered by:* a joint working session, then an eval prototype — *affects:* evidence-tied spec, canonical spec
3. Which spec format does the chosen SDD or coding-agent approach expect? — *answered by:* engineering, and a review of the SDD tooling — *affects:* all
6. What is the intent.md "survival rate" (share accepted into design) and how much spec rework follows the first plan? The playbook proposes both measures but reports neither. — *answered by:* a pilot that logs both from git — *affects:* Committed artifact chain. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
7. What plan granularity works (too fine goes stale, too coarse lets assumptions back in), and what lift did planning give? — *answered by:* CodeRabbit (David Loker) or an internal A/B — *affects:* Plan as the agent-ready artifact. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
8. Could the intent-shaping spec standard take the DESIGN.md shape (machine-checkable fields plus rationale), and what would its linter check? — *answered by:* drafting the standard with product and engineering leads, then linting 10 past specs — *affects:* two-layer artifact, the existing "evidence-tied spec" row (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
9. When the receiver is a coding agent, which handoff form wins on downstream fidelity, review effort and rework: a document, a prototype plus a thin written delta or PR, or an eval set or goal with an executable check; what must the written layer still hold (why, constraints, edge cases, non-functional requirements, a verification section), and who writes it, PM or engineer? — *answered by:* a side-by-side of the forms on the same features; piloting a Notion-style verification section on PM-originated features and counting engineer rewrites — *affects:* all spec-standard options, review-and-autonomy, artifact-and-project-structure, pm-agent-evaluation. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
10. Should every intent artifact carry an explicit stage label (exploration, validated, ready to build), since prototype polish no longer signals that research or review happened? — *answered by:* review of how often polished prototypes are mistaken for decided work in pilot teams — *affects:* intent-shaping-and-spec, artifact-and-project-structure. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
11. If the canonical spec is structured, can open questions be carried as a field with designed degraded states so delivery starts before every decision, which decision classes must close first in a regulated setting, and how do prose readers (risk, legal, executives) review: generated views or a separate prose artifact? — *answered by:* compliance and engineering leads reviewing 3-5 past initiatives; a trial with one reviewer group — *affects:* typed intent model, canonical spec plus risk mitigations, artifact-and-project-structure. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
12. How much spec detail does a coding agent need (is it set by the builder's capability, as Singer says for humans), and how do we keep agent-generated specs short enough for humans to read (12-page PRDs where 3 would do)? — *answered by:* matched tasks handed to agents at different spec depths; spec-length and reader-specific views in pilots — *affects:* intent-shaping-and-spec, work-structure, artifact-and-project-structure. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
13. Should the canonical statement of intent be editable by agents at all? — *answered by:* whether teams that lock intent docs catch more code/intent drift than code-as-truth teams — *affects:* intent-shaping-and-spec, context-and-memory, review-and-autonomy. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
14. Is the dependency and open-questions check a distinct agent job worth separating from drafting, or part of spec review? — *answered by:* comparing defect and rework rates with a separate checker agent vs a single drafting agent — *affects:* intent-shaping-and-spec, work-structure. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)


#### Stripe chain additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
A Stripe engineer's whiteboard (Not My Business video, ~12:24) frames product work as **Opportunity → Problem → Goal → Intent → Plan**. A brace over Problem and Goal is labelled, partly legibly, "Customer conv…". Code follows: the plan is handed to a minion. Orbit (Stripe's planning agent) owns Intent research and Plan, and minions own Code. Opportunity, Problem and Goal stay with people. This separates two things the options above treat as one: **intent shaping** (problem, goal, intent) and **planning** (intent to an agent-executable plan).

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plan as the agent-ready artifact (intent in, reviewed plan out) | The person states intent; a planning agent researches the codebase and internal docs and drafts a step plan; teammates comment on it; one button hands it to a coding agent that may run for hours and pause to ask | Stripe Orbit + Minions | The review gate moves to the plan, before code; the intent can stay informal | Problem and goal quality is assumed, not checked; engineer-first, and PM use isn't stated | emerging (first shown 2026-10-22) | [Not My Business video](../research/sources/youtube-notmybusiness-inside-stripe-ships-fast.md) (first-party, vendor-produced) |
| Agent-drafted spec reviewed as an issue, then delegated | The agent drafts a precise spec (contract, valid and invalid examples); the team reviews it before implementation | Stripe extensibility SDK (~20 lint rules) | Catches design issues before code review | Works for bounded work with clear correctness | early-adopter | [SDK post](../research/sources/stripe-ai-didnt-write-our-sdk.md) (first-party) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
4. Is the gap we build for **above intent** (opportunity, problem, goal, which Stripe leaves to people and customer conversations) or **below it** (intent to plan, which Stripe has automated with Orbit)? Geoff's Intent Factory loop starts at "informal intent", so it covers the lower half. — *answered by:* the project owner, plus a time study of where PM hours and delays sit across the six stages — *affects:* agent-led interrogation, prototype-first, plan-as-artifact
5. Does a reviewed plan (Stripe) do the job of a completeness-standard spec (Ramp, Geoff's Action 3), or do they gate different risks? — *answered by:* compare defect and rework rates of plan-gated vs spec-gated agent work in a pilot — *affects:* short evidence-tied spec, plan as artifact

---

## adoption-model: Adoption and enablement model
**Choice:** How do users become effective: training, mandates and metrics, a champions network, or enablement built into the product?
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The best harness fails if people stay stuck; Ramp's account makes adoption design part of the product.
**Related themes:** [[enablement-by-product]], [[non-engineer-builders]], [[org-learning-loop]]   **Related dimensions:** [[capability-distribution-trust]], [[governance-control-plane]]

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Product-led enablement | Auto-configured on install, skills and nudges teach 'what good looks like' | Ramp Glass | Day-one results without training | Claim that product taught faster than training is anecdotal | early-adopter | [Goddijn](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party) |
| Mandates, leaderboards, proficiency ladder | L0-L3 levels, usage leaderboards, AI in hiring screens, hackathon, no caps | Ramp culture | Rapid visible adoption | Vanity-metric risk; Ramp-specific culture of velocity | early-adopter | [Charles AI-pilled](../research/sources/x-geoffintech-ai-pilled-article.md) (first-party) |
| Training and workshops | Traditional enablement | most orgs | Familiar | Ramp reports it mattered less than day-one skills | mainstream | [Goddijn](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party) |
| Decentralised authoring, central trust | Domain teams write skills in their own repos; a central registry reviews, versions and distributes; adoption comes from contributors becoming authors | PayPal skills registry ("Decentralize authoring. Centralize trust, governance, and distribution"); Ramp Dojo | Skills reflect domain expertise; org learns once; fits non-engineer contributors | Review bottleneck; no published reuse or quality data from either; depends on a platform team | emerging | [PayPal](../research/sources/paypal-agent-skills-at-enterprise-scale.md) (first-party); [Goddijn](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party) |
| Intake alias, then skills, plus champions and interns | Staff send unwanted work to a small team that does it with AI; recurring patterns become skill files, connections and automated apps; champions and assigned interns drive uptake | Cloudflare OS rollout (thousands of weekly users, >4,000 apps in 30 days, self-reported) | Finds real jobs before building; reveals which to standardise | Manual, costly early phase (admitted); self-reported numbers with no measurement method; Cloudflare-specific talent | early-adopter | [Rhea post](../research/sources/cloudflare-how-we-rethink-work-with-cloudflare-os.md) (first-party, self-reported) |
| Admin-curated agent catalog surfaced in the host users already use | An admin selects which task agents appear as tools in the org's MCP server; users do nothing but connect their host | Glean MCP server (agents as tools), Sentry hosted MCP | No new surface to learn; central curation; consistent across hosts | Tool-count bloat degrades host tool selection; descriptions drive routing; no adoption data published | early-adopter | [Glean](../research/sources/glean-agents-as-mcp-tools.md) (vendor) (vendor claim) |
| Peer showcase channel, then org-wide rollout | PMs demo their shared setups in a channel; personal skills are promoted to team assets | HubSpot PM org; Slack PMs | Spread from real use | No review of shared skills | early-adopter | [hubspot](../research/sources/claude-customers-hubspot-cowork.md), [slack](../research/sources/claude-slack-human-agent-teams.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Open tokens, then reach out to top spenders | No per-person budgets; cost managed after an experiment takes off | Ramp; DoorDash | Experimentation | Cost surprises; contrasts with the vendor's gate-then-cap advice | early-adopter | [ramp-office-hours](../research/sources/claude-customers-ramp-office-hours.md), [doordash](../research/sources/claude-customers-doordash-office-hours.md) (vendor claim); vs [cost-visibility](../research/sources/claude-cost-visibility-control.md) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Forcing event, with no bake-off | Commit without an evaluation; the company goes live over one weekend; a 48-hour backlog-to-production event | League (regulated healthcare) | Speed | Security foundation built after (about a quarter) | early-adopter | [league](../research/sources/claude-customers-league.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Steering committee of builders who train their teams | 15 cross-functional builders ship agents in a week from a repo template, then cascade | ABC Legal | Non-engineer builders with governance | Git was the hurdle | early-adopter | [abc-legal](../research/sources/claude-abc-legal-managed-agents-builders.md) (vendor claim) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |
| Per-person tool budget; buy what spreads | Each person gets a monthly budget for approved tools, and whatever peers adopt widely is bought company-wide | Brex (second-hand) | A bottom-up signal of adoption | Tool sprawl; governance comes after the fact | early-adopter | [DoP engineering velocity](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-engineering-velocity-non-engineers.md) (independent, relaying) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| AI fluency rubric for every role | A published rubric of the AI use expected in each role, which rates manual-only work "unacceptable" | Zapier (Wade Foster) | Makes the expectation explicit | Mandates usage, not outcomes; risk of performative use | early-adopter | [DoP AI fluency](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-ai-fluency.md) (independent, relaying) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Separate fast track in parallel with careful rollout | An independent team or program (labs team near the CEO, a frontier program for pioneers inside and outside the company, or a fast AI group split from a deliberate infrastructure group) pilots rough agents and new ways of working before company rollout | Google Labs (builder ladder piloted first); Microsoft Frontier; Airtable AI Platform vs slow-thinking group | Learn a year ahead without forcing everyone; tests org changes safely | Two tracks and two cultures to run; isolation from business units; outcomes unknown | early-adopter | [woodward](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-josh-woodward-labs-team.md), [chennapragada](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-aparna-chennapragada-microsoft.md), [liu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-howie-liu-airtable-reorg.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Recurring peer demo rituals | About 10 minutes of every weekly team meeting for members to demo AI use, including mundane uses; Demo Fridays, show-and-tells, pairing lunches, roundtables, learning labs | A consulting client measured it as a significant driver; Vercel Demo Fridays; Anthropic pairwise lunches; Zapier leader-run show-and-tells | Cheap, peer-led, normalises simple uses, routes feedback to whoever built the process; gives non-engineers permission to build | Depends on managers; no published effect size; can become showing off if leads present | early-adopter | [cw-driving-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-driving-ai-adoption.md), [cw-engineers-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineers-ai-adoption.md), [cw-cursor-pm](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-cursor-for-pm-work.md) (anecdote); [rauch](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-guillermo-rauch-v0.md) (vendor claim); [fung](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-fiona-fung-after-coding-solved.md), [foster](../research/sources/howiai-zapier-wade-foster-ai-stack.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Teams decide what stays human | Each team defines within its own scope what AI should handle and what stays human, with a real veto ("not that, not yet, not like this") | One operator's previous company | Turns resistance into a design input; shows where the human must stay; feeds activity-classification | Slower; teams may veto too much; needs leadership backing | emerging | [cw-driving-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-driving-ai-adoption.md) (anecdote) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Explicit permission (or prohibition of the old way) for pilot teams | Leaders tell a team it must work the new way (no docs, no Figma, always-on room) and penalise reverting, where teams don't feel allowed to | Gusto CTO's plan to scale the Cofounder way of working | Removes the hidden blocker of perceived rules | Heavy-handed; may not survive regulated review requirements | emerging | [kim](../research/sources/howiai-gusto-eddie-kim-cofounder.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Internal quest marketplace with a paved road to production | Anyone posts an automation need (risk rating, weeks saved, beneficiary); engineers, peers or an agent pick it up; output is a repo or skill built on a pre-vetted compliant template; XP rewards and weekly demos | Sendbird "Automators" | Turns latent demand into a visible, governed backlog; non-engineers ship safely | Needs a dedicated internal AI engineer and an Infosec task force; shadow-roadmap risk; no outcome data | emerging | [sendbird](../research/sources/howiai-sendbird-john-kim-adoption-playbook.md) (first-party) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Standard shape adopted by use, not mandate (new row) | The agent's output format for cards and asks becomes the platform's input format only when platform teams prefer asks arriving that way | The Verify failure (no fit, no mandate, no funding) vs Notify's self-serve trial with one bounded human check | Avoids the imposed standard nobody fills | Slower; some regulated routes must be mandated anyway | emerging | [NAO on Verify](../research/sources/nao-investigation-into-verify.md) (independent); [GOV.UK Notify](../research/sources/govuk-notify-trial-mode.md) (first-party); [Bottcher](../research/sources/fowler-bottcher-what-i-talk-about-platforms.md) (anecdote) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- *Product-led enablement*: Stripe pre-installed Claude Code on every laptop and devbox, pre-configured, behind a signed binary ([stripe](../research/sources/claude-customers-stripe-claude-code-rollout.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Decentralised authoring, central trust*: Cowork maturity ladder from chat to department plugins, champion-led "bottom-up discovery, top-down scale", with champion-authored skills as the leading indicator ([deploying-cowork](../research/sources/claude-guide-deploying-cowork-across-org.md), first-party). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- *Training and workshops*: survey barriers are integration (46%), data (42%) and change management (39%) ([state-of-agents](../research/sources/claude-2026-state-of-ai-agents.md), vendor claim). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Mandates, leaderboards, proficiency ladder**: LinkedIn put AI fluency into hiring and biannual reviews, created a Full Stack Builder title and ladder, and gated pod access on giving feedback; only about 5% adopt unaided — [cohen](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tomer-cohen-full-stack-builder.md) (first-party); Cisco made AI fluency non-negotiable top-down — [patel](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-jeetu-patel-cisco-ai.md) (first-party, low detail); Netflix added one cross-role AI-fluency aspiration rather than rewriting each level and allows AI in coding interviews — [stone](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-elizabeth-stone-netflix-systems-thinkers.md) (first-party); Intercom paired a near-$100M AI bet with rewritten values and formula-driven reviews, turnover about 40% — [mccabe](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-mccabe-intercom-ai-bet.md) (first-party); Sendbird's five-tier token ladder for tailored enablement plus executive one-on-ones with non-users — [sendbird](../research/sources/howiai-sendbird-john-kim-adoption-playbook.md) (first-party); Coinbase's timed PR speed run and data analysis to find and copy power users — [coinbase](../research/sources/howiai-coinbase-chintan-turakhia-ai-adoption.md) (first-party, show notes); Webflow builder days (half of design on Cursor after day one; 80+ prototypes on day two) — [wolan](../research/sources/howiai-webflow-rachel-wolan-chief-of-staff.md) (first-party, self-reported); Zapier's per-level fluency rubrics and the "delegation trap" of a memo cascading to one IC — [foster](../research/sources/howiai-zapier-wade-foster-ai-stack.md) (first-party); a small-team lead's six months of assigned experiments, then non-adoption becomes a performance conversation — [cw-driving-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-driving-ai-adoption.md) (anecdote); a mandate with a choice of tools worked, a mandate from outside the team did not — [cw-engineers-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-engineers-ai-adoption.md) (anecdote). Counter: top-down mandates alone are probably negative ROI — [sherwin-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sherwin-wu-openai-codex.md) (first-party, impression only). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Visibility as enablement, CEO in public**: the top adoption predictor in Every's consulting is whether the CEO uses a chatbot daily; a hedge fund pairs an AI-first memo with weekly prompt-sharing and usage emails — [shipper-every](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-every-ai-native.md) (first-party, consultant selling the service); a CEO's daily 4-6 a.m. AI block, "almost always impossible" bottom-up — [reganti-badam](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-reganti-badam-ai-deployments.md) (anecdote); Gumroad's CEO competing in a Devin PR contest and recording screen-shares — [lavingia](../research/sources/howiai-gumroad-sahil-lavingia-v0-devin.md) (first-party); Sendbird's top token users are the CTO and chief architect; Whoop's head of product solves problems live — [25-tactics](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-25-ai-adoption-tactics.md) (anecdote); Warp factory threads in public Slack show average users power users' prompts — [warp-factory](../research/sources/howiai-warp-zach-lloyd-software-factory.md) (vendor claim); public channels spread prompting skill — [scott-wu-devin](../research/sources/howiai-scott-wu-devin-junior-engineer.md) (vendor claim); one company-wide channel for testing models — [penn](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dianne-penn-evals-prds.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Training and workshops**: Pendo's designer-led workshops plus a golden path agreed with legal, security and finance — [greenbaum](../research/sources/howiai-brian-greenbaum-pendo-ai-adoption.md) (first-party, show notes); four weeks per team, an hour a week, job-specific prompts, on a 10% eager / 10% refuser / 80% "tell me how" split — [shipper-every](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-every-ai-native.md) (first-party); course-led ladders (20,000+ workers; 30,000+ PMs) — [raviv-copilot](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tal-raviv-ai-copilot.md), [pm-leverage-ladders](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-pm-leverage-ladders.md), [product-sense-1](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-ai-product-sense-part-1.md) (anecdote); a 30-day micro-assignment program — [gridley](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-hilary-gridley-supermanager.md) (first-party); quarterly learning day, AI guild, roving unblocker — [cw-vibe-coding](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-vibe-coding-team-sport.md) (anecdote). Caveat: setup is easy, managing context is the hard skill; one practitioner's folder setup took six months to teach — [cw-fragmented](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-fragmented-ai-usage.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Intake alias, then skills, plus champions and interns**: Every's dedicated AI operations lead collects repetitive tasks and builds prompts and workflows for everyone (15 people) — [shipper-every](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-every-ai-native.md) (first-party); a staffed tiger team of technical-adjacent non-engineers — [sherwin-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sherwin-wu-openai-codex.md) (first-party); a 6-8 person AI working group with Slack channels, learning labs and contests — [cw-fragmented](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-fragmented-ai-usage.md) (anecdote); champions plus a small pilot in a large financial company — [cw-discovery-big-org](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-discovery-big-org.md) (anecdote); a vendor hand-onboarded 200-300 early users in two weeks — [ugarte](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-roman-ugarte-grok-bot.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Product-led enablement**: Claude Code added /powerup onboarding against its no-onboarding principle because users faced "100 features"; customising skills needs too many concepts — [cat-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-cat-wu-anthropic-product-team.md) (first-party); Cowork built from latent demand seen in Claude Code use — [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party); Notion's playground commands teach prerequisites, yet it is "still really for me" with 5-10 heavy users — [notion-playground](../research/sources/howiai-notion-brian-lovin-prototype-playground.md) (first-party); one PM's harness packaged as a plugin that personalises itself in about 15 minutes with auto-updates that spare personal memory — [blum](../research/sources/howiai-melio-daniel-blum-pm-assistant.md) (anecdote); a PM's meta-project that helps colleagues create their own — [cw-team-velocity](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-team-velocity.md) (anecdote); setup skills, example prompts and no token caps at first — [cw-driving-adoption](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-driving-ai-adoption.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Decentralised authoring, central trust**: Block's bottom-up pockets (sales, engineering) plus top-down standardisation; 40-50 internal MCP extensions — [block-goose](../research/sources/howiai-block-goose-brosamer-axen.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **One domain, one surface first**: async Codex Cloud was hard to adopt; growth came from interactive IDE/CLI pairing whose configuration later enables delegation; internal power users were a misleading sample — [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim). Cost transparency per operation as a tactic in a regulated company — [kaul](../research/sources/howiai-prerna-kaul-fda-submissions.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Adoption sorts the workforce: AI lifts about half and destabilises others, more at 10,000+ person companies (23% vs 15% at 1-10); the top fear is more work for the same pay — [tech-workers-2026](../research/sources/lenny-newsletter-tech-workers-2026-survey.md) (independent). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Overlapping rows (contribution merge 2026-10-24): *Peer showcase channel, then org-wide rollout* and *Recurring peer demo rituals* both spread use through peer demos; kept separate because the first adds promotion of personal skills to team assets.

### Key questions to choose
1. Would our target users' orgs tolerate mandates/leaderboards, or does adoption have to be product-led? — *answered by:* user interviews — *affects:* all
2. How is 'adoption' defined and does it correlate with outcomes (Ramp reports 99.5% but no outcome data)? — *answered by:* ask Ramp; define own metrics — *affects:* all
3. Who should author and maintain standard skills for our users (a central product team, domain experts, or the users themselves), and what incentive or tooling makes them do it? - *answered by:* user interviews with domain experts; trial of a contribute-and-review flow - *affects:* decentralised authoring vs product-led enablement
4. Is an intake-alias discovery phase (watch real requests, then productise the patterns) a cheaper way to choose which jobs to standardise than designing a jobs map first? — *answered by:* small pilot with one team; compare with the PM jobs map — *affects:* intake-alias vs product-led enablement
- (Track 3) Do users call task agents from the harness they already use (Claude Desktop, Cursor, Claude Code, Codex) or from a new surface, and which hosts must we support? Protocol features (MCP Tasks, A2A clients, plugins) differ by host. — *answered by:* user interviews, usage data — *affects:* MCP-agent catalog vs own surface
6. Is 1,370 Stripe engineers seats or weekly actives, and what are the active rates behind the 88-97% adoption claims (Cyera, Zapier, Jamf)? — *answered by:* customers' platform teams — *affects:* all. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
7. Anthropic's scaling guide is engineer-only (one line on PMs). What does a PM-first rollout need that an engineering rollout doesn't? — *answered by:* HubSpot or Miro PM leads; a pilot — *affects:* all ([scaling-agentic-coding](../research/sources/claude-guide-scaling-agentic-coding.md)). (from research-claude-resources-articles)
8. Do personal tutorials, which teach PMs to build their own dashboards, Projects and loops, produce anything others reuse, or do they need a packaging step (Glass's "one person's win becomes everyone's baseline")? — *answered by:* counting reuse of PM-built tools in a pilot — *affects:* adoption-model, capability-distribution-trust (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
9. Does the adoption plan turn productivity gains into relief or into a higher quota, and how would staff know? — *answered by:* an explicit policy on scope and workload, plus burnout and sentiment tracking alongside usage — *affects:* adoption-model, target-outcome. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
10. Should enablement start by giving each team the decision of what stays human, or by mandating use and measuring it? — *answered by:* a pilot comparing veto-style design sessions with a mandate, measuring sustained weekly use after 8-12 weeks — *affects:* adoption-model, activity-classification, review-and-autonomy. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
11. Which platform teams would accept asks in a standard shape today, and what would they want in return (fewer malformed requests, a demand signal)? — *answered by:* platform owner interviews — *affects:* adopted vs mandated standard (from research-dependency-agent)


#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
Baseline evidence: before Kai, 8,500 of ~10,000 Stripes used LLM tools daily (Oct 2025), via GoLLM presets and a LibreChat front end on Toolshed ([Latent Space, Sands](../research/sources/latentspace-emily-sands-stripe.md) (first-party)).

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| One front door, no knobs, pilot then company demo | Ship a v0 fast, pilot with the most eager function (GTM), then a company-wide demo; no setup ("available to every employee on Day 0"); remove configuration choices | Stripe Kai (296 to 5,000+ users in ~4 weeks; 83% weekly) | Fast ramp among people who had bounced off configurable tools | Self-reported; Stripe's AI-forward culture and infra may not transfer | early-adopter | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party); [LangChain case study](../research/sources/langchain-how-stripe-built-kai-deep-agents.md) (vendor claim); [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |
| Put the install prompt at the moment of intent | Distribution plays (install prompts at CLI login, docs buttons) beat better content | Stripe skill for external developers (30-35% copied the install command) | Cheap adoption lever | Measured for developers, not employees | early-adopter | [steering experiments](../research/sources/stripe-ai-steering-experiments.md) (first-party) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
5. How much of Kai's ramp is the product vs Stripe's preconditions (an AI-forward culture, 8,500 daily LLM-tool users already in Oct 2025, a mature data catalog)? — *answered by:* compare adoption curves at a less AI-forward org; ask Stripe what the pre-Kai baseline was — *affects:* all adoption options



River/Aquifer additions (competitive-teardown-shopify-river, 2026-10-24):

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Visibility as enablement, CEO in public | Agent works only in public; the CEO runs his own channel (#tobi_river, 100+ watchers); learning by watching, "no curriculum, no training plan, no manager"; AI use assessed in performance reviews | Shopify River | Fast diffusion (5,938 users in 30 days by May 2026, self-reported) | Needs leadership modelling and a culture of working in public; chilling effect (see [[trust-privacy-defaults]]) | early-adopter | [Tobi article](../research/sources/x-tobi-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (first-party); [BVP](../research/sources/bvp-inside-shopifys-ai-first-engineering-playbook.md) (first-party) |
| Internal marketing with an agent persona | AI-generated ads with the agent as a character (Mac-vs-PC parody) to win back users who tried an early version | Shopify Remote Dev team (River share of merged mobile PRs 6%→61%) | Reverses "this is not for me" after a bad first try | Attribution mixed with infra improvements; self-reported | early-adopter | [Remote Dev](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-remote-dev.md) (first-party) |

Department of Product pass (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24):

| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Agentic pods: engineers embedded with a function | Two-week pods pair an AI-proficient engineer with a domain expert: shadow, prioritise, build with them, validate, ship | Uber (~30 engineers; 16 pods in 16 functions; e.g. city capital allocation 15 h → 30 min) | Agents fit real workflows; whole workflows redesigned; adoption built in | Engineer capacity; scales only with pods; outcomes self-reported | early-adopter | [Uber CTO](../research/sources/x-praveen-uber-agentic-pods.md) (first-party) |
| One domain, one surface first | Start with the most repeatable job and the tool its people already use, then widen | Stripe Kai (GTM pilot, per Stripe; "sales reps researching accounts", per DoP) | Fast proof, clear measure | Can stall as a point tool | early-adopter | [Department of Product](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-stripe-kai.md) (independent); [How I AI](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |


---

## agent-interface: Agent interface: own app, chat, or embedded
**Choice:** Where people meet the agent: our own app (desktop or web workspace), the chat tool they already live in (Slack, Teams), the IDE/CLI, embedded inside existing tools (docs, tracker, browser), or one agent service behind several of these.
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The surface decides who can use the agent on day one, what work is visible to others, what UI we must fund (artifact rendering, review panes, cost meters), and how much of the experience a chat vendor controls. River (Slack only) and Glass (own desktop app) are the two strongest opposite data points.
**Related themes:** [[agent-as-service-many-surfaces]], [[org-learning-loop]], [[shared-persistent-sessions]], [[remote-agent-invocation]], [[enablement-by-product]]   **Related dimensions:** [[trust-privacy-defaults]], [[session-persistence-sharing]], [[artifact-and-project-structure]], [[adoption-model]], [[remote-invocation-route]], [[runtime-location]]
Opened by competitive-teardown-shopify-river, in parallel with competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents.

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Chat-native only (agent as a channel member) | The agent has no UI of its own; you @-mention it in a channel and it replies in thread; outputs are links (PRs, sites) | Shopify River (Slack, no DMs, "@river" top-level post then thread) | Zero install; joins human threads already in progress ("@river would be good to get your opinion"); visibility and search come free from the chat tool; 5,938 employees in 30 days by May 2026, 121,804 threads in Sep 2026 (self-reported) | Chat UI is poor for long artifacts, diffs, rich review, cost meters; rich outputs need a second surface (Shopify built Quick and Live Preview links); chat vendor owns UX, retention and API limits; visibility follows the chat tool's permission model | early-adopter | [Under the River](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-under-the-river.md) (first-party); [Tobi article](../research/sources/x-tobi-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (first-party); [Tobi replies](../research/sources/x-tobi-river-replies-may-2026.md) (first-party); [Tobi chart](../research/sources/x-tobi-river-floods-shopify-chart.md) (first-party) |
| Chat-native plus a companion web surface for rich output | Chat is the front door; links open a web page for what chat can't show (live app preview, hosted artifact) | River + Quick sites + Remote Dev Live Preview (simulator streamed to a shareable Quick page) | Keeps chat as the shared place while giving review a real UI | Two surfaces to maintain; still no place to browse or manage sessions outside chat | early-adopter | [Quick](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-quick.md) (first-party); [Remote Dev](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-remote-dev.md) (first-party) |
| Own app / workspace | A desktop or web app built for agent work: panes, inline rendering, skills marketplace, scheduling, cost meter | Ramp Glass (desktop app, SSO, split panes, Dojo); Claude Cowork; Cloudflare OS | Full control of UX for long and artifact-heavy work; can expose memory, cost and review; suits non-engineers' multi-step work | A new surface to adopt; work is private unless the app builds sharing (Glass added share-session); laptop-bound runtime in Glass's case | early-adopter | [Goddijn](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party); [Buchan](../research/sources/x-shanebuchan-how-we-built-glass.md) (first-party); [Cloudflare OS](../research/sources/cloudflare-os-announcement.md) (vendor claim) |
| IDE / CLI | The agent lives in the developer's terminal or editor | Claude Code, Cursor, Codex CLI | Best for engineers' deep work; mature tooling | "Local agents have a ceiling": private, dies with the session, others don't learn (Shopify's stated reason for building River); excludes most non-engineers | mainstream | [Under the River](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-under-the-river.md) (first-party) |
| Embedded in existing tools / host-agnostic service | One agent service reached from many hosts: chat, web, browser extension, docs, tracker, or as tools in another harness via MCP | Ramp Inspect (Slack, web with hosted VS Code, Chrome extension); Glean agents as MCP tools; Open-Inspect (web, Slack, GitHub, Linear) | Meets each user where they are; one backend and session model | Capabilities differ per surface; harder to make work visible across surfaces; more integration upkeep | early-adopter | [Modal on Inspect](../research/sources/modal-ramp-inspect-background-agent.md) (vendor claim); [Glean](../research/sources/glean-agents-as-mcp-tools.md) (vendor claim); [Open-Inspect](../research/sources/github-open-inspect-background-agents.md) (first-party) |
| Public community chat as the primary interface (precedent outside agents) | Users must work in shared public channels to use the tool | Midjourney's Discord-first years | Users learn a new tacit skill by watching others' prompts | Consumer precedent; opinion, not data | n/a (precedent) | [Simon Willison](../research/sources/simonwillison-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (independent) |
| Vendor chat-agent offering | The model vendor's or chat vendor's own agent in Slack/Teams | Claude Tag in Slack; Codex Slack integration; Devin in Slack | No build; chat-native from day one | The vendor's identity and visibility model applies (channel members can direct the agent within its bundle; approval responses readable in channel) | early-adopter | [Tenable on Claude Tag](../research/sources/tenable-claude-tag-slack-access-model.md) (independent); [Codex Slack docs](../research/sources/openai-codex-cloud-slack.md) (first-party); [Answer.AI on Devin](../research/sources/answerai-month-with-devin.md) (independent) |
| Separate design surface with a handoff to the coding harness | Prototypes and decks are made on a design canvas grounded in the design system, then one command pulls them into Claude Code | Claude Design | Visual iteration for PMs, with continuity into the repo | Another surface to govern. It is a research preview, and export failed when DoP tested it | preview | [DoP Claude Design](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-claude-design.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Personal PM cockpit with a "draft an update" action | A PM vibe-codes a local dashboard over their own data, with an action that generates an update for a chosen audience | DoP product dashboard | Tailored and immediate | Personal and local, on dummy data unless wired up; nothing is shared | early-adopter | [DoP product dashboard](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-product-dashboard-stakeholder-updates.md) (independent) (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series) |
| Structured-form slash commands | A slash command opens a structured form that launches a workflow, like filling in a brief | Cowork plugins for enterprise | (not stated in the contribution) | (not stated in the contribution) | emerging | [cowork-plugins-enterprise](../research/sources/claude-cowork-plugins-enterprise.md) (first-party) (from research-claude-resources-articles) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
