- New evidence: one Claude that escalates from chat to long-running work, after users could not tell "where a task belonged" ([cowork-chat-unified](../research/sources/claude-cowork-chat-unified.md)); a task board as orchestrator (Notion, Rakuten Kanban) ([notion](../research/sources/claude-customers-notion-managed-agents.md), [rakuten](../research/sources/claude-customers-rakuten-every-employee-builder.md)); one front door over 40 tools instead of each SaaS tool's AI ([cyera](../research/sources/claude-customers-cyera-cowork-front-door.md)) (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Own app / workspace**: OpenAI tried Codex inside ChatGPT desktop and Atlas, but marketing, comms, finance and legal staff would not leave the Codex app despite code and command approvals; strategy became one app that starts simple — [ambrosino](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-ambrosino-codex-product-work.md) (first-party); Grok Bot is a separate app rather than a tab in Cursor, internals hidden, automations in natural language — [ugarte](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-roman-ugarte-grok-bot.md) (vendor claim); one box that chooses harness and model, with Work mode as Codex minus the coding UI — [seshan](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-tara-seshan-persistent-coworkers.md) (first-party); Claude's multiple entry points exist because form factors are unsettled; the end state is probably one place — [rieseberg](../research/sources/howiai-felix-rieseberg-cowork.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Embedded in existing tools / host-agnostic service**: Notion engineers @mention Codex from a task comment and get a PR in the thread — [nystrom](../research/sources/howiai-notion-ryan-nystrom-spec-driven.md) (first-party); Dots is reachable through any channel with no model picker — [sottiaux](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sottiaux-chatgpt-dots.md) (vendor claim); SaaS used inside the agent workspace via an in-app browser — [shipper-paradox](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-dan-shipper-ai-paradox.md) (anecdote); a SaaS builder shipped an external MCP server because enterprise customers may only use their corporate-blessed AI — [cw-am-calls](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-am-calls-mcp-server.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- **Chat-native plus a companion web surface**: chat as the right catch-all with functional GUIs for experts — [weil](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-kevin-weil-openai-cpo.md) (first-party), [embiricos](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-embiricos-codex-human-bottleneck.md) (vendor claim); "chat is not the final boss" — [udezue](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-udezue-ai-reshaping-pm-role.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Status and review pane: background agents need a status line with progressive disclosure (working; on what; the steps; raw calls only if needed) — [muse](../research/sources/howiai-meta-muse-alex-cornell.md) (first-party); latency kills synchronous use of a personal agent and phone-only presence fails at work — [duet-review](../research/sources/howiai-claire-vo-claude-duet-review.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Key questions to choose
1. Where do our target users already ask colleagues for help and discuss work (Slack, Teams, doc comments, tracker), and is that tool a system of record or just a chat pipe? — *answered by:* user interviews; chat-tool usage data from IT — *affects:* chat-native vs own app
2. Which of the first jobs produce artifacts that chat can't show well (specs, prototypes, decks, dashboards, diffs), and would a companion link surface be enough? — *answered by:* jobs map plus a two-version prototype (thread only vs thread + web page) on two real jobs — *affects:* chat-native only vs chat + companion vs own app
3. Does the org's chat tool allow a bot of this kind: API rate limits, retention settings, eDiscovery, admin approval for apps, Enterprise Grid features? — *answered by:* IT / chat-platform owner — *affects:* chat-native options, vendor chat-agent
4. Is Shopify's adoption (5,938 users in 30 days) due to the Slack surface, the monorepo substrate, or a CEO-led culture? Can we separate them? — *answered by:* ask Shopify builders if reachable; compare with Ramp's own-app adoption curve; more research — *affects:* all
5. Do non-engineers (PMs, designers, support) get as much from a chat-only agent as engineers do? Shopify says PMs and designers use River via Remote Dev but gives no split by role. — *answered by:* ask Shopify; pilot with mixed roles — *affects:* chat-native vs own app
6. If we choose a chat surface, which one: is the target org on Slack or Teams, and does it differ across target orgs? — *answered by:* the project owner / target-org inventory — *affects:* chat-native, vendor offering
8. Does a PM product agent live in the work tracker (where Notion and Asana put it), in chat, or in its own app? — *answered by:* user interviews (from research-claude-resources-articles)
9. Is our PM agent an MCP client that reaches other tools, or a server that other agents call? DoP reports that Intercom chose the first and Stripe the second. — *answered by:* where PMs already work, and whether the agent's value depends on being the front door — *affects:* agent-interface, remote-invocation-route (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
10. If a regulated customer's employees may only use one corporate-blessed assistant, is a PM harness a product they open, or a set of skills and MCP tools delivered into that assistant? — *answered by:* procurement and policy interviews with target enterprises — *affects:* agent-interface, harness-layering, loop-source. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
11. For each persona (PM, designer, engineer, reviewer), what level of agent activity should be visible by default and what one tap away for audit? — *answered by:* usability tests of a layered status design — *affects:* own app, review UI, trust-privacy-defaults. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Current lean and what would change it
No lean. Evidence so far: chat-native wins on reach and on making work visible by default (River); an own app wins on rich, artifact-heavy, multi-step work for non-engineers (Glass); and both large adopters ended up with more than one surface (River + Quick/Live Preview; Inspect's Slack + web + extension). A finding that our first jobs are artifact-heavy would push toward an own app or chat + companion; a finding that most value comes from pulling the agent into existing human threads would push toward chat-native.


#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| One agent service, web app default plus chat, browser extension and API embedding (Stripe detail) | "The agent is a service, not an application, and surfaces are simply customized views into it": an opinionated web app most people use (no setup, day 0), a Slack integration, Chrome extensions inside third-party web tools, and internal apps embedding the agent via API (e.g. Finance's budget-modelling app) | Stripe Kai | Meets people in each tool without a product per surface; one agent and governance model behind all | Building the API, extension and embeds; rich artifacts still need the web app | early-adopter | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party); [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |
| Chat reaction as the front door for unattended task agents (evidence row for chat-native) | Any Slack message becomes a task by emoji reaction; results reported in thread; runs on a phone | Stripe Minions | Lowest activation energy; the whole company is already in Slack | Only for one-shot tasks with strong CI | early-adopter | [How I AI, Kaliski](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-minions-kaliski.md) (first-party) |
| Specialist agents with their own app, exposed to other agents over MCP | A specialist keeps its own UI (browser tab with prototype and chat) but publishes tools so a general agent can drive it | Stripe Harbor (driven by Kai and coding agents) | Rich, job-specific UI where it matters, no forced app-switch elsewhere | Several apps to learn | early-adopter | [Harbor post](../research/sources/stripe-harbor-ai-prototyping.md) (first-party) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
7. Stripe rejected both a standalone app ("would force users out of their natural workflows") and a product per surface ("hard to maintain"), yet most use is still its web app. What share of use would land in embedded surfaces for our users, and is the embed work worth funding before the web app? — *answered by:* ask Stripe for the surface split; instrument a pilot with web plus one embed — *affects:* embedded/host-agnostic, own app, chat-native

## trust-privacy-defaults: Trust and privacy defaults for sessions and context
**Choice:** Who can see an agent session (its transcript, artifacts and memory) by default: only its owner, the people invited, a team or project, or everyone in the org; and whether private work is allowed at all.
**Why it matters to the strategy:** It decides how much the org learns from each session (Shopify's whole thesis) against what users will be willing to ask, what sensitive data leaks, and whether the design survives a regulated setting. It is a policy choice as much as a product one and has its own owners (security, legal, HR, works councils).
**Related themes:** [[public-by-default-agent-work]], [[org-learning-loop]], [[shared-persistent-sessions]], [[identity-delegation]], [[task-context-isolation]], [[scoped-governance-containers]], [[compounding-context-repo]]   **Related dimensions:** [[agent-interface]], [[session-persistence-sharing]], [[agent-identity-delegation]], [[governance-control-plane]], [[context-and-memory]], [[artifact-and-project-structure]]
Opened by competitive-teardown-shopify-river, in parallel with competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents.

### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Private by default, no sharing | Sessions belong to one user; nothing visible to others | Local Claude Code, Cursor, ChatGPT ("a private window" in Tobi's words) | Users ask anything; no leakage between colleagues; simplest compliance story | Org learns nothing ("the only person who learns anything is the person at the keyboard"); transcripts deleted (Claude Code 30-day default) | mainstream | [Tobi article](../research/sources/x-tobi-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (first-party, argues against); [ITdaily](../research/sources/itdaily-claude-code-30-day-transcript-deletion.md) (independent) |
| Private by default, share on demand | The owner chooses to share a session (snapshot link, hand-off, invite) | Ramp Glass share-session; Claude Code on the web Team-visibility link | Users control exposure; supports hand-off | Few sessions may be shared (Ramp gives no share rate); learning depends on individual effort | early-adopter | [Glass launch](../research/sources/ramp-introducing-glass.md) (first-party); [Claude cloud docs](../research/sources/anthropic-claude-code-cloud-sessions.md) (first-party) |
| Private draft, then publish the outcome | Work privately; share a cleaned artifact, skill or blueprint, not the transcript | Cloudflare OS blueprints (code without data or history); skills marketplaces (Glass Dojo) | Learning through reusable recipes; experiments stay private | Loses "how people begin the work", which Tobi says matters most; relies on people publishing | emerging | [Cloudflare OS](../research/sources/cloudflare-os-announcement.md) (vendor claim); [Goddijn](../research/sources/x-sebgoddijn-glass-article.md) (first-party); [Bernstein](../research/sources/asq-bernstein-transparency-paradox.md) (independent: privacy zones, then share once there is data) |
| Team- or project-scoped by default | Sessions visible to a team, project or workspace with its own policies | Confluence space permissions; Stripe Kai project-scoped tool policies | Learning within the group that shares context; sensitive work fenced by container | Silos between teams; container design and membership upkeep | early-adopter | [Confluence restrictions](../research/sources/atlassian-confluence-page-restrictions.md) (first-party) |
| Public by default org-wide, no private mode | Every session is a transcript open to all employees; the agent refuses DMs and private channels ("you can't go into a private channel and say, 'Hey, River, help me build'", Thawar) | Shopify River ("River only works in the open. No direct messages"); Shopify Quick (all sites open to all employees, no owners) | Osmosis learning (Lehrwerkstatt); a corpus to mine into skills and defaults (merge rate 36%→77% claimed); "honesty pressure" on agent and user; co-participation (171,853 same-thread links in Sep 2026) | Chilling effect ("Some questions that would have been asked in a DM just don't get asked", River's own words); sensitive data (customer PII, security findings, HR, M&A, incidents) in open threads; channel join policy becomes the access control; broad visibility can breed hiding (Bernstein); unclear fit with regulated walls; all evidence self-reported | early-adopter (one large adopter) | [Under the River](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-under-the-river.md) (first-party); [Tobi article](../research/sources/x-tobi-learning-on-the-shop-floor.md) (first-party); [River's reaction](../research/sources/x-tobi-river-reaction-to-shop-floor-post.md) (first-party); [Quick](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-quick.md) (first-party); [Bernstein](../research/sources/asq-bernstein-transparency-paradox.md) (independent, counter-evidence); [Beyond the Pilot](../research/sources/venturebeat-beyond-the-pilot-farhan-thawar.md) (first-party); critiques: [Magica, quoting Slack's CPO on a boundary for "genuinely sensitive work"](../research/sources/magica-shopify-river-agent-context-cost.md) (independent), [AI Slopcast: an archive of everyone's gaps is "a treasure trove" for HR](../research/sources/oleg-guru-ai-slopcast-shopify-river-lehrwerkstatt.md) (independent), [HN anecdote](../research/sources/hn-shopify-river-discussion.md) (anecdote) |
| Public by default with fenced exceptions | Open by default, with named private zones (security, HR, legal, regulated teams) and information barriers | Implied by River's security-remediation threads (where they live is unstated); Slack information barriers | Most of the learning benefit, with walls where law or risk requires | Who decides the exceptions; exceptions grow; agent memory and skills may carry data across the wall | emerging (inferred; no documented example) | [River vuln remediation](../research/sources/shopify-engineering-river-vulnerability-remediation.md) (first-party, silent on visibility); [Slack information barriers](../research/sources/slack-information-barriers-docs.md) (first-party: barriers don't stop interaction in shared channels) |
| Channel-membership-scoped agent credentials | The agent acts with a bundle or service identity in a channel; whoever can post there can direct it, and new members see scrollback | Claude Tag; Codex Slack (responses readable by channel members) | Simple to deploy in chat | Visibility and authority both follow channel membership; public-channel join policy governs access to bundled credentials | early-adopter | [Tenable](../research/sources/tenable-claude-tag-slack-access-model.md) (independent); [Codex Slack](../research/sources/openai-codex-cloud-slack.md) (first-party) |
| Task-scoped permissions derived from the request | Before running, the system infers from the request which tools and scopes are needed and grants only those (read-only for "summarise my inbox"), with taint tracking so humans are asked only on high-risk actions | Google's CaMeL (research technique) | Blocks prompt injection for single-mode tasks without relying on guardrails | Fails when one task reads untrusted data and also writes or sends; requires re-architecture | emerging (research) | [schulhoff](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sander-schulhoff-ai-security.md), [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent) (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive) |
| Timings per platform and gate, never per person (new row) | The agent's wait-time and response data is aggregated by platform and gate for the portfolio signal; owner-level data stays in the session | The project's chilling-effect evidence | The portfolio signal without surveillance | Loses the "which owner is overloaded" view some managers want | n/a | [Bernstein](../research/sources/asq-bernstein-transparency-paradox.md) (independent) (from research-dependency-agent) |

See also, evidence for existing options and placement notes (from the contribution merge, 2026-10-24):
- New evidence: public by default with fenced exceptions (Anthropic, Slack CPO) vs Claude Tag's "generous access first" vs PHI walled off by channel scoping (Tennr, Insight Health) ([human-agent-teams](../research/sources/claude-building-effective-human-agent-teams.md), [slack](../research/sources/claude-slack-human-agent-teams.md), [tag-healthcare](../research/sources/claude-tag-healthcare-human-agent-teams.md)). Prompt content is in Cowork's OTel by default ([ciso-guide](../research/sources/claude-ciso-guide-agentic-ai.md)) (from research-claude-resources-articles)
- **Isolation by task context, not just user permission**: any data the AI can access, a user can make it leak; any action it can take, a user can make it take — [schulhoff](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sander-schulhoff-ai-security.md) (independent); ChatGPT Sites shows each viewer only the data their own connectors grant — [korevec](../research/sources/howiai-openai-korevec-chatgpt-sites.md) (vendor claim). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
- Lethal trifecta: any PM agent that reads untrusted text (customer email, tickets, forums) while holding private data and an outbound channel is exposed; filters at 97% are a failing grade; cut the exfiltration leg — [willison](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-simon-willison-state-of-ai.md) (independent). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)

### Key questions to choose
1. What share of the target jobs touch data that can't be open to the whole org (customer PII, financials, security, HR, legal, unreleased strategy), and can those jobs be fenced without killing the default? — *answered by:* jobs map scored with a data-classification owner; security/privacy policy owner — *affects:* public-by-default, fenced exceptions, team-scoped
2. Is the target a regulated organisation with information barriers, records-retention, or works-council rules on employee monitoring? If so, is public-by-default allowed at all? — *answered by:* the project owner; compliance, legal and (in the EU) works council — *affects:* public options
3. How large is the chilling effect: which questions stop being asked when sessions are public, and are they the low-value ones (River's claim) or the important, uncertain ones (Bernstein's finding)? — *answered by:* a pilot with matched teams (public vs private default), counting questions asked and their kind; user interviews — *affects:* public-by-default vs private-by-default
4. Does visibility itself drive the learning, or does it come from someone mining the corpus into skills (Shopify does both)? If mining is what matters, private-by-default with an opt-in corpus might capture most of the value. — *answered by:* ask Shopify; a pilot measuring reuse of others' threads vs reuse of mined skills — *affects:* public-by-default vs private draft then publish
5. When a session is visible, does the viewer see only what their own permissions allow (re-check), or everything the session saw with the creator's access? — *answered by:* security owner; prototype — *affects:* all shared options; see [[agent-identity-delegation]]
6. What does our org's culture support: would a CEO-modelled public channel (Tobi's #tobi_river, 100+ watchers) work here, or does public-by-default need a leadership mandate we don't have? — *answered by:* the project owner / leadership; user interviews — *affects:* public-by-default
7. How are memory and skills learned from public sessions scrubbed of sensitive data before they are reused everywhere? — *answered by:* prototype of a mining pipeline with a redaction step; Track 5 memory research — *affects:* public-by-default, private draft then publish
10. How does public-by-default work under information barriers in a regulated org? — *answered by:* compliance (from research-claude-resources-articles)
11. Which PM-agent jobs combine reading untrusted input (customer feedback, email, web pages) with write actions to shared systems, so task-scoped permissions alone cannot protect them? — *answered by:* a read/write map of the target PM jobs — *affects:* trust-privacy-defaults, governance-control-plane, sequencing. (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
12. Is per-owner response data acceptable to works councils or HR in the target organisation, and if not, is the per-platform view enough for platform owners? — *answered by:* HR and policy owners; platform owner interviews — *affects:* timings per platform and gate, never per person (from research-dependency-agent)

### Current lean and what would change it
No lean. Shopify is the only large-scale evidence for public-by-default and it is all self-reported; the one independent study found the opposite effect in a very different setting (factory floor, 2009). A finding that the target org is regulated, or that the first jobs are mostly sensitive, pushes toward private or team-scoped defaults with a publish step; a pilot showing a small chilling effect and high reuse of others' threads would push toward public-by-default.


#### Stripe knowledge-agents additions (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24)
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Private single-user sessions, personal sources opt-in per session (Stripe detail for "Private by default") | Sessions belong to the user (sharing on the roadmap); org chart, date and project system known by default; Drive, Slack and DMs connect only if the user opts in, togglable per session or day | Stripe Kai | Users control what personal context the agent sees; low leakage risk | Context "locked in"; org learns only through published skills | early-adopter | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party); [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |
| Isolation by task context, not just user permission | The platform forbids combining data from two unrelated customer contexts in one session even when the user can access each; boundary is "what should this task be allowed to view given this context?" | Stripe Kai | Prevents cross-customer contamination in analyses and artifacts | Needs data tagged by context and enforcement in tools; mechanism not disclosed | emerging | [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party) |
| Team- or project-scoped by default (Stripe evidence) | Projects (5 to 500 people, open to join) carry tool policies, default model, human confirmation on sensitive tools, and for HR a separately secured back end | Stripe Kai projects | Policy fits the work's sensitivity; one owner decides for many | Session visibility inside a project not stated (sessions appear single-user) | early-adopter | [How I AI, Krishnamurthy](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-kai-krishnamurthy.md) (first-party) |
| Open by default for coding-agent runs, private for knowledge work (mixed defaults by work family) | Coding-agent runs posted in a public Slack channel others watch; knowledge-work sessions private | Stripe Minions robots channel (76 watchers) vs Kai | Learning from visible runs where data is low-risk (code in QA devboxes) | Two policies to explain | anecdote | [How I AI, Kaliski](../research/sources/howiai-stripe-minions-kaliski.md) (first-party); [Stripe Kai post](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md) (first-party) |

Key questions (Stripe pass):
8. Stripe makes coding runs visible and knowledge-work sessions private; Shopify makes everything public. Is the right default set per work family or data class rather than org-wide? — *answered by:* jobs map scored by data class; ask Stripe and Shopify why their defaults differ — *affects:* all options
9. How would task-context isolation be enforced in our setting (data tags, tool-level checks, session forks)? — *answered by:* security owner; prototype with two customer datasets — *affects:* private, project-scoped and public options


Department of Product pass (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents, 2026-10-24): an independent explainer extends Stripe's task-context isolation to other domains: never two candidates for the same role side by side; never two patients summarised together; never performance and compensation data combined in one output. It argues such **combination guardrails** must be enforced at infrastructure or session level, not left to skill owners, and offers a discovery method: list what each group may access, then look at combinations, define each rule by harm and detection, and rank by likelihood × severity ([Department of Product](../research/sources/departmentofproduct-stripe-kai.md), independent). Key question: 10. Which combinations of individually accessible data must never meet in one session in our target orgs (e.g. two customers' roadmaps, a person's performance and pay), and can they be enforced below the skill layer? — *answered by:* security and policy owner workshop using that method — *affects:* all options


---

## dependency-knowledge-and-intake: Dependency knowledge and intake
**Choice:** Where the organisation's knowledge of dependencies, platforms and gates lives, who verifies it, and how far an agent may act on a platform's intake and a governance gate (read, draft, submit on behalf of a person, submit under policy with automatic approval for routine cases).
**Why it matters to the strategy:** The research found no shipped product that discovers undeclared cross-platform dependencies, and found that every planning agent reads only what people recorded; so the choice of where the record lives and who keeps it true decides what any dependency agent can do, and the choice of how far it may act at intake and gates decides its risk in a regulated firm.
**Related themes:** [[agent-legible-platform]], [[onboarding-as-code]], [[governance-as-code]], [[stale-records-mislead-agents]], [[agent-originated-requests]], [[process-governance-load]]   **Related dimensions:** [[context-and-memory]], [[governance-control-plane]], [[agent-identity-delegation]], [[review-and-autonomy]]

Synthesis: [tasks/research-dependency-agent/00-summary.md](../tasks/research-dependency-agent/00-summary.md) and its deliverables 01 to 06. The organising idea: a dependency agent's value is bounded by how legible the organisation's platforms and processes are to it (the environment axis in [05-maturity-model.md](../tasks/research-dependency-agent/05-maturity-model.md)).

### Sub-choice: dependency-record-home — Where dependency and platform knowledge lives and how it stays true

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Existing catalogues and CMDB, read as they are | The agent reads Backstage, Port, the CMDB, CODEOWNERS and the ticket tool and treats them as truth | Port MCP server; Backstage catalogue | No new store; starts today | Half of engineers do not trust the metadata; CODEOWNERS silently drops departed owners; CMDB ships a 60-day staleness metric | mainstream (the records), emerging (agent reads) | [Port survey](../research/sources/port-state-of-idp-2025-metadata-trust.md) (vendor claim); [GitHub](../research/sources/github-docs-about-code-owners.md) (first-party); [ServiceNow CMDB](../research/sources/servicenow-cmdb-health-kpis-metrics.md) (vendor) |
| Owner-verified platform cards with expiry, written by the agent and attested by owners (specialises *Owner-curated, verified knowledge* in context-and-memory, sub-choice context-creation) | A standard card per platform (purpose, owner, contract, intake, checks, approvers, lead times, calendar), provisional until a named person verifies, expiring if not re-verified | Stripe Trailhead's verify-or-archive rule; OpsLevel's suggest-then-confirm owners; ING's per-service owner, contract, SLO, unit cost | Records that agents can trust, kept current as a by-product of asks | Owners' attention is the scarce resource; needs expiry to be enforced; standard shape must be adopted, not imposed | early-adopter | [Stripe panel](../research/sources/stripe-sessions-2024-10x-engineer-panel.md) (first-party); [OpsLevel](../research/sources/opslevel-docs-service-detection.md) (first-party); [ING](../research/sources/techzine-nardiello-ing-decade-cloud-native.md) (first-party) |
| Runtime-derived catalogue with provenance | Facts about system dependencies derived from traffic and deploys, each with a source and a confidence | Proposed by a practitioner; partial in vendors | Fresh for system edges | Process, approval and funding dependencies leave no call graph | emerging | [The Reliability Engineering](../research/sources/thereliabilityengineering-service-catalog-already-wrong.md) (anecdote) |
| Regulatory registers as the record | The EU DORA register of ICT contracts and dependency map, the model inventory with inter-dependencies, the third-party inventory, kept for the regulator and read by the agent | EU DORA Articles 8 and 28; PRA SS1/23 | Legally required anyway; audit-grade | 6.5% of first registers passed all checks; built for the supervisor, not for product work | early-adopter | [EU DORA](../research/sources/eu-dora-regulation-ict-risk-third-party.md) (law); [ESAs dry run](../research/sources/esas-dora-dry-run-register-of-information.md) (regulator); [PRA](../research/sources/pra-ss1-23-model-risk-management-principles.md) (regulator) |
| Session-of-record per feature, decisions written back automatically | The dependency session is the record; every answer and decision is written at decision time with owner, date and source | Film 13's context layer; pipeline change records; access audits with "Requested by AI on behalf of" | The freshest kind of record (written automatically at decision time) | Needs the shared-persistent session the register already debates | emerging | [ServiceNow change](../research/sources/servicenow-docs-devops-change-velocity-approval.md) (vendor); [SailPoint MCP](../research/sources/sailpoint-docs-mcp-server.md) (first-party) |

### Sub-choice: agent-intake-rights — How far the agent may act on a platform's intake and a governance gate

#### Options
| Option | What it is | Examples | Gives you | Costs / risks | Maturity | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Read and draft only | The agent maps, pre-fills forms and drafts asks; a person submits and sends | The MVP in [06-mvp.md](../tasks/research-dependency-agent/06-mvp.md) | No identity or policy decisions needed; no bot noise | The person stays the relay for submission | mainstream | [Nudge](../research/sources/arxiv-maddila-nudge-overdue-pull-requests.md) (first-party: targeted reminders work, untargeted volume is switched off) |
| Submit on behalf of a named person; a human approves; the decision recorded | The agent submits access, registration or change requests under its own identity with a sponsor, acting for a person; approval stays human | Entra agent access packages; SailPoint MCP; Kong portal MCP (humans issue credentials) | Removes the form-fill; keeps the approval | Needs agent identity and delegation; whether approval policies bind MCP-initiated runs is undocumented in some tools | emerging | [Entra](../research/sources/microsoft-entra-agent-access-packages.md), [SailPoint](../research/sources/sailpoint-docs-mcp-server.md), [Kong](../research/sources/kong-docs-dev-portal-mcp-server.md) (first-party); [Port](../research/sources/port-docs-remote-mcp-server.md) (first-party, gap noted) |
| Submit under policy with automatic approval for routine, low-risk requests (evidence row for Gates as data, governance-control-plane) | Policy decides routine intake (tests, scans, risk score, data classification); exceptions to people. The full option is *Gates as data: evidence packet in, policy decision out* in [[governance-control-plane]] | ServiceNow pipeline change approval; Immuta low-risk auto-grant; Opal Zero (a model evaluates against written policy) | The run stage; throughput | Risk scoring at a bank was near chance; auto-approval that regulators do not accept; the gate made easier to skip (Swedbank) | early-adopter (change), emerging (access) | [ServiceNow](../research/sources/servicenow-docs-devops-change-velocity-approval.md) (vendor); [NAB](../research/sources/itnews-nab-cast-cloud-controls.md) (first-party via press); [Kapel et al., ING](../research/sources/arxiv-kapel-ing-change-incident-prediction.md) (first-party, peer-reviewed); [Opal](../research/sources/opal-zero-launch-press-release.md) (vendor claim); [Finansinspektionen](../research/sources/fi-swedbank-change-management-sanction.md) (regulator) |
| First-line only at judgement gates | At model validation, privacy assessment, threat modelling and architecture review, the agent classifies applicability, pre-fills and routes, and never validates or approves | The independence rules in SR 11-7 and SS1/23; the ICO's published DPIA screening | Regulator-compatible by construction | Caps the agent's reach at the gates that hurt most | n/a (a constraint) | [SR 11-7](../research/sources/federalreserve-sr-11-7-model-risk-guidance.md), [PRA](../research/sources/pra-ss1-23-model-risk-management-principles.md), [ICO](../research/sources/ico-when-do-we-need-a-dpia.md) (regulator) |

### Key questions to choose
1. Where does the dependency context for a recent feature actually live, and how long did it take to assemble? — *answered by:* a context audit of two or three shipped features with their product owners — *affects:* all record-home options
2. Will owners verify a platform card when asked the smallest question riding on a real ask, and at what rate? — *answered by:* the MVP's first quarter, measuring verification rate — *affects:* owner-verified cards vs existing records as they are
3. Does the second line accept an agent-assembled evidence packet as first-line evidence with the agent's identity on it? — *answered by:* risk and audit owners — *affects:* submit-on-behalf-of vs read-and-draft at gates
4. Which gates have published, checkable criteria and an evidence list, and which platforms already have intake as a schema? — *answered by:* an inventory with the change, security, privacy and platform owners — *affects:* which platforms and gates can be at the submit options
5. Do approval policies bind requests an agent initiates through MCP in our tools (Port, Backstage, the ITSM tool)? — *answered by:* a vendor check and a sandbox test — *affects:* submit-under-policy
6. What is the actual intake-to-decision lead time per platform? (No source publishes one.) — *answered by:* the agent's own timings from real asks — *affects:* where the walk-environment investment goes first

### Current lean and what would change it
Lean: owner-verified platform cards with expiry, written as a by-product of real asks, in a session of record; rights at read-and-draft until a labelled sample and an identity model allow submit-on-behalf-of; first-line only at judgement gates throughout. Reasons: no shipped product discovers undeclared dependencies, the records are stale by default, by-product capture is the pattern that worked at the beacons, and the regulatory evidence punishes gates made easier to skip. Would change if owners verify at a useful rate unprompted (then a standalone curation pass is viable), or if a vendor ships cross-platform discovery with sourced edges and a write-back store.

## Cross-cutting key questions


1. Who is the user we are choosing for (individual PM, product team, whole org), and does the answer differ by dimension? — *answered by:* the project owner / user interviews
2. What should further work cover first: a prototype to choose between options, or a build of the leading option?
3. Is the target setting a regulated organisation? If so, how does regulatory review shape every dimension: review gating, prototype-as-spec, context governance, and harness choice? — *answered by:* the project owner; risk and compliance policy owners — *affects:* [[review-and-autonomy]], [[intent-shaping-and-spec]], [[context-and-memory]], [[governance-control-plane]] (added by research-product-agent-and-harness)
4. Shared-agent identity (creator, requester, service account) is a product decision as much as a security one: what is the least surprising behaviour for our users when a teammate sends an instruction to an agent holding someone else's access? — *answered by:* user interviews plus security owner — *affects:* governance-control-plane, session-persistence-sharing (Track 5)
5. (Track 3) Is the first deployed task agent meant to be called from many harnesses we do not control, or only from our own product? The answer decides whether protocols (MCP/A2A) matter at all or a vendor API suffices. — *answered by:* the project owner / user interviews; see [[remote-invocation-route]]
6. (Track 2) Is the thing being built a harness at all, or the assets and governance around vendor harnesses? The independent evidence says the competitive unit has moved from the loop to the ecosystem surface (skills, marketplaces, governance), and that building the loop is cheap while upkeep and evals are not. — *answered by:* project scoping; Track 4 and 5 findings; a scoped upkeep estimate

- (Track 6) Does the project treat "unstructured" work as out of scope for standardisation (leave it in a flexible harness) or as something that still needs structured context (folders, memory, rubrics)? The evidence (best-practices guidance, vault anecdotes) says the path is open but the context is structured. — *answered by:* the project owner; user interviews

- (user-story-mapping) Which embodiment fits depends on a fact we lack in several stories: how PMs keep artifacts today, whether reviewers will enter agent sessions, and whether a machine-checkable spec standard exists. Are these three facts the first things to establish before any prototype? The cross-cutting stories ([S11–S15](../tasks/user-story-mapping/stories-cross-cutting.md)) and the S05 pair ([worked derivation](../tasks/user-story-mapping/worked-derivation-s05.md)) mark where each bites. — *answered by:* a short interview round plus one clickable prototype pair — *affects:* all
7. Ramp changed its answer on who supplies the loop (vendor SDK → own harness) and where it runs (laptop → cloud workers) within about six months of shipping Glass. Should the next phase take a reversible first step and an explicit revisit point rather than a one-time pattern choice, and what would the revisit criteria be? — *answered by:* the project owner / the strategy's framing; evidence in [Building Laminar](../research/sources/ramp-builders-building-laminar.md) (competitive-teardown-ramp-glass, re-check 2026-10-24)
8. (competitive-teardown-stripe-knowledge-agents) Stripe runs its internal knowledge agent on the same harness, sandbox, orchestration and access control as its customer-facing agents, arguing the shared substrate forces the external security bar inside and improves both. Does our next phase of work assume the internal PM agent and any customer-facing agent share a substrate, or are they separate bets? It bears on loop-source, runtime-location, governance-control-plane and trust-privacy-defaults. — *answered by:* the project owner / product leadership; ask Stripe what sharing has cost them — *affects:* the scope of the request ([source](../research/sources/stripe-meet-knowledge-ai-platform.md), first-party)

- (competitive-teardown-shopify-river) Does the strategy assume the target org will accept agent work being visible by default? Shopify's River (public-only, no DMs) shows the learning upside; the cost (chilling effect, sensitive data, regulated walls) is unmeasured. The answer moves [[agent-interface]], [[trust-privacy-defaults]], [[context-and-memory]] (corpus mining), [[adoption-model]] and [[agent-identity-delegation]] together. — *answered by:* the project owner; policy/compliance owners; a matched public-vs-private pilot
9. Every first-party page here prescribes; none reports a controlled outcome. Which two or three claims would the strategy rely on, and can any be checked independently (customer platform teams, a pilot)? — *affects:* all leans. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
10. Anthropic's own guidance moved within six months (multi-agent patterns in April, fold subagents back into one loop in October; chat vs Cowork advice in June, merged in September). How should the strategy treat vendor guidance that is this young? — *affects:* work-structure, agent-interface. (from research-claude-resources-articles)
11. Almost every number in PM-audience explainers belongs to the vendor; DoP relays Strella, Amplitude, DoorDash, Anthropic, Rippling and Vercel figures without checking them. Must every figure be traced to its primary source and labelled before the strategy uses it? — *answered by:* the claim-check practice the story worker already uses — *affects:* all dimensions (from research-department-of-product-knowledge-series)
12. How many concurrent agent sessions or outputs can one PM or reviewer oversee before review quality drops; does a triage or oversight interface change that number (practitioners use paper lists and folders for 10-20 sessions); and when product people ship more, where does downstream review capacity come from (agent review, a "utility engineer", design-system-only teams)? — *answered by:* a time-and-error study at 1, 3, 6 and 10 concurrent sessions; review queue times in builder pilots — *affects:* review-and-autonomy, session-persistence-sharing, agent-interface, work-structure, target-user, target-outcome. Evidence: [cw-pms-coding-tools](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-pms-ai-coding-tools.md), [graham](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-molly-graham-delegating-to-ai.md), [carson](../research/sources/howiai-ryan-carson-devin-20k-month.md), [cw-product-engineers](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-product-engineers-pilot.md) (anecdote); [sherwin-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sherwin-wu-openai-codex.md) (first-party). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
13. Which of our harness investments (skills, file-based context, workflow scaffolding, persona topologies) will models absorb within 12-24 months, and how do we re-test the harness without each scaffold at each model release? (A team dropped BMAD after Opus 4.6 and GPT-5.5; a vibe-coding course went obsolete in months.) — *answered by:* re-testing without each scaffold at each model release — *affects:* harness-upkeep, loop-source, capability-format-portability, work-structure, context-and-memory. Evidence: [sherwin-wu](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-sherwin-wu-openai-codex.md), [cherny](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-boris-cherny-after-coding.md) (first-party); [cw-ai-operating-models](../research/sources/lenny-community-wisdom-ai-operating-models.md), [jovanovic](../research/sources/lenny-podcast-lazar-jovanovic-vibe-coder.md) (anecdote). (from research-lennys-newsletter-archive)
14. Is the compounding effect real: does a second session on a related feature start further along, and at what verification rate does that happen? No source measures it. — *answered by:* the MVP's reuse and verification rates over a quarter — *affects:* dependency-knowledge-and-intake, context-and-memory, sequencing (from research-dependency-agent)
15. Is priority churn on the platform side, rather than dependencies, the larger cause of slip in our organisation, and should the agent report it as its own state? — *answered by:* the ask register's "waiting on a platform whose priorities changed" count — *affects:* target-outcome, dependency-knowledge-and-intake (from research-dependency-agent)
16. Which of the FCA's forthcoming rules on agent identity and delegated authority will bind what an agent may submit, and when? — *answered by:* compliance, tracking the FCA framework — *affects:* agent-identity-delegation, governance-control-plane, dependency-knowledge-and-intake (from research-dependency-agent)
